\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\in\begin{cases}\mathcal{SG}(\lVert\boldsymbol{\sigma}\rVert_{2}),&\text{if $\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}$ are mutually independent,}\\
\mathcal{SG}(\lVert\boldsymbol{\sigma}\rVert_{1}),&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Similarly, if
X_{i}\in\mathcal{SE}(\nu_{i},\alpha_{i})
for some
\sigma_{i},\>\alpha_{i}>0
, then letting
\boldsymbol{\nu}=\{\nu_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
and
\boldsymbol{\alpha}=\{\alpha_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\in\begin{cases}\mathcal{SE}(\lVert\boldsymbol{\nu}\rVert_{2},\lVert\boldsymbol{\alpha}\rVert_{\infty}),&\text{if $\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}$ are mutually independent,}\\
\mathcal{SE}(\lVert\boldsymbol{\nu}\rVert_{1},\lVert\boldsymbol{\alpha}\rVert_{\infty}),&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Proof.

First suppose that
X_{i}\in\mathcal{SE}(\nu_{i},\alpha_{i})
and that
\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
is a collection of independent random variables, then by independence:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda X_{i}\right)\right].

Next, note that if
|\lambda|<\lVert\boldsymbol{\alpha}\rVert^{-1}_{\infty}
, then
|\lambda|<\alpha_{i}^{-1}
for each
i\in[n]
and thus:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda X_{i}\right)\right]\leq\exp\left[\frac{\lambda^{2}\nu_{i}^{2}}{2}\right]

which implies:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left[\frac{\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{n}\nu^{2}_{i}}{2}\right].

So
\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\in\mathcal{SE}(\lVert\boldsymbol{\nu}\rVert_{2},\lVert\boldsymbol{\alpha}\rVert_{\infty})
. When
\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are not independent, then for any
|\lambda|\leq\lVert\boldsymbol{\alpha}\rVert^{-1}_{\infty}
and any collection of parameters
\{p_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
such that
p_{i}>0
and
\sum_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}^{-1}=1
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\exp\left(\lambda X_{i}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}[\exp\left(p_{i}\lambda X_{i}\right)]\right)^{1/p_{i}}

(Hölder’s inequality)

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{p^{2}_{i}\nu_{i}^{2}\lambda^{2}}{2}\right)\right]\right)^{1/p_{i}}

(
X_{i}\in\mathcal{SE}(\nu_{i},\alpha_{i})
)

\displaystyle=\exp\left[\frac{\lambda^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}\nu_{i}^{2}}{2}\right].

Since our choice of
\{p_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
was arbitrary so long as
p_{i}>0
and
\sum_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}^{-1}=1
, we can set each
p_{i}=\frac{\lVert\boldsymbol{\nu}\rVert_{1}}{\nu_{i}},
then we get:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left[\frac{\lambda\lVert\boldsymbol{\nu}\rVert^{2}_{1}}{2}\right]

showing that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\in\mathcal{SE}(\lVert\boldsymbol{\nu}\rVert_{1},\lVert\boldsymbol{\alpha}\rVert_{\infty}).
The proof for the case where
X_{i}\in\mathcal{SG}(\sigma_{i})
follows an identical argument as above with
\sigma_{i}
replacing
\nu_{i}
and ignoring any constraints on
\lambda
.

∎

A.2.2
Sums of
\alpha
-Mixing Processes

Lemma 2

(
Padilla
et al. 2023
Lemma 3)
.

Let
\nu>0
be given. Suppose that
\{X_{t}\}_{t=1}^{\infty}
is a stationary
\alpha
-mixing time-series with mixing coefficients
\{\alpha_{k}\}_{k=0}^{K}
. Suppose that
\mathbb{E}[X_{t}]=0
and that there exist constants
\delta,\Delta,D_{1},D_{2}>0
such that:

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 1}\;\mathbb{E}\left[\left|X_{t}\right|^{2+\delta+\Delta}\right]\leq D_{1}

and:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+1)^{\frac{\delta}{2}}\alpha_{k}^{\frac{\Delta}{2+\delta+\Delta}}\leq D_{2}.

Then there exists some universal constant
C>0
such that for any
a\in(0,1)
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}X_{t^{\prime}}\right|\leq a^{-1}C\sqrt{t}\left[\log(\nu t)+1\right],\;\;\forall\;t\geq\nu^{-1}\right)\geq 1-a^{2}.

Lemma 3
.

Suppose that
\{X_{t}\}_{t\geq 1}
is a stationary,
\alpha
-mixing processes with mixing coefficients and moments that satisfy the conditions of Lemma
2
. Then for
T\geq 1
,
t_{0}\in[T]
, and sequence
\{a_{T}\}_{T\geq 1}
such that
a_{T}>0
and
\lim_{T\to\infty}a_{T}=\infty
, there exists a universal constant
C>0
such that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t=t_{0}+1}^{T}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}X_{t^{\prime}}\right|>Ca_{T}\sqrt{t-t_{0}}\log T\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{a_{T}^{2}},

and:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t=1}^{t_{0}-1}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}X_{t^{\prime}}\right|>Ca_{T}\sqrt{t_{0}-t}\log T\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{a^{2}_{T}},

and:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}X_{t^{\prime}}\right|>Ca_{T}\sqrt{T-t+1}\log T\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{a_{T}^{2}}.

Proof.

By Lemma
2
, there is some constant
C>0
so that if we let
\nu=1
and
a=a_{T}^{-1}
, then:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t=1}^{\infty}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}X_{t^{\prime}}\right|>Ca_{T}\sqrt{t}\left[\log t+1\right]\right\}\right)\leq\frac{1}{a_{T}^{2}}.

Note that since
\{X_{t}\}_{t=1}^{\infty}
is a stationary process, so we can shift the time indices of the sum by
t_{0}-1
to get:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{a_{T}^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t=1}^{\infty}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}X_{t^{\prime}}\right|>Ca_{T}\sqrt{t}\left[\log t+1\right]\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t=t_{0}+1}^{\infty}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}X_{t^{\prime}}\right|>Ca_{T}\sqrt{t-t_{0}}\left[\log(t-t_{0})+1\right]\right\}\right)

(stationarity)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t=t_{0}+1}^{T}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}X_{t^{\prime}}\right|>Ca_{T}\sqrt{t-t_{0}}\log(t-t_{0})\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t=t_{0}+1}^{T}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}X_{t^{\prime}}\right|>Ca_{T}\sqrt{t-t_{0}}\log T\right\}\right).

(
T>t-t_{0}
)

Similarly:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{a_{T}^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t=1}^{\infty}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}X_{t^{\prime}}\right|>Ca_{T}\sqrt{t}\left[\log t+1\right]\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t=1}^{t_{0}-1}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}X_{t^{\prime}}\right|>Ca_{T}\sqrt{t}\log t\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t=1}^{t_{0}-1}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t_{0}-t}X_{t^{\prime}}\right|>Ca_{T}\sqrt{t_{0}-t}\log(t_{0}-t)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t=1}^{t_{0}-1}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}X_{t^{\prime}}\right|>Ca_{T}\sqrt{t_{0}-t}\log T\right\}\right).

(
T>t_{0}-t
)

Lastly:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{a_{T}^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t=1}^{\infty}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}X_{t^{\prime}}\right|>Ca_{T}\sqrt{t}\left[\log t+1\right]\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}X_{t^{\prime}}\right|>Ca_{T}\sqrt{t}\log t\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T-t+1}X_{t^{\prime}}\right|>Ca_{T}\sqrt{T-t+1}\log(T-t+1)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}X_{t^{\prime}}\right|>Ca_{T}\sqrt{T-t+1}\log T\right\}\right).

(
T>T-t+1
)

∎

A.3
Theorem
1
Event Bounds

In Lemma
4
, we assume
\lVert\mathbf{y}_{t}\rVert_{\psi_{2}}
is uniformly bounded over all
t,d\geq 1
. As per Remark
5
, this assumption holds when either: i) each entry of
\mathbf{y}_{t}
is independent and
\mathcal{SG}(\sigma)
for some
\sigma<\infty
, ii)
\mathbf{y}_{t}\sim\mathcal{N}_{d}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t},\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{-1})
and
\inf_{d\geq 1}\lambda_{\min}>0
, where
\lambda_{\min}
is the smallest eigenvalue of
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}
, or iii)
y_{t,j}\in\mathcal{SG}(\sigma_{j})
for each
j\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
with
\sup_{d\geq 1}\lVert\boldsymbol{\sigma}\rVert_{2}<\infty
,
\lVert\boldsymbol{\sigma}\rVert_{1}=\mathcal{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
, or
\lVert\boldsymbol{\sigma}\rVert_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
.

i)

When each entry of
\mathbf{y}_{t}
is independent and
y_{t,j}\in\mathcal{SG}(\sigma)
for each
j\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
, then for any
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{B}^{2}_{1}
, by Lemma
1
we have
\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbf{y}_{t}\rangle\in\mathcal{SG}(\sigma)
.

ii)

For any
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{B}^{2}_{1}
, we have:

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbf{y}_{t}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{v},\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{y}_{t}\rangle

\displaystyle=\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{v}\rVert_{2}\left\langle\frac{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{v}}{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{v}\rVert_{2}},\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{y}_{t}\right\rangle.

Since
\mathbf{y}_{t}
is Gaussian, the coordinates of
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{y}_{t}
are independent and belong to
\mathcal{SG}(1)
, so by Lemma
1
, the inner-product above is
\mathcal{SG}(1)
. Also,
\inf_{d\geq 1}\lambda_{\min}>0
by assumption so:

\displaystyle\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{v}\rVert_{2}\leq\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{B}^{2}_{1}}\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{v}\rVert_{2}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda_{\min}}}<\infty.

iii)

If
y_{t,j}\in\mathcal{SG}(\sigma_{j})
, then for any
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{B}^{2}_{1}
:

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbf{y}_{t}\rangle

\displaystyle\in\mathcal{SG}(\langle\mathbf{v},\boldsymbol{\sigma}\rangle)

(Lemma
1
)

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathcal{SG}(\lVert\mathbf{v}\rVert_{2}\lVert\boldsymbol{\sigma}\rVert_{2}).

(Cauchy-Schwarz inequality)

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathcal{SG}(\sqrt{d}\lVert\boldsymbol{\sigma}\rVert_{1}).

(
\lVert\mathbf{v}\rVert_{2}=1
and
\lVert\boldsymbol{\sigma}\rVert_{2}\leq\sqrt{d}\lVert\boldsymbol{\sigma}\rVert_{1}
)

In the last line we can replace
\lVert\boldsymbol{\sigma}\rVert_{1}
with
\lVert\boldsymbol{\sigma}\rVert_{\infty}
.

Lemma 4
.

Let
\{\mathbf{y}_{t}\}_{t=1}^{T}
be a sequence of independent random vectors with
\mathbf{y}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
{\text{Var}}(\mathbf{y}_{t})=\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{-1}
and
\sup_{t\geq 1,d\geq 1}\lVert\mathbf{y}_{t}\rVert_{\psi_{2}}<\infty
. Let
\lambda_{\max}
and
\lambda_{\min}
be the largest and smallest eigenvalues of
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}
respectively and assume that
\sup_{d\geq 1}\lambda_{\min}<\infty
and
\inf_{d\geq 1}\lambda_{\min}>0
. Under Assumption
1
, if we define the normalized terms
\mathbf{z}_{t}:=\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{y}_{t}-\mathbf{b}_{0}\mathbbm{1}\{t\geq t_{0}\})
, then for any
\beta>0
, there exist universal constants
C_{\beta},C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that we can define the set:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\beta}:=\left\{1\leq t\leq T\>:\>|t_{0}-t|>\frac{C_{\beta}\log T}{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{1/2}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}\right\}

(A.5)

and the events:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}}\left\{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0},\sum_{t^{\prime}=\min\{t_{0},t\}}^{\max\{t_{0},t\}-1}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rangle\right|<\frac{|t_{0}-t|\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{8}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0},\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rangle\right|<\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{8\sqrt{d}}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{3}

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}}\left\{\left|\left\langle\sum_{t^{\prime}=\min\{t_{0},t\}}^{\max\{t_{0},t\}-1}\frac{\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{|t_{0}-t|}},\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=\max\{t_{0},t\}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=\max\{t_{0},t\}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}}\right\rangle\right|<\sqrt{C_{2}\log T}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{4}

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{t=t_{0}}^{T}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{T-t+1}\left\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}-d\right|<C_{2}\sqrt{d}\log T\right\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{5}

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{1\leq t\leq T\>:\>t\neq t_{0}}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{|t_{0}-t|}\left\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=\min\{t_{0},t\}}^{\max\{t_{0},t\}-1}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}-d\right|<C_{2}\sqrt{d}\log T\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{6}

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{t=t_{0}}^{T}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T-t+1}}\left\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}-\sqrt{d}\right|<\sqrt{C_{2}d\log T}\right\},

and the joint event
\mathcal{E}:=\cap_{i=1}^{6}\mathcal{E}_{i}
, and we will have
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\geq 1-C_{1}T^{-\beta}
.

Proof.

By the union bound
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{c})=1-P(\cup_{i=1}^{6}\mathcal{E}^{c}_{i})<1-\sum_{i=1}^{6}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{c}_{i})
, so it is sufficient to show that for some
C_{i}>0
,
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{c}_{i})\leq C_{i}T^{-\beta}
for each
i
. By assumption, there exists some
K<\infty
such that
\sup_{t\geq 1,d\geq 1}\lVert\mathbf{y}_{t}\rVert_{\psi_{2}}\leq K
, so for any
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{B}^{2}_{1}
we have:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbf{z}_{t}\right\rangle\right\rVert_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle=\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{v}\rVert_{2}\left\lVert\left\langle\frac{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{v}}{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{v}\rVert_{2}},\mathbf{y}_{t}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{y}_{t}]\right\rangle\right\rVert_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{B}^{2}_{1}}\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{v}\rVert_{2}\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{B}^{2}_{1}}\left\lVert\left\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbf{y}_{t}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{y}_{t}]\right\rangle\right\rVert_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}}\left\lVert\mathbf{y}_{t}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{y}_{t}]\right\rVert_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}}\left\lVert\mathbf{y}_{t}\right\rVert_{\psi_{2}}.

(Lemma 2.6.8 of
Vershynin 2018
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}}K

Since
\lambda_{\max}
is finite by assumption, and
\{\mathbf{z}_{t}\}_{t=1}^{T}
is a independent sequence, then by Propositions 2.5.2 and 2.6.1 of
Vershynin 2018
there exists some constant
C>0
such that for any index set
\mathcal{T}\subseteq[T]
with cardinality
|\mathcal{T}|
we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\left\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbf{z}_{t}\right\rangle\in\mathcal{SG}\left(C\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}|\mathcal{T}|}K\right).

So letting
\mathbf{v}=\frac{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}}{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}}
, then by the Chernoff bound (
A.2
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0},\sum_{t^{\prime}=\min\{t_{0},t\}}^{\max\{t_{0},t\}-1}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rangle\right|\geq\frac{|t_{0}-t|\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{8}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left[-\frac{|t_{0}-t|\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}}{128\lambda_{\max}C^{2}K^{2}}\right].

For all
t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}
and
C_{\beta}\geq 128(1+\beta)\lambda_{\max}C^{2}K^{2}
, we then have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0},\sum_{t^{\prime}=\min\{t_{0},t\}}^{\max\{t_{0},t\}-1}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rangle\right|\geq\frac{|t_{0}-t|\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{8}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{T^{1+\beta}}.

Therefore:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{C}_{1})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}}\left\{\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0},\sum_{t^{\prime}=\min\{t_{0},t\}}^{\max\{t_{0},t\}-1}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rangle\right|\geq\frac{|t_{0}-t|\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{8}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0},\sum_{t^{\prime}=\min\{t_{0},t\}}^{\max\{t_{0},t\}-1}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rangle\right|\geq\frac{|t_{0}-t|\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{8}\right)

(union bound)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}}\frac{2}{T^{1+\beta}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{T^{\beta}}.

(
|\mathcal{T}_{\beta}|\leq T
)

We can again use the Chernoff bound (
A.2
) to get:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0},\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rangle\right|\geq\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{8\sqrt{d}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left[-\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}}{128\lambda_{\max}C^{2}K^{2}d}\right].

We have
\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}\geq\lambda_{\min}\lVert\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}
and by Assumption
1
, there is some positive sequence
\{a_{T}\}_{T\geq 1}
such that
a_{T}\to\infty
and
(T-t_{0}+1)\lVert\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}\geq a_{T}d\log T
. Therefore:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0},\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rangle\right|\geq\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{8\sqrt{d}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left[-\frac{\lambda_{\min}a_{T}\log T}{128\lambda_{\max}C^{2}K^{2}}\right].

So for
T
large enough that
a_{T}\geq\frac{128\lambda_{\max}C^{2}K^{2}\beta}{\lambda_{\min}}
, we again have
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{c})\leq 2T^{-\beta}
.

Again, since
\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=\max\{t_{0},t\}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=\max\{t_{0},t\}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}}\in\mathbb{B}^{2}_{1}
for each
t
, so by the Chernoff bound (
A.2
) we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\left\langle\sum_{t^{\prime}=\min\{t_{0},t\}}^{\max\{t_{0},t\}-1}\frac{\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{|t_{0}-t|}},\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=\max\{t_{0},t\}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=\max\{t_{0},t\}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}}\right\rangle\right|\geq\sqrt{C_{2}\log T}\right)\leq 2\exp\left[-\frac{C_{2}\log T}{2C^{2}K^{2}\lambda_{\max}}\right]

So for
C_{2}\geq 2(1+\beta)C^{2}K^{2}\lambda_{\max}
, we can use the same argument as we did for
\mathcal{E}_{1}
above to get
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{3}^{c})\leq 2T^{-\beta}
.

Next, since our observations are independent across
t
, we have
\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}\mathbf{z}_{t}]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}]\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}_{t}]=0
for
t\geq t^{\prime}
, and since
\mathbb{E}[z_{t,j}^{2}]=1
by construction, then:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{T-t+1}\left\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T-t+1}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}]=d

Since:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{\sqrt{|\mathcal{T}|}}\sum_{t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim K\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}}

then by the Hanson–Wright inequality (see e.g. exercise 6.2.5 of
Vershynin 2018
), there is a universal constant
K_{1}>0
so that for any
t\geq t_{0}
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T-t+1}\left\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\in\mathcal{SE}(K_{1}\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}d},K_{1}),

so by (
A.3
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{T-t+1}\left\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}-d\right|\geq C_{2}\sqrt{d}\log T\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left[-\frac{C_{2}\log T}{2K_{1}}\min\left\{\sqrt{d},\frac{C_{2}\log T}{K_{1}\lambda_{\max}}\right\}\right].

Since
\sqrt{d}\geq 1
, for
C_{2}>2K_{1}(1+\beta)
and
T
large enough that
\log T\geq\frac{K_{1}\lambda_{\max}}{C_{2}}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{T-t+1}\left\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}-d\right|\geq C_{2}\sqrt{d}\log T\right)\leq\frac{1}{T^{1+\beta}}.

Thus:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{c}_{4})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t=t_{0}}^{T}\left\{\left|\frac{1}{T-t+1}\left\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}-d\right|\geq C_{2}\sqrt{d}\log T\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{T-t+1}\left\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}-d\right|\geq C_{2}\sqrt{d}\log T\right)

(union bound)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=t_{0}}^{T}\frac{2}{T^{(1+\beta)}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{T^{\beta}}.

An identical argument gives
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{c}_{5})\leq 2T^{-\beta}
. Next, for any
x,\delta\geq 0
we have
|x-1|\geq\delta\implies|x^{2}-1|\geq\max\{\delta,\delta^{2}\}
(see 3.2 in
Vershynin 2018
). Using this fact and that
d^{1/4}\sqrt{C_{2}\log T}\leq C_{2}\sqrt{d}\log T
for large
T
, we have:

\displaystyle\left\{\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T-t+1}}\left\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}-1\right|\geq d^{1/4}\sqrt{C_{2}\log T}\right\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\left\{\left|\frac{1}{T-t+1}\left\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert^{2}_{2}-1\right|\geq C_{2}\sqrt{d}\log T\right\}

and thus
\mathcal{E}_{6}^{c}\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{4}^{c}\implies\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{6}^{c})\leq 2T^{-\beta}
.
∎

A.4
Proof of Theorem
1

If we take the ratio of the posterior probabilities of the change-point location from the mean-scp model, we get:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\mathbb{P}(\gamma=t_{0}\>|\>\mathbf{y}_{1:T};\omega_{0},\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T})}{\mathbb{P}(\gamma=t\>|\>\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>;\omega_{0},\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T})}

\displaystyle=\log\frac{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\gamma=t_{0}\>;\omega_{0})\mathbb{P}(\gamma=t_{0}\>;\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T})/p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>;\omega_{0},\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T})}{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\gamma=t\>;\omega_{0})\mathbb{P}(\gamma=t\>;\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T})/p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>;\omega_{0},\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T})}

(Bayes’ rule)

\displaystyle=\log\frac{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\tau=t_{0}\>;\omega_{0})}{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\tau=t\>;\omega_{0})}+\log\frac{\pi_{t_{0}}}{\pi_{t}}.

By the assumptions of Theorem
1
, for large
T
there is some constant
C_{\pi}>0
such that for all
t\in[T]
:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\pi_{t}}{\pi_{t_{0}}}\leq C_{\pi}\log T.

(A.6)

Therefore, if we can show that for any
\beta>0
, there are some constants
C,C_{\beta}>0
such that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcap_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}}\left\{\log\frac{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\tau=t_{0}\>;\omega_{0})}{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\tau=t\>;\omega_{0})}>C_{\pi}\log T\right\}\right)\geq 1-CT^{-\beta}

(A.7)

where
\mathcal{T}_{\beta}
is the set defined in (
A.5
), then with probability at least
1-CT^{-\beta}
, we will have:

\displaystyle\max_{t\>:\>|t-t_{0}|>\frac{C_{\beta}\log T}{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{1/2}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}}\;\mathbb{P}(\tau=t\;|\;\mathbf{y}_{1:T};\omega_{0},\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T})<\mathbb{P}(\tau=t_{0}\;|\;\mathbf{y}_{1:T};\omega_{0},\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T}).

Before proving (
A.7
), note that:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}=\min_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{x}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{\lVert\mathbf{x}\rVert_{2}^{2}}\implies\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}\geq\lambda_{\min}\lVert\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}\implies\frac{C_{\beta}\log T}{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{1/2}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}\leq\frac{C_{\beta}\log T}{\lambda_{\min}\lVert\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}},

so the localization error vanishes when
\lambda_{\min}\lVert\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}\gg\log T
. Since
|t-t_{0}|>1
for all
t\neq t_{0}
, in this case we have:

\displaystyle\lim_{T\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcap_{t\neq t_{0}}\left\{\log\frac{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\tau=t_{0}\>;\omega_{0})}{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\tau=t\>;\omega_{0})}>C_{\pi}\log T\right\}\right)=1

i.e.
\lim_{T\to\infty}\mathbb{P}(\hat{\tau}_{\text{MAP}}=t_{0})=1
. Note that when
\lambda_{\min}
is fixed and
\lVert\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{\infty}
is bounded over all
d
, then it is only possible for
\lVert\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}
to grow like
\log T
when
d\gtrsim\log T
, i.e. when
\lVert\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, then
d\gg\log T
is a necessary condition for exact recovery of
t_{0}
. When
d\geq a_{T}\log T
for some
a_{T}\to\infty
, then exact recovery is possible when at least
b_{T}\log T
elements of
\mathbf{b}_{0}
are bounded below in absolute value by
c_{T}
for some
b_{T}=\mathcal{O}(a_{T})
. This holds even when
c_{T}\to 0
so long as
b_{T}c_{T}\to\infty
.

Proof.

We begin by directly calculating the log-marginal evidence of
\mathbf{y}_{1:T}
:

\displaystyle\log p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\tau=t;\omega_{0})

\displaystyle=\log\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{P}(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\mathbf{b},\tau=t)\>\;d\mathbb{P}(\mathbf{b};\omega_{0})

\displaystyle=\log\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(2\pi)^{-\frac{dT}{2}}|\boldsymbol{\Lambda}|^{\frac{T}{2}}\exp\left[-\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}-\mathbf{b})\rVert_{2}^{2}}{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt\times\left(\frac{\omega_{0}}{2\pi}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\exp\left[-\frac{\omega_{0}\lVert\mathbf{b}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{2}\right]\;d\mathbf{b}

\displaystyle=\frac{T}{2}\left[\log|\boldsymbol{\Lambda}|-d\log(2\pi)\right]+\frac{d}{2}\log\left(\frac{\omega_{0}}{2\pi}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.19447pt+\log\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\left[-\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}\rVert_{2}^{2}+\omega_{0}\lVert\mathbf{b}\rVert_{2}^{2}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}+-2\langle\mathbf{b},\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}\rangle}{2}\right]d\mathbf{b}

\displaystyle=\frac{T}{2}\left[\log|\boldsymbol{\Lambda}|-d\log(2\pi)\right]+\frac{d}{2}\log\left(\omega_{0}\right)-\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.19447pt+\log\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(2\pi)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\exp\left[-\frac{\lVert[(T-t+1)\boldsymbol{\Lambda}+\omega_{0}\mathbf{I}_{d}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}\rVert_{2}^{2}-2\langle\mathbf{b},\boldsymbol{\Lambda}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}\rangle}{2}\right]\;d\mathbf{b}

\displaystyle=\log\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(2\pi)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\exp\left[-\frac{\lVert[(T-t+1)\boldsymbol{\Lambda}+\omega_{0}\mathbf{I}_{d}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}\rVert_{2}^{2}-2\langle\mathbf{b},\boldsymbol{\Lambda}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}\rangle}{2}\right]\;d\mathbf{b}+C

(
C
constant independent of
\tau
)

If we define
\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{t}:=(T-t+1)\boldsymbol{\Lambda}+\omega_{0}\mathbf{I}_{d}
and
\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{t}:=\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{t}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}
, then we have:

\displaystyle\log p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\tau=t;\omega_{0})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\log|\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{t}|+\log\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big|\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{t}\big|(2\pi)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\exp\left[-\frac{\lVert\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{t}(\mathbf{b}-\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{t})\rVert_{2}^{2}}{2}+\frac{\lVert\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{t}\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{2}\right]\;d\mathbf{b}+C

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\log|\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{t}|+\frac{1}{2}\lVert\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{t}\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}+C.

Letting
\omega_{0}\to 0
we have
\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{t}\to(T-t+1)\boldsymbol{\Lambda}
, therefore:

\displaystyle\lim_{\omega_{0}\to 0}\log p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\tau=t;\omega_{0})

\displaystyle=-\frac{d}{2}\log(T-t+1)-\frac{1}{2}\log|\boldsymbol{\Lambda}|+\frac{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{2(T-t+1)}+C

\displaystyle:=\log\alpha_{t}.

Since the determinant and
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{2}
are continuous, for small
\omega_{0}
, we can use
\log\alpha_{t}
to approximate the log-evidence in (
A.7
).
1
1

1

Formally, for any
\epsilon>0
, we can show that there is a choice of
\omega_{0}
small enough so that for each
t
,
|\frac{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\tau=t;\omega_{0})}{\Delta_{t}}-1|<\epsilon
with high probability as
T\to\infty
. So if we can show that for each
t
we consider,
\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}>C_{\pi}\log T
with high probability, we have
\log\frac{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\tau=t_{0};\omega_{0})}{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\tau=t;\omega_{0})}=\log\frac{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\tau=t_{0};\omega_{0})}{\alpha_{t_{0}}}+\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}+\log\frac{\alpha_{t}}{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\tau=t;\omega_{0})}>C_{\pi}\log T-2\log(1-\epsilon)
with high probability, which is the desired result.
Let
C_{\beta}
be large enough so that the statement of Lemma
4
holds. Then we will be on the event
\mathcal{E}
defined in Lemma
4
with probability approaching one as
T\to\infty
. We can show that the event in (
A.7
) holds on
\mathcal{E}
. To do so, we define the standardized terms
\mathbf{z}_{t}:=\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{y}_{t}-\mathbf{b}_{0}\mathbbm{1}\{t\geq t_{0}\})
and consider the following two cases:

Case 1:
t>t_{0}
and
t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}
.

For
t\geq t_{0}
we have:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}-\mathbf{b}_{0}+\mathbf{b}_{0})\right\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}+2(T-t+1)\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0},\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rangle+(T-t+1)^{2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}

and thus:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-\frac{d}{2}\log\left(\frac{T-t_{0}+1}{T-t+1}\right)+\frac{(t-t_{0})\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}{2}+\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{2(T-t_{0}+1)}-\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{2(T-t+1)}-\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0},\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rangle\right|.

Let
C_{\beta}>0
be large enough so that we are on the event
\mathcal{E}
defined in Lemma
4
with high probability, then for
t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}
on
\mathcal{E}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
we have:

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0},\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rangle\right|<\frac{(t-t_{0})\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{4}

and thus:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-\frac{d}{2}\log\left(\frac{T-t_{0}+1}{T-t+1}\right)+\frac{(t-t_{0})\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}{4}+\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{2(T-t_{0}+1)}-\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{2(T-t+1)}.

(A.8)

For the remaining random terms, we have:

\displaystyle\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t_{0}+1}-\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}+2\langle\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}},\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rangle+\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t_{0}+1}-\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t+1}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{t-t_{0}}{T-t_{0}+1}\right)\left[\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{t-t_{0}}-d\right]-\left(\frac{t-t_{0}}{T-t_{0}+1}\right)\left[\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t+1}-d\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{2\sqrt{(t-t_{0})(T-t+1)}\left(\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\frac{\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{T-t+1}}\rVert_{2}-\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{d}\right)\left\langle\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}\frac{\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{t-t_{0}}},\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}}\right\rangle}{T-t_{0}+1}

\displaystyle>-\left(\frac{t-t_{0}}{T-t_{0}+1}\right)\left|\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{t-t_{0}}-d\right|-\left(\frac{t-t_{0}}{T-t_{0}+1}\right)\left|\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t+1}-d\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt-\frac{2\sqrt{(t-t_{0})(T-t+1)}\left|\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\frac{\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{T-t+1}}\rVert_{2}-\sqrt{d}\right|\left|\left\langle\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}\frac{\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{t-t_{0}}},\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}}\right\rangle\right|}{T-t_{0}+1}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt-\frac{2\sqrt{(t-t_{0})(T-t+1)d}\left|\left\langle\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}\frac{\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{t-t_{0}}},\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}}\right\rangle\right|}{T-t_{0}+1}.

(A.9)

There exists some
C_{1}\geq 1
so that on the event
\mathcal{E}
defined in Lemma
4
we have:

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}\frac{\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{t-t_{0}}},\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle<\sqrt{C_{1}\log T}

(on
\mathcal{E}_{3}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\left\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t+1}-d\right|

\displaystyle<C_{1}\sqrt{d}\log T

(on
\mathcal{E}_{4}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\left\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}{t-t_{0}}-d\right|

\displaystyle<C_{1}\sqrt{d}\log T

(on
\mathcal{E}_{5}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\left\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}}{\sqrt{T-t+1}}-\sqrt{d}\right|

\displaystyle<\sqrt{C_{1}d\log T}.

(on
\mathcal{E}_{6}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
)

Returning to (
A.9
), we can use the bounds above to write:

\displaystyle\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{2(T-t_{0}+1)}-\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{2(T-t+1)}

\displaystyle>-\frac{C_{1}(t-t_{0})\sqrt{d}\log T}{T-t_{0}+1}-\frac{2C_{1}\sqrt{d(t-t_{0})(T-t+1)}\log T}{T-t_{0}+1}.

By Assumption
1
, there is some sequence
\{a_{T}\}_{t\geq 1}
such that:

\displaystyle a_{T}\to\infty\text{ and }\lVert\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}(T-t_{0}+1)\geq a_{T}d\log T,

(A.10)

and thus:

\displaystyle-\frac{C_{1}(t-t_{0})\sqrt{d}\log T}{T-t_{0}+1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{C_{1}(t-t_{0})\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}\sqrt{d}\log T}{(T-t_{0}+1)\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{C_{1}(t-t_{0})\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}\sqrt{d}\log T}{\lambda_{\min}(T-t_{0}+1)\lVert\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}

(
\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}\geq\lambda_{\min}\lVert\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}
)

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{C_{1}(t-t_{0})\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}a_{T}\sqrt{d}}.

(by
A.10
)

For
T
large enough so that
a_{T}\sqrt{d}\geq\frac{8C_{1}}{\lambda_{\min}}
, we can rewrite (
A.8
) as:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-\frac{d}{2}\log\left(\frac{T-t_{0}+1}{T-t+1}\right)+\frac{(t-t_{0})\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}{8}-\frac{2\sqrt{d(t-t_{0})(T-t+1)}C_{1}\log T}{T-t_{0}+1}.

(A.11)

Focusing on the last term, we can write:

\displaystyle\frac{2\sqrt{d(t-t_{0})(T-t+1)}C_{1}\log T}{T-t_{0}+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{2(t-t_{0})C_{1}\sqrt{d}\log T}{T-t_{0}+1}\sqrt{\frac{T-t+1}{t-t_{0}}}.

Since
\sqrt{(T-t+1)/(t-t_{0})}
is decreasing as a function of
t
and
\inf\{t\geq t_{0}\>|\>t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}\}=t_{0}+\frac{C_{\beta}\log T}{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{1/2}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}
, we have:

\displaystyle\frac{2\sqrt{d(t-t_{0})(T-t+1)}C_{1}\log T}{T-t_{0}+1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2C_{1}(t-t_{0})\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{1/2}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}\sqrt{d\log T}}{T-t_{0}+1}\sqrt{\frac{T-t_{0}+1-\frac{C_{\beta}\log T}{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{1/2}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}}{C_{\beta}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2C_{1}(t-t_{0})\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{1/2}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}\sqrt{d\log T}}{\sqrt{C_{\beta}(T-t_{0}+1)}}

(
\frac{C_{\beta}\log T}{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{1/2}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}>0
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2C_{1}(t-t_{0})\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{1/2}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}\sqrt{d\log T}}{\sqrt{C_{\beta}\lambda_{\min}\lVert\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}(T-t_{0}+1)}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2C_{1}(t-t_{0})\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{1/2}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}}{\sqrt{C_{\beta}\lambda_{\min}a_{T}}}.

(by
A.10
)

So for
T
large enough that
a_{T}>\frac{32^{2}C_{1}^{2}}{C_{\beta}\lambda_{\min}}
, we can bound (
A.11
) with:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-\frac{d}{2}\log\left(\frac{T-t_{0}+1}{T-t+1}\right)+\frac{(t-t_{0})\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}{16}.

(A.12)

Note that the RHS expression above is concave as a function of
t
, therefore it is minimized at one of the extremal values
t=T
or
t=t_{0}+\frac{C_{\beta}\log T}{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{1/2}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}
. When
t=T
and
T
is large enough that
T-t_{0}+1\geq 2
and
a_{T}\geq 16C_{\pi}+8
, the lower bound in (
A.12
) becomes:

\displaystyle-\frac{d}{2}\log\left(T-t_{0}+1\right)+\frac{(T-t_{0})\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}{16}

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{d}{2}\log\left(T-t_{0}+1\right)+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}{16}

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{d}{2}\log\left(T-t_{0}+1\right)+\frac{a_{T}d\log T}{16}

(by (
A.10
))

\displaystyle\geq\frac{(a_{T}-8)d\log T}{16}

(
T\geq T-t_{0}+1
)

\displaystyle\geq C_{\pi}\log T.

(
a_{T}\geq 16C_{\pi}+8
)

On the other hand, when
t=t_{0}+\frac{C_{\beta}\log T}{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{1/2}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}
, the lower bound in (
A.12
) becomes:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{2}\log\left(1-\frac{C_{\beta}\log T}{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{1/2}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}(T-t_{0}+1)}\right)+\frac{C_{\beta}\log T}{16}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{d}{2}\log\left(1-\frac{C_{\beta}}{a_{T}d}\right)+\frac{C_{\beta}\log T}{16}.

(by (
A.10
))

Noting that
\frac{C_{\beta}}{a_{T}d}\to 0
, we can take a first order Taylor expansion of
\log(1-x)
around zero to get:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{2}\log\left(1-\frac{C_{\beta}}{a_{T}d}\right)+\frac{C_{\beta}\log T}{16}

\displaystyle=-\frac{C_{\beta}}{2a_{T}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{a^{2}_{T}d}\right)+\frac{C_{\beta}\log T}{16}

The first two terms on the RHS above vanish as
T\to\infty
, so for
C_{\beta}>16C_{\pi}
, we can return to (
A.12
) to get
\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}\geq C_{\pi}\log T
, i.e. the event in (
A.7
) holds for this case.

Case 2:
t<t_{0}
and
t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}
.

As in the previous case, we have:

\displaystyle\frac{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t_{0}+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t_{0}+1}-2\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0},\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rangle+(T-t_{0}+1)\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}

and for
t<t_{0}
, we can write:

\displaystyle\frac{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}+(T-t_{0}+1)\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t+1}-2\left(\frac{T-t_{0}+1}{T-t+1}\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0},\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rangle+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)^{2}}{T-t+1}\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}.

After rearranging terms we get:

\displaystyle\frac{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t_{0}+1}-\frac{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t_{0}+1}-\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t+1}+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)}{T-t+1}\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\quad-2\left(\frac{t_{0}-t}{T-t+1}\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0},\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rangle+2\left(\frac{T-t_{0}+1}{T-t+1}\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0},\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rangle.

Focusing on the second line above, again let
C_{\beta}
be large enough so that Lemma
4
holds and we are on the event
\mathcal{E}
with high probability, then for
t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}
on
\mathcal{E}
we have:

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0},\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rangle\right|<\frac{(t_{0}-t)\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{8}

(on
\mathcal{E}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
)

and:

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0},\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rangle\right|<\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{8}

(on
\mathcal{E}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
)

so:

\displaystyle-2\left(\frac{t_{0}-t}{T-t+1}\right)\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0},\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rangle\right|-2\left(\frac{T-t_{0}+1}{T-t+1}\right)\left|\left\langle\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0},\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle>-\frac{(t_{0}-t)(T-t_{0}+1)\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{2(T-t+1)}.

So on
\mathcal{E}
we have:

\displaystyle\frac{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t_{0}+1}-\frac{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t+1}

\displaystyle>\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t_{0}+1}-\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t+1}+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)}{2(T-t+1)}\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}.

For the remaining random terms, we have:

\displaystyle\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t_{0}+1}-\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t_{0}+1}-\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}+2\langle\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}},\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rangle+\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t+1}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{t_{0}-t}{T-t+1}\right)\left[\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t_{0}+1}-d\right]-\left(\frac{t_{0}-t}{T-t+1}\right)\left[\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{t_{0}-t}-d\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{2\sqrt{(t_{0}-t)(T-t_{0}+1)}\left(\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\frac{\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{T-t_{0}+1}}\rVert_{2}-\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{d}\right)\left\langle\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}\frac{\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{t_{0}-t}},\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}}\right\rangle}{T-t+1}

\displaystyle>-\left(\frac{t_{0}-t}{T-t+1}\right)\left|\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t_{0}+1}-d\right|-\left(\frac{t_{0}-t}{T-t+1}\right)\left|\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{t_{0}-t}-d\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt-\frac{2\sqrt{(t_{0}-t)(T-t_{0}+1)}\left|\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\frac{\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{T-t_{0}+1}}\rVert_{2}-\sqrt{d}\right|\left|\left\langle\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}\frac{\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{t_{0}-t}},\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}}\right\rangle\right|}{T-t+1}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt-\frac{2\sqrt{(t_{0}-t)(T-t_{0}+1)d}\left|\left\langle\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}\frac{\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{t_{0}-t}},\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}}\right\rangle\right|}{T-t+1}.

(A.13)

There exists some
C_{1}\geq 1
so that on the event
\mathcal{E}
defined in Lemma
4
we have:

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}\frac{\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{t_{0}-t}},\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}}{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle<\sqrt{C_{1}\log T}

(on
\mathcal{E}_{3}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\left\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}{T-t_{0}+1}-d\right|

\displaystyle<C_{1}\sqrt{d}\log T

(on
\mathcal{E}_{4}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\left\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}{t_{0}-t}-d\right|

\displaystyle<C_{1}\sqrt{d}\log T

(on
\mathcal{E}_{5}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\left\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}}{\sqrt{T-t_{0}+1}}-\sqrt{d}\right|

\displaystyle<\sqrt{C_{1}\log T}.

(on
\mathcal{E}_{6}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
)

Returning to (
A.13
), the bounds above give:

\displaystyle\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{2(T-t_{0}+1)}-\frac{\lVert\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{2(T-t+1)}

\displaystyle>-\frac{C_{1}(t_{0}-t)\sqrt{d}\log T}{T-t+1}-\frac{2C_{1}\sqrt{(t_{0}-t)(T-t_{0}+1)d}\log T}{T-t+1}.

On
\mathcal{E}
, we therefore have:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>\frac{d}{2}\log\left(\frac{T-t+1}{T-t_{0}+1}\right)+\frac{(t_{0}-t)}{4(T-t+1)}\left[(T-t_{0}+1)\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}-4C_{1}\sqrt{d}\log T-8C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)d}{t_{0}-t}}\log T\right]

\displaystyle>\frac{(t_{0}-t)}{4(T-t+1)}\left[(T-t_{0}+1)\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}-4C_{1}\sqrt{d}\log T-8C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)d}{t_{0}-t}}\log T\right].

(
T-t+1>T-t_{0}+1
)

Note that
\sqrt{(T-t_{0}+1)/(t_{0}-t)}
is increasing as a function of
t
and
\sup\{t\leq t_{0}\>|\>t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}\}=t_{0}-\frac{C_{\beta}\log T}{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{1/2}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}
, so plugging in the value for
t
we have:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>\frac{(t_{0}-t)}{4(T-t+1)}\left[(T-t_{0}+1)\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}-4C_{1}\sqrt{d}\log T-\frac{8C_{1}}{\sqrt{C_{\beta}}}\sqrt{(T-t_{0}+1)\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}d\log T}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{(t_{0}-t)(T-t_{0}+1)\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}}{4(T-t+1)}\left[1-\frac{4C_{1}\sqrt{d}\log T}{(T-t_{0}+1)\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}}-8C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d\log T}{C_{\beta}(T-t_{0}+1)\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{(t_{0}-t)(T-t_{0}+1)\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}}{4(T-t+1)}\left[1-\frac{4C_{1}}{a_{T}\sqrt{d}}-\frac{8C_{1}}{\sqrt{C_{\beta}a_{T}}}\right].

(by (
A.10
))

So fo
T
large enough that
a_{T}\geq\max\{16C_{1},C_{\beta}^{-1}(32C_{1})^{2}\}
, we have:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>\frac{(t_{0}-t)(T-t_{0}+1)\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}}{8(T-t+1)}

The term
(t_{0}-t)/(T-t+1)
is decreasing as a function of
t
, so again plugging in
t=t_{0}-\frac{C_{\beta}\log T}{\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{1/2}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}
gives:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>\frac{C_{\beta}\log T}{8\left(1+\frac{C_{\beta}\log T}{(T-t_{0}+1)\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{1/2}\mathbf{b}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{C_{\beta}\log T}{8\left(1+\frac{C_{\beta}}{da_{T}}\right)}.

(by (
A.10
))

For
C_{\beta}\geq 16C_{\pi}
and
a_{T}\geq C_{\beta}
we get
\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}\geq C_{\pi}\log T
, i.e. the event in (
A.7
) holds for this case.
∎

A.5
Theorem
3
Event Bounds

Lemma 5

(Theorem
3
Event Bounds)
.

Let
\{y_{t}\}_{t=1}^{T}
be a sequence of independent, sub-Gaussian random variables with
\sup_{t\geq 1}\lVert y_{t}\rVert_{\psi_{2}}<\infty
. Let
\mathbb{E}[y_{t}]=b_{0}\mathbbm{1}\{t\geq t_{0}\}
and
{\text{Var}}(y_{t})=(s_{0}^{2})^{\mathbbm{1}\{t\geq t_{0}\}}
, with
b_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
and
s_{0}>0
so that it is possible that
b_{0}=0
or
s_{0}=1
. Define the standardized term
z_{t}:=(y_{t}-b_{0}\mathbbm{1}\{t\geq t_{0}\})/s_{0}^{\mathbbm{1}\{t\geq t_{0}\}}
and suppose that either Assumption
1
,
2
, or both hold.
Then for any
\beta>0
, there exist some constants
C_{1},C_{2},C_{1,\beta},C_{2,\beta}>0
such that if we define the set:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\beta}

\displaystyle:=\left\{1\leq t\leq T-C_{2,\beta}\log(\log T)\>:\>|t_{0}-t|>\frac{C_{1,\beta}\log T}{\max\{\min\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s_{0}^{2}\},(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}\}}\right\}

and the events:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=\min\{t_{0},t\}}^{\max\{t_{0},t\}-1}z_{t^{\prime}}\right|<C_{1}\sqrt{|t_{0}-t|\log T}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right|<C_{1}\sqrt{(T-t+1)\log T}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{3}

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right|<\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{4}

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right|<C_{1}^{1/2}(T-t+1)^{3/4}\log^{1/4}T\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{5}

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=\min\{t_{0},t\}}^{\max\{t_{0},t\}-1}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)\right|\leq C_{1}\sqrt{|t_{0}-t|\log T}+C_{1}\log T\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{6}

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)\right|<\frac{T-t+1}{4}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{7}

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)\right|<C_{1}\sqrt{(T-t+1)\log T}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{8}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\left|b_{0}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right|\leq\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)b_{0}^{2}}{4}\right\},

and the joint event
\mathcal{E}:=\bigcap_{i=1}^{12}\mathcal{E}_{i}
, then we will have
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\geq 1-\frac{C_{2}}{T^{\beta}}-\frac{C_{2,\beta}}{\log^{\beta}T}
.

Proof.

By the union bound
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{c})=1-P(\cup_{i=1}^{12}\mathcal{E}^{c}_{i})<1-\sum_{i=1}^{12}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{c}_{i})
, so it is sufficient to show that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{c}_{i})\leq\frac{C_{i}}{T^{\beta}}+\frac{C_{i,\beta}}{\log^{\beta}T}
for each
i
.

If Assumption
2
does not hold, then we assume
\text{Var}(y_{t})
is constant and it is without loss of generality to assume that
s_{0}=1
. If Assumption
2
does hold, then there is some
\underline{s}>0
such that
s_{0}>\underline{s}
. Let
M_{s}=\min\{1,\underline{s}\}
, then by Lemma 2.6.8 of
Vershynin 2018
we have:

\displaystyle\lVert z_{t}\rVert_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left\lVert y_{t}-b_{0}\mathbbm{1}\{t\geq t_{0}\}\right\rVert_{\psi_{2}}}{M_{s}}\lesssim\frac{\left\lVert y_{t}\right\rVert_{\psi_{2}}}{M_{s}}.

By assumption, there is some
K>0
so that
\sup_{t\geq 1}\lVert y_{t}\rVert_{\psi_{2}}<K
. Therefore,
\lVert z_{t}\rVert_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim M_{s}^{-1}K
, i.e. there exists some constant
K_{1}\geq M_{s}^{-1}K
so that
z_{t}\in\mathcal{SG}(K_{1})
. Then by Lemma
1
, for any index set
\mathcal{T}
, we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}z_{t}\in\mathcal{SG}\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{T}|}K_{1}\right)

and thus, for any index set
\mathcal{T}_{t}
that depends on
t
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{t}}z_{t^{\prime}}\right|\geq C_{1}\sqrt{|\mathcal{T}_{t}|\log T}\right)\leq 2\exp\left[-\frac{C_{1}^{2}\log T}{2K_{1}^{2}}\right].

(Chernoff bound (
A.2
))

So for
C_{1}\geq\sqrt{2K_{1}^{2}(1+\beta)}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}}z_{t^{\prime}}\right|\geq C_{1}\sqrt{|\mathcal{T}_{t}|\log T}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{t}}z_{t^{\prime}}\right|\geq C_{1}\sqrt{|\mathcal{T}_{t}|\log T}\right)

(union bound)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}2\exp\left[-\frac{C^{2}_{1}\log T}{2K_{1}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{T^{\beta}}

(
\mathcal{T}\subset[T]
and
C^{2}_{1}\geq 2K_{1}^{2}(1+\beta)
)

which proves
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{c}_{1}),\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{c}_{2})\leq\frac{2}{T^{\beta}}
.

Next, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{3}^{c})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right|\geq\frac{T-t+1}{4}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right|\geq\frac{T-t+1}{4}\right)

(union bound)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}}2\exp\left[-\frac{T-t+1}{32K^{2}_{1}}\right]

(Chernoff bound (
A.2
))

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{1\leq t\leq T-C_{2,\beta}\log(\log T)}2\exp\left[-\frac{T-t+1}{32K^{2}_{1}}\right]

(
\mathcal{T}_{\beta}\subset[T-C_{2,\beta}\log(\log T)]
)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{1\leq t\leq T-C_{2,\beta}\log T}2\exp\left[-\frac{T-t+1}{32K^{2}_{1}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{T-C_{2,\beta}\log T\leq t\leq T-C_{2,\beta}\log(\log T)}2\exp\left[-\frac{T-t+1}{32K^{2}_{1}}\right].

Note that for the indices of the first sum
T-t+1\geq C_{2,\beta}\log T
and there are at most
T
terms in the sum. Similarly, for the indices of the second sum
T-t+1\geq C_{2,\beta}\log(\log T)
and there are at most
C_{2,\beta}\log T
. Therefore:

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq t\leq T-C_{2,\beta}\log T}2\exp\left[-\frac{T-t+1}{32K^{2}_{1}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left[\log T-\frac{C_{2,\beta}\log T}{32K_{1}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\sum_{T-C_{2,\beta}\log T\leq t\leq T-C_{2,\beta}\log(\log T)}2\exp\left[-\frac{T-t+1}{32K^{2}_{1}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{2,\beta}\exp\left[\log(\log T)-\frac{C_{2,\beta}\log(\log T)}{32K_{1}^{2}}\right].

So for
C_{2,\beta}\geq 32K_{1}^{2}(1+\beta)
we get:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{3}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{T^{\beta}}+\frac{2C_{2,\beta}}{\log^{\beta}T}.

Next, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{4}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right|\geq C_{1}^{1/2}(T-t+1)^{3/4}\log^{1/4}T\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right|\geq C_{1}^{1/2}(T-t+1)^{3/4}\log^{1/4}T\right)

(union bound)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}}2\exp\left[-\frac{C_{1}\sqrt{(T-t+1)\log T}}{2K_{1}^{2}}\right]

(Chernoff bound (
A.2
))

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{1\leq t\leq T-\log T}2\exp\left[-\frac{C_{1}\sqrt{(T-t+1)\log T}}{2K_{1}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\sum_{T-\log T<t\leq T}2\exp\left[-\frac{C_{1}\sqrt{(T-t+1)\log T}}{2K_{1}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left[\log T-\frac{C_{1}\log T}{2K_{1}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad 2\exp\left[\log(\log(T))-\frac{C_{1}\sqrt{\log T}}{2K_{1}^{2}}\right]

We can pick
C_{1}\geq 2K_{1}^{2}(1+\beta)
, then since
\sqrt{T}\gg\log(\log T)
, an identical argument as before gives:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{4}^{c})=

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{T^{\beta}}+\frac{2}{\log^{\beta}T}.

Next, since
\mathbb{E}[z^{2}_{t}]=1
, then by Exercise 2.7.10
Vershynin 2018
,
\lVert z^{2}_{t}-1\rVert_{\psi_{1}}\lesssim\lVert z^{2}_{t}\rVert_{\psi_{1}}
, and by Lemma 2.7.6 of
Vershynin 2018
,
\lVert z^{2}_{t}\rVert_{\psi_{1}}=\lVert z_{t}\rVert_{\psi_{2}}^{2}\leq K_{1}^{2}
. So by Proposition 2.7.1 of
Vershynin 2018
there exists some constant
K_{2}\geq K_{1}^{2}
so that
z_{t}^{2}\in\mathcal{SE}(K_{2},K_{2})
. Then again by Lemma
1
:

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}(z^{2}_{t}-1)\in\mathcal{SE}\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{T}|}K_{2},K_{2}\right).

Using Bernstein’s inequality (see Theorem 2.8.1 of
Vershynin 2018
) for any
t\in[T]
we get:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{5}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=\min\{t_{0},t\}}^{\max\{t_{0},t\}-1}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)\right|>C_{1}\sqrt{|t_{0}-t|\log T}+C_{1}\log T\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2K_{2}}\min\left\{C_{1}\sqrt{|t_{0}-t|\log T}+C_{1}\log T,\frac{C_{1}^{2}(\log T+\log^{2}T/|t_{0}-t|)}{K_{2}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left[-\frac{C_{1}\log T}{2K^{2}_{2}}\right].

(
C_{1},K_{2}>1
WLOG)

So for
C_{1}>2K_{2}^{2}(1+\beta)
, we can use the same union bounding argument to get
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{5}^{c})<T^{-\beta}
.

Note that since
z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}\in\mathcal{SE}(K_{2},K_{2})
and
K_{1}\geq 1
WLOG, then:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)\right|\geq\frac{T-t+1}{4}\right)\leq 2\exp\left[-\frac{T-t+1}{32K_{2}^{2}}\right].

So by an identical argument as we used for
\mathcal{E}_{3}
we can pick
C_{2,\beta}\geq 32K_{2}^{2}(1+\beta)
to get:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{6}^{c})=\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z^{2}_{t^{\prime}}-1\right|\geq\frac{T-t+1}{4}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{T^{\beta}}+\frac{2C_{2,\beta}}{\log^{\beta}T}.

Similarly, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{7}^{c})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)\right|\geq C_{1}\sqrt{(T-t+1)\log T}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)\right|\geq C_{1}\sqrt{(T-t+1)\log T}\right)

(union bound)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}2\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2K_{2}}\min\left\{C_{1}\sqrt{(T-t+1)\log T},\frac{C_{1}^{2}\log T}{K_{2}}\right\}\right]

(Bernstein inequality)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{1\leq t\leq T-\log T}2\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2K_{2}}\min\left\{C_{1}\sqrt{(T-t+1)\log T},\frac{C_{1}^{2}\log T}{K_{2}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{T-\log T<t\leq T}2\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2K_{2}}\min\left\{C_{1}\sqrt{(T-t+1)\log T},\frac{C_{1}^{2}\log T}{K_{2}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left[\log T-\frac{C_{1}\log T}{2K^{2}_{2}}\right]

(
T-t+1\geq\log T
)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{T-\log T<t\leq T}2\exp\left[\log(\log T)-\frac{C_{1}\sqrt{\log T}}{2K^{2}_{2}}\right].

(
T-t+1\geq 1
)

So for
C_{1}\geq 2K_{2}^{2}(1+\beta)
the same argument we used for
\mathcal{E}_{4}
gives:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{7}^{c})=

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{T^{\beta}}+\frac{2}{\log^{\beta}T}.

Finally, note that event
\mathcal{E}_{8}
holds trivially if
b_{0}=0
, while if Assumption
1
holds, then
(T-t_{0}+1)b_{0}^{2}\gg\log T
, so for large
T
we have
(T-t_{0}+1)b_{0}^{2}\geq 32K_{1}^{2}(1+\beta)\log T
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|b_{0}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right|>\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)b_{0}^{2}}{4}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left[-\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)b_{0}^{2}}{32K_{1}^{2}}\right]

(Chernoff bound (
A.2
))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{T^{\beta}}

∎

A.6
Proof of Theorems
2
and
3

Cappello and
Padilla 2025
proved Theorem
2
under the stronger assumptions of Gaussian data,
(s^{2}_{0}-1)^{2}>B
for some fixed
B>0
, and the minimum spacing condition
\Delta_{T}>cT
for some
c\in(0,1/2)
. The generalization of Theorem
2
to sub-Gaussian data, and the signal strength and the minimum spacing conditions implied by Assumption
2
follows the same argument we use in proving Theorem
3
and is therefore omitted.

If we take the ratio of the posterior probabilities of the change-point location from the meanvar-scp model, we get:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\mathbb{P}(\tau=t_{0}\>|\>\mathbf{y}_{1:T};\omega_{0},u_{0},v_{0},\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T})}{\mathbb{P}(\tau=t\>|\>\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>;\omega_{0},u_{0},v_{0},\boldsymbol{\pi})}

\displaystyle=\log\frac{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\tau=t_{0}\>;\omega_{0},u_{0},v_{0})\mathbb{P}(\tau=t_{0}\>;\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T})/p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>;\omega_{0},u_{0},v_{0},\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T})}{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\tau=t\>;\omega_{0},u_{0},v_{0})\mathbb{P}(\tau=t\>;\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T})/p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>;\omega_{0},u_{0},v_{0},\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T})}

(Bayes’ rule)

\displaystyle=\log\frac{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\tau=t_{0}\>;\omega_{0},u_{0},v_{0})}{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\tau=t\>;\omega_{0},u_{0},v_{0})}+\log\frac{\pi_{t_{0}}}{\pi_{t}}.

For some
C_{1,\beta},C_{2,\beta}>0
we can again define the set:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\beta}

\displaystyle:=\left\{1\leq t\leq T-C_{2,\beta}\log(\log T)\>:\>|t_{0}-t|>\frac{C_{1,\beta}\log T}{\max\{\min\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s_{0}^{2}\},(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}\}}\right\}

By the assumptions of Theorem
3
, for large
T
there is some constant
C_{\pi}>0
such that for all
t\in[T]
:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\pi_{t}}{\pi_{t_{0}}}\leq C_{\pi}\log T.

(A.14)

Therefore, if we can show that for large enough
C_{1,\beta}
and
C_{2,\beta}
:

\displaystyle\lim_{T\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcap_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}}\left\{\log\frac{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\tau=t_{0}\>;\omega_{0},u_{0},v_{0})}{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\tau=t\>;\omega_{0},u_{0},v_{0})}>C_{\pi}\log T\right\}\right)=1

(A.15)

then with probability approaching one as
T\to\infty
we will have:

\displaystyle\max_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}}\;\mathbb{P}(\tau=t\;|\;\mathbf{y}_{1:T};\omega_{0},u_{0},v_{0},\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T})<\mathbb{P}(\tau=t_{0}\;|\;\mathbf{y}_{1:T};\omega_{0},u_{0},v_{0},\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T}).

We now prove that there exists a
C_{1,\beta},C_{2,\beta}>0
such that the event in (
A.15
) holds on the event
\mathcal{E}
defined in Lemma
5
.

Proof.

We begin by directly calculating the marginal evidence of
\mathbf{y}_{1:T}
:

\displaystyle p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\;|\;\tau=t;\omega_{0},u_{0},v_{0})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathbb{P}(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>b,s,\tau=t)\>\;d\mathbb{P}(b,s;\omega_{0},u_{0},v_{0})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\prod_{t=1}^{T}p(y_{t}\;|\;b,s,\tau=t)p(b|s;\omega_{0})p(s;u_{0},v_{0})\;db\;ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(2\pi)^{-T/2}s^{(T-t+1)/2}\exp\left[-\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}s(y_{t^{\prime}}-b)^{2}}{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times(2\pi s\omega_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\exp\left[-\frac{\omega_{0}sb^{2}}{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\frac{v_{0}^{u_{0}}}{\Gamma(u_{0})}s^{u_{0}-1}\exp(-v_{0}s)\;db\;ds

\displaystyle\propto\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}\right]\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}s^{\frac{1}{2}}\exp\left[-\frac{s}{2}\left((\omega_{0}+T-t+1)b^{2}-2b\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}y_{t^{\prime}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times s^{u_{0}+\frac{T-t+1}{2}-1}\exp\left[-s\left(v_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}\right)\right]\;db\;ds

\displaystyle=\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}\right]\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}s^{\frac{1}{2}}\exp\left[-\frac{s\overline{\omega}_{t}}{2}\left(b-\overline{b}_{t}\right)^{2}\right]s^{\overline{u}_{t}-1}\exp\left[-s\overline{v}_{t}\right]\;db\;ds

(
\overline{\omega}_{t}=\omega_{0}+T-t+1,\;\overline{b}_{t}=\overline{\omega}_{t}^{-1}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}y_{t^{\prime}},\;\overline{u}_{t}=u_{0}+\frac{T-t+1}{2},\;\overline{v}_{t}=v_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-\frac{\overline{\omega}_{t}\overline{b}_{t}^{2}}{2}
)

\displaystyle\propto\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}\right]\overline{\omega}_{t}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\Gamma(\overline{u}_{t})}{\overline{v}_{t}^{\overline{u}_{T}}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}p(b|s;\overline{b}_{t},\overline{\omega}_{t})p(s;\overline{u}_{t},\overline{v}_{t})\;db\;ds

\displaystyle=\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}\right]\overline{\omega}_{t}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\Gamma(\overline{u}_{t})}{\overline{v}_{t}^{\overline{u}_{T}}}.

Taking the log of the expression above we get:

\displaystyle\log p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\;|\;\tau=t;\omega_{0},u_{0},v_{0})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\log\overline{\omega}_{t}+\log\Gamma(\overline{u}_{t})-\overline{u}_{t}\log\overline{v}_{t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\log(\omega_{0}+T-t+1)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}+\log\Gamma\left(u_{0}+\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\>-\left(u_{0}+\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[v_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-\frac{\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}y_{t^{\prime}}\right)^{2}}{\omega_{0}+T-t+1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad\>+C

where
C
is a constant that does not depend on the value of
\tau
. Recall Binet’s first formula for the log gamma function for
x>0
(see e.g. p. 249 of
Whittaker and
Watson 1996
):

\displaystyle\log\Gamma(x)=\left(x-\frac{1}{2}\right)\log x-x+\frac{1}{2}\log 2\pi+\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{t}+\frac{1}{e^{t}-1}\right)\frac{e^{-tx}}{t}\;dt.

For
x\geq 1/2
we have:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{t}+\frac{1}{e^{t}-1}\right)\frac{e^{-tx}}{t}\;dt\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{t}+\frac{1}{e^{t}-1}\right)\frac{e^{-t/2}}{t}\;dt\approx 0.153

and since
\frac{T-t+1}{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}
, we can write:

\displaystyle\log\Gamma\left(u_{0}+\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)=\left(u_{0}+\frac{T-t}{2}\right)\log\left(u_{0}+\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)-\frac{T-t+1}{2}+\mathcal{O}(1)

and thus:

\displaystyle\log p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\;|\;\tau=t;\omega_{0},u_{0},v_{0})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\log(\omega_{0}+T-t+1)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\>+\left(u_{0}+\frac{T-t}{2}\right)\log\left(u_{0}+\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)-\frac{(T-t+1)}{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\>-\left(u_{0}+\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[v_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-\frac{\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}y_{t^{\prime}}\right)^{2}}{\omega_{0}+T-t+1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\>+\mathcal{O}(1).

Letting
\{\omega_{0},u_{0},v_{0}\}\to\mathbf{0}
, we have:

\displaystyle\lim_{\omega_{0},u_{0},v_{0}\to\mathbf{0}}\log p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\;|\;\tau=t;\omega_{0},u_{0},v_{0})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\log(T-t+1)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\>+\left(\frac{T-t}{2}\right)\log\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)-\frac{(T-t+1)}{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\>-\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-\frac{\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}y_{t^{\prime}}\right)^{2}}{T-t+1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\>+\mathcal{O}(1)

\displaystyle=-\log(T-t+1)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-\frac{(T-t+1)}{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\>-\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}}{T-t+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}y_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t+1}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\>+\mathcal{O}(1)

\displaystyle:=\log\alpha_{t}.

(A.16)

Note that when
t=T
, then:

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}}{T-t+1}=\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}y_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t+1}\right)^{2}

but
T\not\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}
, so
\alpha_{t}
is still well-defined for each
t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}
. As in the proof of Theorem
1
, we can use
\alpha_{t}
to approximate the log-evidence for small values of
\omega_{0}
,
u_{0}
, and
v_{0}
(see Footnote
1
). Let
C_{1,\beta}
be large enough so that the statement of Lemma
5
holds. Then we will be on the event
\mathcal{E}
defined in Lemma
5
with probability approaching one as
T\to\infty
. We can show that the event in (
A.15
) holds on
\mathcal{E}
. To do so, we define the standardized terms:

\displaystyle z_{t}=\begin{cases}y_{t},&\text{if }t<t_{0},\\
\frac{y_{t}-b_{0}}{s_{0}},&\text{if }t\geq t_{0}.\end{cases}

There are two cases to consider:

Case 1:
t>t_{0}
, and
t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}
.

From (
A.16
) we have:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle=\log\left(\frac{T-t+1}{T-t_{0}+1}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-\frac{(t-t_{0})}{2}

\displaystyle\quad\>+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}}{T-t+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}y_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t+1}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\>-\left(\frac{T-t_{0}+1}{2}\right)\log\left[\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}}{T-t_{0}+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}y_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t_{0}+1}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\>+\mathcal{O}(1).

Since
t>t_{0}
, we can standardize each term in the sums to get:

\displaystyle\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}(s_{0}z_{t^{\prime}}+b_{0})^{2}

\displaystyle=s_{0}^{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)+2b_{0}s_{0}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}z_{t^{\prime}}+(t-t_{0})(b_{0}^{2}+s_{0}^{2})

and:

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}}{T-t+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}y_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t+1}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(s_{0}z_{t^{\prime}}+b_{0})^{2}}{T-t+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(s_{0}z_{t^{\prime}}+b_{0})}{T-t+1}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=s_{0}^{2}\left[\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}}{T-t+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t+1}\right)^{2}\right].

If we define the function
f(x):=x-\log(x)-1
,
2
2

2

Note that this function previously appeared in
Bai 2010
as a measure of the signal strength of
s_{0}^{2}
.
then we now have:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle=\log\left(\frac{T-t+1}{T-t_{0}+1}\right)+\frac{s_{0}^{2}}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)+b_{0}s_{0}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}z_{t^{\prime}}+\left(\frac{t-t_{0}}{2}\right)[b_{0}^{2}+f(s_{0}^{2})]

\displaystyle\quad\>+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}}{T-t+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t+1}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\>-\left(\frac{T-t_{0}+1}{2}\right)\log\left[\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}}{T-t_{0}+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t_{0}+1}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\>+\mathcal{O}(1).

(A.17)

For any
t<T
, because
x^{2}
is strictly convex on
(0,\infty)
, then with probability one we have:

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}}{T-t+1}>\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t+1}\right)^{2}\implies\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t+1}\right)^{2}>-1.

(A.18)

Therefore, we can use the inequality
\frac{x}{1+x}\leq\log(1+x)\leq x
for
x>-1
to get:

\displaystyle-\log\left[1+\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)}{T-t_{0}+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t_{0}+1}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)}{T-t_{0}+1}+\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t_{0}+1}\right)^{2}>-\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)}{T-t_{0}+1}

and:

\displaystyle\log\left[1+\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t+1}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t+1}\right)^{2}}{1+\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t+1}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t+1}\right)^{2}-\frac{\left[\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t+1}\right)^{2}\right]^{2}}{1+\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t+1}\right)^{2}}.

Combining these bounds with (
A.17
) gives:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle\geq\log\left(\frac{T-t+1}{T-t_{0}+1}\right)+\left(\frac{t-t_{0}}{2}\right)\left[b_{0}^{2}+f(s_{0}^{2})\right]+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)+b_{0}s_{0}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}z_{t^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad\>-\frac{\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right)^{2}}{2(T-t+1)}-\frac{\frac{(T-t+1)}{2}\left[\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t+1}\right)^{2}\right]^{2}}{1+\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t+1}\right)^{2}}+\mathcal{O}(1).

(A.19)

On the event
\mathcal{E}
defined in Lemma
5
, for some constant
C_{1}>0
we have:

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}z_{t^{\prime}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sqrt{(t-t_{0})\log T},

(on
\mathcal{E}_{1}\subset\mathcal{E}
)

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{t-1}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sqrt{(t-t_{0})\log T}+C_{1}\log T

(on
\mathcal{E}_{5}\subset\mathcal{E}
)

and since
-C_{1}|s_{0}^{2}-1|\log T\geq-C_{1}(\overline{s}^{2}+1)\log T=\mathcal{O}(\log T)
and
\log\left(\frac{T-t+1}{T-t_{0}+1}\right)=\mathcal{O}(\log T)
, we have

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{t-t_{0}}{2}\right)\left[b_{0}^{2}+f(s_{0}^{2})\right]-\left(\frac{|s_{0}^{2}-1|}{2}+|b_{0}|s_{0}\right)C_{1}\sqrt{(t-t_{0})\log T}

\displaystyle\quad\>-\frac{\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right)^{2}}{2(T-t+1)}-\frac{\frac{(T-t+1)}{2}\left[\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t+1}\right)^{2}\right]^{2}}{1+\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t+1}\right)^{2}}+\mathcal{O}(\log T).

(A.20)

Next, on the event
\mathcal{E}
defined in Lemma
5
, for the same universal constant
C_{1}>0
we have:

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right|

\displaystyle<C_{1}\sqrt{(T-t+1)\log T}

(on
\mathcal{E}_{2}\subset\mathcal{E}
)

and thus:

\displaystyle-\frac{\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right)^{2}}{2(T-t+1)}>-\frac{C_{1}^{2}}{2}\log T.

Next, for
t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}
on the event
\mathcal{E}
, we have:

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t+1}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2},

(on
\mathcal{E}_{3}\subset\mathcal{E}
)

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right|

\displaystyle<C_{1}^{1/2}(T-t+1)^{3/4}\log^{1/4}T

(on
\mathcal{E}_{4}\subset\mathcal{E}
)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4},

(on
\mathcal{E}_{6}\subset\mathcal{E}
)

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)\right|

\displaystyle<C_{1}\sqrt{(T-t+1)\log T},

(on
\mathcal{E}_{7}\subset\mathcal{E}
)

so on
\mathcal{E}
:

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{(T-t+1)}{2}\left[\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t+1}\right)^{2}\right]^{2}}{1+\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}-\left(\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}}{T-t+1}\right)^{2}}\leq 4C_{1}^{2}\log T.

Combining these results, we can rewrite (
A.20
) on
\mathcal{E}
as the deterministic bound:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{t-t_{0}}{2}\right)\left[b_{0}^{2}+f(s_{0}^{2})\right]-\left(\frac{|s_{0}^{2}-1|}{2}+|b_{0}|s_{0}\right)C_{1}\sqrt{(t-t_{0})\log T}+\mathcal{O}(\log T).

(A.21)

We now show that bound (
A.21
) is increasing as a function of
t-t_{0}
. Taking the derivative of this bound with respect to
t-t_{0}
yields:

\displaystyle\frac{b_{0}^{2}+f(s_{0}^{2})}{2}-\frac{C_{1}}{2}\left(\frac{|s_{0}^{2}-1|}{2}+|b_{0}|s_{0}\right)\sqrt{\frac{\log T}{t-t_{0}}}.

(A.22)

Note that (
A.22
) is decreasing as a function of
t-t_{0}
. Suppose that:

\displaystyle\min\left\{b_{0}^{2},\frac{b_{0}^{2}}{s_{0}^{2}}\right\}\geq(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}\implies|t-t_{0}|\geq\frac{C_{1,\beta}\log T}{\min\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s_{0}^{2}\}},\;\forall\;t\in\mathcal{T}_{\beta}

and thus (
A.22
