\displaystyle\quad\>+\mathcal{O}(1).

(A.45)

As in the proof of Theorem
3
, we treat the case of
t_{0}-t\geq T-t_{0}+1
and
t_{0}-t<T-t_{0}+1
separately.

Case 2.a:
t_{0}-t\leq T-t_{0}+1
.

We can start by rewriting bound (
A.45
) as:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)+\frac{(t_{0}-t)}{2}\log s_{0}^{2}+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{\frac{(t_{0}-t)(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{T-t+1}}{1+s_{0}^{-2}\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\>+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{2(T-t+1)^{2}}-\frac{2(T-t_{0}+1)|b_{0}|C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T}{(T-t+1)^{2}}}{1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}+\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\>-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)+\frac{1}{2(T-t_{0}+1)}\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+s_{0}^{-2}\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})\right]

\displaystyle\quad\>+\mathcal{O}(1).

(A.46)

For some
C_{1}\geq 1
, on
\mathcal{E}
we have:

\displaystyle\max\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)\right|,\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)\right|,\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}z_{t^{\prime}}\right|,\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}(T-t+1)}\log T

(on
\cap_{i=1}^{4}\mathcal{E}_{i}\subset\mathcal{E}
)

and thus:

\displaystyle\left|s_{0}^{-2}\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\leq(1+s_{0}^{-2}+2s_{0}^{-1})\left[\frac{C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{\sqrt{T-t+1}}+\frac{C_{1}^{2}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{T-t+1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\underline{s}^{2}C_{1}^{2}}{C_{2,a}}.

(
s_{0}^{2}>\underline{s}^{2}
and
T-t+1\geq C_{2,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T
)

So for
C_{2,a}>8\underline{s}^{2}C_{1}^{2}
, we have
|s_{0}^{-2}\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})|>-1
and we can use the inequality
\log(1+x)>x/(x+1)
for
x>-1
to write:

\displaystyle\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+s_{0}^{-2}\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})\right]

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\left[s_{0}^{-2}\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})-\frac{s_{0}^{-4}\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})^{2}}{1+s_{0}^{-2}\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2s_{0}^{2}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)-\frac{\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}z_{t^{\prime}}\right)^{2}}{2s_{0}^{2}(T-t+1)}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)-\frac{\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right)^{2}}{2(T-t+1)}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt\;-\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}z_{t^{\prime}}}{s_{0}(T-t+1)}-\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\frac{s_{0}^{-4}\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})^{2}}{1+s_{0}^{-2}\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}

We also have:

\displaystyle\frac{\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}z_{t^{\prime}}\right)^{2}}{2s_{0}^{2}(T-t+1)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}^{2}}{2\underline{s}^{2}}a_{T}\log^{2}T

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}z_{t^{\prime}}}{s_{0}(T-t+1)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}^{2}}{\underline{s}}a_{T}\log^{2}T.

For
C_{2,a}>16\underline{s}^{2}C_{1}^{2}
:

\displaystyle\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\frac{s_{0}^{-4}\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})^{2}}{1+s_{0}^{-2}\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq(T-t+1)(1+s_{0}^{-2}+2s_{0}^{-1})^{2}\left[\frac{C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{\sqrt{T-t+1}}+\frac{C_{1}^{2}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{T-t+1}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16\underline{s}^{2}C_{1}^{4}a_{T}^{2}\log^{4}T}{T-t+1}

(
s_{0}^{2}\geq\underline{s}^{2}
)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}^{2}a_{T}\log^{2}T.

(
T-t+1\geq C_{2,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T>16\underline{s}^{2}C_{1}^{2}a_{T}\log^{2}T
)

Furthermore, since
T-t+1\geq T-t_{0}+1
, we have:

\displaystyle\frac{\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right)^{2}}{2(T-t_{0}+1)}-\frac{\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right)^{2}}{2(T-t+1)}\geq 0

so returning to bound (
A.46
) we have:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>\frac{(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)+\frac{(t_{0}-t)}{2}\log s_{0}^{2}+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{\frac{(t_{0}-t)(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{T-t+1}}{1+s_{0}^{-2}\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\>+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{2(T-t+1)^{2}}-\frac{2(T-t_{0}+1)|b_{0}|C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T}{(T-t+1)^{2}}}{1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}+\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\>+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T).

(A.47)

Note that if
s_{0}^{2}=1
, then we can completely ignore the first line of (
A.47
), on the other hand, if
s_{0}^{2}\neq 1
, then by assumption
(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}(T-t_{0}+1)\gg a_{T}\log^{2}T
. However,
0<\underline{s}^{2}\leq s_{0}^{2}\leq\overline{s}^{2}<\infty
implies
(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, so we must have
T-t_{0}+1\gg a_{T}\log^{2}T
. We showed above that on
\mathcal{E}
:

\displaystyle\left|s_{0}^{-2}\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\leq(1+s_{0}^{-2}+2s_{0}^{-1})\left[\frac{C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{\sqrt{T-t+1}}+\frac{C_{1}^{2}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{T-t+1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 4\underline{s}^{2}\left[\frac{C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{\sqrt{T-t_{0}+1}}+\frac{C_{1}^{2}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{T-t_{0}+1}\right]

(
T-t+1\geq T-t_{0}+1
)

i.e.,
\left|s_{0}^{-2}\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})\right|\to 0
on
\mathcal{E}
when
s_{0}^{2}\neq 1
. Next, we can write:

\displaystyle\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{\frac{(t_{0}-t)(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{T-t+1}}{1+s_{0}^{-2}\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}\right]

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{(t_{0}-t)(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{T-t+1}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt\>+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1-\left(\frac{s_{0}^{-2}\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}{1+s_{0}^{-2}\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}\right)\left(\frac{\frac{(t_{0}-t)(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{T-t+1}}{1+\frac{(t_{0}-t)(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{T-t+1}}\right)\right].

We have just shown that:

\displaystyle\frac{s_{0}^{-2}\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}{1+s_{0}^{-2}\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{\sqrt{T-t_{0}+1}}\right),

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{(t_{0}-t)(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{T-t+1}}{1+\frac{(t_{0}-t)(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{T-t+1}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{(t_{0}-t)(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{T-t+1}\right)=\mathcal{O}(1)

(
s_{0}^{-2}=\mathcal{O}(1)
)

so for some constant
C_{2}>0
, a first order Taylor approximation of
\log(1+x)
around zero gives:

\displaystyle\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1-\left(\frac{\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}{1+\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}\right)\left(\frac{\frac{(t_{0}-t)(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{T-t+1}}{1+\frac{(t_{0}-t)(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{T-t_{0}+1}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=(t_{0}-t)(s_{0}^{-2}-1)\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{\sqrt{T-t_{0}+1}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T)

\displaystyle=(s_{0}^{-2}-1)\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T\right)+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T)

(
t_{0}-t\leq T-t_{0}+1
)

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{C_{2}}{2}|s_{0}^{-2}-1|\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T)

We also have:

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)\right|<C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T

(on
\mathcal{E}_{2}\subset\mathcal{E}
)

and since
C_{1}>C_{2}
WLOG, then we can now lower bound (
A.47
) with:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-C_{1}|s_{0}^{-2}-1|\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T+\frac{(t_{0}-t)}{2}\log s_{0}^{2}+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{(t_{0}-t)(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{T-t+1}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\>+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{2(T-t+1)^{2}}-\frac{2(T-t_{0}+1)|b_{0}|C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T}{(T-t+1)^{2}}}{1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}+\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\>+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T).

(A.48)

Case 2.a.i:
\min\left\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s_{0}^{2}\right\}\geq(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}

Suppose that:

\displaystyle\min\left\{b_{0}^{2},\frac{b_{0}^{2}}{s_{0}^{2}}\right\}\geq(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}

\displaystyle\implies|t-t_{0}|\geq\frac{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{\min\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s_{0}^{2}\}},\;\forall\;t\in\mathcal{T}_{a_{T}}.

For
C_{1,a}>64C_{1}^{2}
, we can write:

\displaystyle\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{2(T-t+1)^{2}}-\frac{2(T-t_{0}+1)|b_{0}|C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T}{(T-t+1)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{(T-t+1)^{2}}\left[\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{|b_{0}|\sqrt{t_{0}-t}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{(T-t+1)^{2}}\left[\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2C_{1}}{\sqrt{C_{1,a}}}\right]

(
|t-t_{0}|\geq\frac{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{\min\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s_{0}^{2}\}}
)

\displaystyle>\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{4(T-t+1)^{2}}.

(
C_{1,a}>64C_{1}^{2}
)

Furthermore, we have shown that on
\mathcal{E}
:

\displaystyle\left|\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\leq(1+s_{0}^{2}+2s_{0})\left[\frac{C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{\sqrt{T-t+1}}+\frac{C_{1}^{2}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{T-t+1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\overline{s}^{2}C_{1}^{2}}{C_{2,a}}

(
s_{0}^{2}<\overline{s}^{2}
and
T-t+1\geq C_{2,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T
)

and we have:

\displaystyle-1<\underline{s}^{2}-1<\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}<\overline{s}^{2}-1<\infty.

So for
C_{2,a}>8C_{1}^{2}
, in this case we have:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-C_{1}|s_{0}^{-2}-1|\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T+\frac{(t_{0}-t)}{2}\log s_{0}^{2}+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{(t_{0}-t)(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{T-t+1}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\>+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}\right]+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T)

Note that the term:

\displaystyle\frac{(t_{0}-t)}{2}\log s_{0}^{2}+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{(t_{0}-t)(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{T-t+1}\right]

is decreasing in
t
and is clearly equal to zero when
t=t_{0}
. To see this we can take the derivative with respect
t
to get:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}}{1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}}-\log\left[1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq 0.

(
\log(1+x)>x/(1+x)
for
x>-1
)

Therefore:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-C_{1}|s_{0}^{-2}-1|\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}\right]+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T)

(A.49)

Suppose first that
\lim_{T\to\infty}b_{0}^{2}=0
, then for large
T
:

\displaystyle\lim_{T\to\infty}\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}=0\implies\log\left[1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)^{2}b_{0}^{4}}{(T-t+1)^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{32\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}.

So for large
T
, we can lower bound (
A.49
) in this case with:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-C_{1}|s_{0}^{-2}-1|\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{64\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)}+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T)

\displaystyle\geq(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}\left[\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)}{64\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)}-\frac{C_{1}s_{0}^{-2}\min\{1,s_{0}^{-1}\}\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{|b_{0}|\sqrt{t_{0}-t}}\right]+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T)

(
(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}\leq\min\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s_{0}^{2}\}
)

\displaystyle\geq(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}\left[\frac{1}{128\overline{s}^{2}}-\frac{C_{1}\underline{s}^{-2}}{\sqrt{C_{1,a}}}\right]+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T)

(
|t_{0}-t|\geq\frac{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{\min\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s_{0}^{2}\}}
)

so for
C_{1,a}>(128C_{1}\overline{s}^{2}/\underline{s}^{2})^{2}
and some
B_{1}>0
, we have:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T)

\displaystyle\geq(C_{1,a}-B_{1})a_{T}\log^{2}T

(
|t_{0}-t|\geq\frac{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{\min\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s_{0}^{2}\}}
)

and thus (
A.38
) holds for
C_{1,a}>B_{1}+C_{\pi}
.

Next, if
b_{0}^{2}>\underline{b}^{2}
for some
\underline{b}^{2}>0
, then we can show that the bound (
A.49
) is decreasing in
t
. We verify below that the second term is decreasing in
t
, and the first term disappears when
s_{0}^{2}=1
, so it is without loss of generality to assume that
s_{0}^{2}\neq 1
. Taking the derivative with respect to
t
yields:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{C_{1}|s_{0}^{-2}-1|\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{\sqrt{t_{0}-t}}-\log\left[1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}\right]+\frac{\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{8\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}-\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)}}{1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}}\right]

(A.50)

Suppose that
b_{0}^{2}\to\infty
as
T\to\infty
, then since we can write:

\displaystyle-\log\left[1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}\right]+\frac{\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{8\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}-\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)}}{1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}}\leq-\frac{\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)^{2}b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}}{1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}}

(
\log(1+x)\geq x/(1+x)
for
x>-1
)

and:

\displaystyle\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)^{2}b_{0}^{2}}{(T-t+1)^{2}}=\frac{b_{0}^{2}}{\left(1+\frac{t_{0}-t}{T-t_{0}+1}\right)^{2}}\geq\frac{b_{0}^{2}}{4}.

(
t_{0}-t\leq T-t_{0}+1
)

Then for large
T
:

\displaystyle-\log\left[1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}\right]+\frac{\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{8\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}-\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)}}{1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}}\lesssim-\frac{T-t_{0}+1}{t_{0}-t}.

At the same time we have:

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{T-t_{0}+1}{t_{0}-t}}{\frac{C_{1}|s_{0}^{-2}-1|\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{\sqrt{t_{0}-t}}}\geq\frac{\sqrt{T-t_{0}+1}}{C_{1}|\underline{s}^{-2}-1|\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}.

(
t_{0}-t\leq T-t_{0}+1
)

Since
s_{0}^{2}\neq 0
, then by assumption
(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}(T-t_{0}+1)\gg a_{T}\log^{2}T
, but since
(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, then
T-t_{0}+1\gg a_{T}\log^{2}T
and the lower bound above is diverging as
T\to\infty
, and thus the first term in (
A.50
) is dominated by the remaining terms and we get that (
A.50
) is negative for large
T
.

On the other hand, if
b_{0}^{2}=\mathcal{O}(1)
so that
b_{0}^{2}<\overline{b}^{2}
for some
\overline{b}^{2}<\infty
, then since the first term of (
A.50
) is decreasing in
t
, we have:

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}|s_{0}^{-2}-1|\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{\sqrt{t_{0}-t}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}\min\{|b_{0}|,|b_{0}|/s_{0}\}\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{s_{0}^{2}\sqrt{t_{0}-t}}

(
(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}\leq\min\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s_{0}^{2}\}
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}\min\{|b_{0}|,|b_{0}|/s_{0}\}\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{s_{0}^{2}\sqrt{t_{0}-t}}\bigg|_{t=\sup\{t\in\mathcal{T}_{a_{T}}:t\leq t_{0},\;t_{0}-t\leq T-t_{0}+1\}}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1}\min\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s^{2}_{0}\}}{s_{0}^{2}\sqrt{C_{1,a}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}\overline{b}^{2}}{\underline{s}^{4}\sqrt{C_{1,a}}}

(
|b_{0}|\leq\overline{b}<\infty
)

while defining:

\displaystyle h(t):=\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)}

and taking the derivative of the other terms with respect to
t
yields:

\displaystyle\frac{h^{\prime\prime}(t)}{1+\frac{h(t)}{T-t+1}}-\frac{\left[\frac{h(t)}{T-t+1}+h^{\prime}(t)\right]^{2}}{(T-t+1)\left[1+\frac{h(t)}{T-t+1}\right]^{2}}.

Since:

\displaystyle h^{\prime\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=-\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)^{2}b_{0}^{2}}{8\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{3}}<0

then:

\displaystyle-\log\left[1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}\right]+\frac{\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{8\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}-\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)}}{1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq-\log\left[1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}\right]+\frac{\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{8\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}-\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)}}{1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}}\bigg|_{t=\inf\{t\in\mathcal{T}_{a_{T}}:t\leq t_{0},\;t_{0}-t\leq T-t_{0}+1\}}

\displaystyle=-\log\left[1+\frac{b_{0}^{2}}{32\overline{s}^{2}}\right]+\frac{\frac{b_{0}^{2}}{32\overline{s}^{2}}}{1+\frac{b_{0}^{2}}{32\overline{s}^{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq-\log\left[1+\frac{\underline{b}^{2}}{32\overline{s}^{2}}\right]+\frac{\frac{\underline{b}^{2}}{32\overline{s}^{2}}}{1+\frac{\underline{b}^{2}}{32\overline{s}^{2}}}

(
0<\underline{b}<|b_{0}|
)

Once more,
\log(1+x)>x/(1+x)
for
x>-1
, and since the last bound above is fixed with respect to
T
, then there is some
b^{*}>0
so that:

\displaystyle-\log\left[1+\frac{\underline{b}^{2}}{32\overline{s}^{2}}\right]+\frac{\frac{\underline{b}^{2}}{32\overline{s}^{2}}}{1+\frac{\underline{b}^{2}}{32\overline{s}^{2}}}<-b^{*}.

So if
C_{1,a}>(C_{1}\overline{b}^{2}/\underline{s}^{4}b^{*})^{2}
, then (
A.50
) is negative and the bound (
A.49
) is minimized at:

\displaystyle t

\displaystyle=\sup\;\{t\in\mathcal{T}_{a_{T}}:t<t_{0}\}

\displaystyle\leq t_{0}-\frac{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{\min\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s_{0}^{2}\}}.

and thus:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-\frac{C_{1}|s_{0}^{2}-1|\sqrt{C_{1,a}}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{s_{0}^{2}\min\{|b_{0}|,|b_{0}|/s_{0}\}}

\displaystyle\quad\>+\frac{T-t_{0}+1}{2}\left(1+\frac{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{\min\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s_{0}^{2}\}(T-t_{0}+1)}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{\frac{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t_{0}+1)\min\{1,s_{0}^{-2}\}}}{\left(1+\frac{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{\min\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s_{0}^{2}\}(T-t_{0}+1)}\right)^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\>+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T).

By assumption we have:

\displaystyle\min\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s_{0}^{2}\}(T-t_{0}+1)

\displaystyle\gg a_{T}\log^{2}T

so for any
C_{1,a}>0
, for large
T
we have:

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{\min\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s_{0}^{2}\}(T-t_{0}+1)}\leq\sqrt{2}-1

and:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-\frac{C_{1}|s_{0}^{2}-1|\sqrt{C_{1,a}}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{s_{0}^{2}\min\{|b_{0}|,|b_{0}|/s_{0}\}}+\frac{T-t_{0}+1}{2}\log\left[1+\frac{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{32\overline{s}^{2}(T-t_{0}+1)}\right]+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T)

Suppose that
b_{0}^{2}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, then it must be the case that
T-t_{0}+1\gg a_{T}\log^{2}T
, in which case we can use another first order Taylor approximation to write:

\displaystyle\frac{T-t_{0}+1}{2}\log\left[1+\frac{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{32\overline{s}^{2}(T-t_{0}+1)}\right]=\frac{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{32\overline{s}^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(a_{T}\log^{2}T\right)

and since
(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}\leq\min\{|b_{0}|,|b_{0}|/s_{0}\}
, for some
B_{1}>0
we have:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-\frac{C_{1}\sqrt{C_{1,a}}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{\underline{s}^{2}}+\frac{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{32\overline{s}^{2}}-B_{1}a_{T}\log^{2}T

so we can select
C_{1,a}>0
large so that (
A.38
) holds. On the other hand, if
b_{0}^{2}\to\infty
, then we make use of the assumption:

\displaystyle T-t_{0}+1\gtrsim a_{T}\log^{2}T

to write:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-\frac{C_{1}\overline{s}(\underline{s}^{-2}+1)\sqrt{C_{1,a}}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{|b_{0}|}+\frac{B_{2}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{2}\log\left[1+\frac{C_{1,a}}{32\overline{s}^{2}B_{2}}\right]-B_{1}a_{T}\log^{2}T

for some
B_{2}>0
. Since
|b_{0}|\to\infty
, for large
T
we

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}\overline{s}(\underline{s}^{-2}+1)}{|b_{0}|}\leq B_{1}

and clearly we can pick
C_{1,a}>0
large so that:

\displaystyle\log\left[1+\frac{C_{1,a}}{32\overline{s}^{2}B_{2}}\right]>2B_{1}+C_{\pi}

so again (
A.38
) holds for this case.

Case 2.a.ii:
\min\left\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s_{0}^{2}\right\}\leq(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}

Returning to bound (
A.48
), we can write:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-C_{1}|s_{0}^{-2}-1|\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T+\frac{(t_{0}-t)}{2}\log s_{0}^{2}+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{(t_{0}-t)(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{T-t+1}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\>+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1-\frac{\frac{2(T-t_{0}+1)|b_{0}|C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T}{(T-t+1)^{2}}}{1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}+\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\>+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T).

(A.51)

Since
(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}=\mathcal{O}(1)
by assumption, then:

\displaystyle(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}(T-t_{0}+1)\gg a_{T}\log^{2}T\implies T-t_{0}+1\gg a_{T}\log^{2}T.

We have shown that on
\mathcal{E}
:

\displaystyle\left|\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\leq(1+s_{0}^{2}+2s_{0})\left[\frac{C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{\sqrt{T-t+1}}+\frac{C_{1}^{2}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{T-t+1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 4\overline{s}^{2}\left[\frac{C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{\sqrt{T-t_{0}+1}}+\frac{C_{1}^{2}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{T-t_{0}+1}\right]

so for large
T
we have
\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})>-\underline{s}^{2}/2
, and we have:

\displaystyle-1<\underline{s}^{2}-1<\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}<\overline{s}^{2}-1<\infty

so:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-C_{1}|s_{0}^{-2}-1|\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T+\frac{(t_{0}-t)}{2}\log s_{0}^{2}+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{(t_{0}-t)(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{T-t+1}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\>+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1-\frac{4(T-t_{0}+1)|b_{0}|C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T}{\underline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\>+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T).

(A.52)

Next, since
(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}=\mathcal{O}(1)
and
\min\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s_{0}^{2}\}\leq(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}
, then then there is some
\overline{b}>0
so that:

\displaystyle\frac{4(T-t_{0}+1)|b_{0}|C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T}{\underline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}\leq\frac{4\overline{b}C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{\underline{s}^{2}\sqrt{T-t_{0}+1}}.

(
T-t+1\geq T-t_{0}+1\geq t_{0}-t
)

Since
T-t_{0}+1\gg a_{T}\log^{2}T
in this case, then the RHS above is converging to zero and we can use another first order Taylor approximation to write:

\displaystyle\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1-\frac{4(T-t_{0}+1)|b_{0}|C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T}{\underline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{4(T-t_{0}+1)|b_{0}|C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T}{\underline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)}+\mathcal{O}\left(a_{T}\log^{2}T\right)

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{4\overline{s}C_{1}|s_{0}^{2}-1|}{\underline{s}^{2}}\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T+\mathcal{O}\left(a_{T}\log^{2}T\right).

(
\min\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s_{0}^{2}\}\leq(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}
)

So for some
C_{2}>8\overline{s}C_{1}/\underline{s}^{2}
, we can write:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-C_{2}|s_{0}^{2}-1|\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T+\frac{(t_{0}-t)}{2}\log s_{0}^{2}+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{(t_{0}-t)(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{T-t+1}\right]+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T)

\displaystyle=h_{T}(t,t_{0})

We now show that for any
t_{0}
,
h_{T}
is decreasing in
t
. Analyzing the first derivative of
h_{T}
we have:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial h_{T}}{\partial t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left[C_{2}|s_{0}^{2}-1|\sqrt{\frac{a_{T}\log^{2}T}{t_{0}-t}}-\log\left[1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)(T-t_{0}+1)}{T-t+1}\right]+\frac{\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}}{1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)(T-t_{0}+1)}{T-t+1}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left[C_{2}|s_{0}^{2}-1|\sqrt{\frac{a_{T}\log^{2}}{(T-t_{0}+1)x}}-\log\left[1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{1+x}\right]+\frac{\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{1+x}}{1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{1+x}}\right]

(
x=\frac{t_{0}-t}{T-t_{0}+1}
)

Next, note that the term:

\displaystyle g_{1}(x):=C_{2}|s_{0}^{2}-1|\sqrt{\frac{a_{T}\log^{2}}{(T-t_{0}+1)x}}

is convex and decreasing as a function of
x
. On the other hand, if we define:

\displaystyle g_{2}(x)

\displaystyle:=-\log\left[1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{1+x}\right]+\frac{\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{1+x}}{1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{1+x}}

then we have:

\displaystyle\frac{dg_{2}}{dx}=\frac{\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}{(1+x)^{3}}}{\left(1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{1+x}\right)^{2}}>0

so this term is increasing as a function of
x
. Therefore:

\displaystyle g_{1}(x)+g_{2}(x)

\displaystyle\leq g_{1}(x)\bigg|_{x=\inf\>\{x\in[0,1]\>:\>t_{0}-t\leq T-t_{0}+1,\;t\in\mathcal{T}_{a_{T}}\}}+g_{2}(x)\bigg|_{x=\sup\>\{x\in[0,1]\>:\>t_{0}-t\leq T-t_{0}+1,\;t\in\mathcal{T}_{a_{T}}\}}

\displaystyle\leq g_{1}\left(\frac{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}\right)+g_{2}\left(1\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{2}(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}{\sqrt{C_{1,a}}}-\log\left[1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}\right]+\frac{\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}}{1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}}.

Taking the derivative of the second and third term with respect to
s_{0}
gives:

\displaystyle-\frac{s_{0}(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2\left(1+\frac{s_{0}^{2}-1}{2}\right)^{2}}

(A.53)

Suppose that there is some
\overline{\nu}>1
so that
s_{0}^{2}>\overline{\nu}^{2}
for all
T
, then (
A.53
) is negative, implying:

\displaystyle\frac{C_{2}(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}{\sqrt{C_{1,a}}}-\log\left[1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}\right]+\frac{\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}}{1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{2}(\overline{s}^{2}-1)^{2}}{\sqrt{C_{1,a}}}-\log\left[1+\frac{(\overline{\nu}^{2}-1)}{2}\right]+\frac{\frac{(\overline{\nu}^{2}-1)}{2}}{1+\frac{(\overline{\nu}^{2}-1)}{2}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{C_{2}(\overline{s}^{2}-1)^{2}}{\sqrt{C_{1,a}}}-\nu^{*}.

The inequality
\log(1+x)>x/(x+1)
for
x>-1
and the fact that
\overline{\nu}^{2}>1
imply that
\nu^{*}>0
, so if we pick
C_{1,a}>(C_{1}(\overline{s}^{2}-1)^{2}/\nu^{*})^{2}
, then we will have
\partial h_{T}/\partial t<0
. On the other hand, if there is some
\underline{\nu}<1
so that
s_{0}^{2}<\overline{\nu}^{2}
for all
T
, then (
A.53
) is positive, implying:

\displaystyle\frac{C_{2}(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}{\sqrt{C_{1,a}}}-\log\left[1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}\right]+\frac{\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}}{1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{2}(\overline{s}^{2}-1)^{2}}{\sqrt{C_{1,a}}}-\log\left[1+\frac{(\underline{\nu}^{2}-1)}{2}\right]+\frac{\frac{(\underline{\nu}^{2}-1)}{2}}{1+\frac{(\underline{\nu}^{2}-1)}{2}}

and we can use the same argument to pick
C_{1,a}
large enough so that
\partial h_{T}/\partial t<0
. On the other hand, if
\lim_{T\to\infty}s_{0}^{2}=1
, we can again use the inequality
\log(1+x)>x/(1+x)
for
x>-1
to write:

\displaystyle-\log\left[1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}\right]+\frac{\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}}{1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}}\leq 0

and at the same time, a first order Taylor approximation of
\log(1+x)
gives:

\displaystyle-\log\left[1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}\right]+\frac{\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}}{1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}+\mathcal{O}\left((s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}\right)-\frac{\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}}{1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left((s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}\right)+\frac{\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}{4}}{1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left((s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}\right)

so this term is negative and converges to zero at rate
\mathcal{O}((s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2})
. Therefore, there is some
B_{1}>0
such that:

\displaystyle\lim_{T\to\infty}\frac{\frac{C_{2}(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}{\sqrt{C_{1,a}}}}{\log\left[1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}\right]-\frac{\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}}{1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}}}=\frac{B_{1}}{\sqrt{C_{1,a}}}.

So if we pick
C_{1,a}>B_{1}^{2}
, then for large
T
we will also have
\partial h_{T}/\partial t<0
in this case, confirming that
h_{T}(t,t_{0})
is decreasing in
t
, and thus:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>h_{T}(t,t_{0})\big|_{t=\sup\;\{t\in\mathcal{T}_{a_{T}}:t\leq t_{0}\}}

\displaystyle\geq h_{T}\left(t_{0}-\frac{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}},t_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=-C_{2}\sqrt{C_{1,a}}a_{T}\log^{2}T+\frac{C_{1,a}\log s_{0}^{2}}{2(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}a_{T}\log^{2}T

\displaystyle\quad\>+\left(\frac{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{2(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}\right)\left(1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\>+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T)

By assumption:

\displaystyle\lim_{T\to\infty}\frac{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}=0\implies\lim_{T\to\infty}\frac{(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}}=0.

Therefore, another first order Taylor approximation gives:

\displaystyle\left(\frac{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{2(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}\right)\left(1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}{C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1,a}(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{2(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}a_{T}\log^{2}T+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{C_{1,a}^{2}a_{T}\log^{4}T}{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)+C_{1,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1,a}(s_{0}^{-2}-1)}{2(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}a_{T}\log^{2}T+\mathcal{O}\left(a_{T}\log^{2}T\right)

(
(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}\gg a_{T}\log^{2}T
)

and thus for some
B_{1}>0
:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>\left(\frac{C_{1,a}f(s_{0}^{-2})}{2(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}-C_{2}\sqrt{C_{1,a}}-B_{1}\right)a_{T}\log^{2}T

Once again, if
s_{0}^{2}\geq\overline{\nu}^{2}>1
for all
T
, then
f(s_{0}^{-2})/(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}\geq f(\overline{\nu}^{-2})/(\overline{s}^{2}-1)^{2}>0
, while if
s_{0}^{2}\leq\underline{\nu}^{2}<1
, then
f(s_{0}^{-2})/(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}\geq f(\underline{\nu}^{-2})>0
, and since:

\displaystyle\lim_{s_{0}^{2}\to 1}\frac{f(s_{0}^{-2})}{(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}=\frac{1}{2}

then if
\lim_{T\to\infty}s_{0}^{2}=1
, then for large enough
T
,
f(s_{0}^{-2})/(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}>1/4
, so again we can pick
C_{1,a}
large enough that:

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1,a}f(s_{0}^{-2})}{2(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}-C_{2}\sqrt{C_{1,a}}-B_{1}>C_{\pi}

proving (
A.38
) holds for this case.

Case 2.b:
t_{0}-t\leq T-t_{0}+1
.

For some
C_{1}\geq 1
, on
\mathcal{E}
we have:

\displaystyle\max\left\{\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)\right|,\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)\right|,\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}z_{t^{\prime}}\right|,\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}(T-t+1)}\log T

(on
\cap_{i=1}^{4}\mathcal{E}_{i}\subset\mathcal{E}
)

and thus:

\displaystyle\left|\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\leq(1+s_{0}^{2}+2s_{0})\left[\frac{C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{\sqrt{T-t+1}}+\frac{C_{1}^{2}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{T-t+1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\overline{s}^{2}C_{1}^{2}}{C_{2,a}}.

(
s_{0}^{2}<\overline{s}^{2}
and
T-t+1\geq C_{2,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T
)

So for
C_{2,a}>8\overline{s}^{2}C_{1}^{2}
, we have
|\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})|>-1
and we can use the inequality
\log(1+x)>x/(x+1)
for
x>-1
to write:

\displaystyle\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})\right]

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\left[\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})-\frac{\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})^{2}}{1+\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)-\frac{\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}z_{t^{\prime}}\right)^{2}}{2(T-t+1)}+\frac{s_{0}^{2}}{2}\left[\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)-\frac{\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right)^{2}}{(T-t+1)}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\;-\frac{s_{0}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}z_{t^{\prime}}}{(T-t+1)}-\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\frac{\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})^{2}}{1+\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}

We also have:

\displaystyle\frac{\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}z_{t^{\prime}}\right)^{2}}{2(T-t+1)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}^{2}}{2}a_{T}\log^{2}T

\displaystyle\frac{s_{0}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{t_{0}-1}z_{t^{\prime}}}{(T-t+1)}

\displaystyle\leq\overline{s}C_{1}^{2}a_{T}\log^{2}T

\displaystyle\frac{s_{0}^{2}\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}z_{t^{\prime}}\right)^{2}}{2(T-t+1)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\overline{s}^{2}C_{1}^{2}}{2}a_{T}\log^{2}T.

For
C_{2,a}>16\overline{s}^{2}C_{1}^{2}
:

\displaystyle\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\frac{\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})^{2}}{1+\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}

\displaystyle\geq(T-t+1)(1+s_{0}^{2}+2s_{0})^{2}\left[\frac{C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{\sqrt{T-t+1}}+\frac{C_{1}^{2}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{T-t+1}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16\overline{s}^{2}C_{1}^{4}a_{T}^{2}\log^{4}T}{T-t+1}

(
s_{0}^{2}\leq\overline{s}^{2}
)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}^{2}a_{T}\log^{2}T.

(
T-t+1\geq C_{2,a}a_{T}\log^{2}T>16\overline{s}^{2}C_{1}^{2}a_{T}\log^{2}T
)

Returning to bound (
A.45
), we now have:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)-\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)}{2}\log s_{0}^{2}+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}}{1+\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\>+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{2(T-t+1)^{2}}-\frac{2(T-t_{0}+1)|b_{0}|C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T}{(T-t+1)^{2}}}{1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}+\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\>+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T).

(A.54)

Note that if
s_{0}^{2}=1
, then we can completely ignore the first line of (
A.54
), on the other hand, if
s_{0}^{2}\neq 1
, then by assumption
(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}(T-t_{0}+1)\gg a_{T}\log^{2}T
. However,
0<\underline{s}^{2}\leq s_{0}^{2}\leq\overline{s}^{2}<\infty
implies
(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, so we must have
T-t_{0}+1\gg a_{T}\log^{2}T
. We showed above that on
\mathcal{E}
:

\displaystyle\left|\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\leq(1+s_{0}^{2}+2s_{0})\left[\frac{C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{\sqrt{T-t+1}}+\frac{C_{1}^{2}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{T-t+1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 4\overline{s}^{2}\left[\frac{C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{\sqrt{T-t_{0}+1}}+\frac{C_{1}^{2}a_{T}\log^{2}T}{T-t_{0}+1}\right]

(
T-t+1\geq T-t_{0}+1
)

i.e.,
\left|\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})\right|\to 0
on
\mathcal{E}
when
s_{0}^{2}\neq 1
. Next, we can write:

\displaystyle\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}}{1+\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}\right]

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt\>+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1-\left(\frac{\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}{1+\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}\right)\left(\frac{\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}}{1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}}\right)\right].

We have just shown that:

\displaystyle\frac{\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}{1+\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{\sqrt{T-t+1}}\right)=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\sqrt{a_{T}}\log T}{\sqrt{T-t_{0}+1}}\right)

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}}{1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)(T-t_{0}+1)}{T-t+1}\right)=\mathcal{O}(1)

so another first order Taylor approximation of
\log(1+x)
around zero gives:

\displaystyle\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1-\left(\frac{\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}{1+\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}\right)\left(\frac{\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}}{1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=(s_{0}^{2}-1)\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{(T-t_{0}+1)a_{T}}\log T\right)+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T).

Similarly, for some
C_{1}>0
on
\mathcal{E}
we have:

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{0}}^{T}(z_{t^{\prime}}^{2}-1)\right|\leq C_{1}\sqrt{(T-t_{0}+1)a_{T}}\log T

(on
\mathcal{E}_{4}\subset\mathcal{E}
)

So for some
C_{2}\geq C_{1}/2
, we can now rewrite (
A.54
) on
\mathcal{E}
as:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-C_{2}|s_{0}^{2}-1|\sqrt{(T-t_{0}+1)a_{T}}\log T-\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)}{2}\log s_{0}^{2}+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\>+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{2(T-t+1)^{2}}-\frac{2(T-t_{0}+1)|b_{0}|C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T}{(T-t+1)^{2}}}{1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}+\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\>+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T).

(A.55)

Case 2.b.i:
\min\left\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s_{0}^{2}\right\}\geq(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}

Using the same argument from Case 2.a.i, it is straight forward to show that the term:

\displaystyle-\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)}{2}\log s_{0}^{2}+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}\right]

is decreasing in
t
and equal to zero when
t=t_{0}
, and that on
\mathcal{E}
:

\displaystyle\log\left[1+\frac{\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{2(T-t+1)^{2}}-\frac{2(T-t_{0}+1)|b_{0}|C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T}{(T-t+1)^{2}}}{1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}+\xi(T,t_{0},t,s_{0})}\right]\geq\log\left[1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}\right].

So in this case we can write:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-C_{2}|s_{0}^{2}-1|\sqrt{(T-t_{0}+1)a_{T}}\log T+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(t_{0}-t)b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}\right]+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T).

The second term is decreasing in
t
and
t_{0}-t\geq T-t_{0}+1
implies
t\leq 2t_{0}-T-1
in this case so for some
B_{1}>0
:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-C_{2}|s_{0}^{2}-1|\sqrt{(T-t_{0}+1)a_{T}}\log T+(T-t_{0}+1)\log\left[1+\frac{b_{0}^{2}}{16^{2}\overline{s}^{2}}\right]-B_{1}a_{T}\log^{2}T.

Next we have:

\displaystyle-C_{2}|s_{0}^{2}-1|\sqrt{(T-t_{0}+1)a_{T}}\log T+(T-t_{0}+1)\log\left[1+\frac{b_{0}^{2}}{16^{2}\overline{s}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq(T-t_{0}+1)\left(\log\left[1+\frac{b_{0}^{2}}{16^{2}\overline{s}^{2}}\right]-C_{2}(\overline{s}^{2}+1)\sqrt{\frac{a_{T}\log^{2}T}{T-t_{0}+1}}\right).

Suppose that
b_{0}^{2}\to\infty
as
T\to\infty
, then since
T-t_{0}+1\gtrsim a_{T}\log^{2}T
, there is some
B_{2}>0
so that:

\displaystyle\frac{a_{T}\log^{2}T}{B_{2}}\left(\log\left[1+\frac{b_{0}^{2}}{16\overline{s}^{2}}\right]-\frac{C_{2}(\overline{s}^{2}+1)}{\sqrt{B_{2}}}\right)

So (
A.38
) holds for
T
large enough that:

\displaystyle\log\left[1+\frac{b_{0}^{2}}{16^{2}\overline{s}^{2}}\right]>\frac{C_{2}(\overline{s}^{2}+1)}{\sqrt{B_{2}}}+B_{2}(B_{1}+C_{\pi}).

On the other hand, if
b_{0}^{2}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, then it must be the case that
T-t_{0}+1\gg a_{T}\log^{2}T
. If
b_{0}^{2}>\underline{b}^{2}>0
for all
T
, then for large
T
we have:

\displaystyle\log\left[1+\frac{b_{0}^{2}}{16^{2}\overline{s}^{2}}\right]-C_{2}(\overline{s}^{2}+1)\sqrt{\frac{a_{T}\log^{2}T}{T-t_{0}+1}}>\frac{1}{2}\log\left[1+\frac{\underline{b}^{2}}{16^{2}\overline{s}^{2}}\right]>0

and:

\displaystyle\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)}{2}\log\left[1+\frac{\underline{b}^{2}}{16^{2}\overline{s}^{2}}\right]-B_{2}a_{T}\log^{2}T\geq C_{\pi}a_{T}\log^{2}T.

Finally, if
\lim_{T\to\infty}b_{0}^{2}=0
, then a first order Taylor expansion of
\log(1+x)
gives:

\displaystyle\log\left[1+\frac{b_{0}^{2}}{16^{2}\overline{s}^{2}}\right]=\frac{b_{0}^{2}}{16^{2}\overline{s}^{2}}+\mathcal{O}(b_{0}^{4})\geq\frac{b_{0}^{2}}{512\overline{s}^{2}}

where the last inequality holds for large
T
, and thus:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-C_{2}(\overline{s}^{2}+1)\sqrt{(T-t_{0}+1)a_{T}}\log T+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)b_{0}^{2}}{512\overline{s}^{2}}-B_{1}a_{T}\log^{2}T.

But
(T-t_{0}+1)b_{0}^{2}\gg a_{T}\log^{2}T
by assumption, so the second term dominates for large
T
we again get that (
A.38
) holds for this case.

Case 2.b.ii:
\min\left\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s_{0}^{2}\right\}<(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}

Returning to bound (
A.55
), we can use the same argument from Case 2.a.ii to get:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-C_{2}|s_{0}^{2}-1|\sqrt{(T-t_{0}+1)a_{T}}\log T-\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)}{2}\log s_{0}^{2}+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\>+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1-\frac{4(T-t_{0}+1)|b_{0}|C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T}{\underline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}\right]+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T).

and:

\displaystyle\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1-\frac{4(T-t_{0}+1)|b_{0}|C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T}{\underline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{4(T-t_{0}+1)|b_{0}|C_{1}\sqrt{a_{T}(t_{0}-t)}\log T}{\underline{s}^{2}(T-t+1)}+\mathcal{O}\left(a_{T}\log^{2}T\right)

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{4\overline{s}C_{1}|s_{0}^{2}-1|}{\underline{s}^{2}}\sqrt{a_{T}(T-t_{0}+1)}\log T+\mathcal{O}\left(a_{T}\log^{2}T\right).

(
T-t+1\geq t_{0}-t\geq T-t_{0}+1
and
\min\{b_{0}^{2},b_{0}^{2}/s_{0}^{2}\}\leq(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}
)

So for
C_{3}>C_{2}+4\overline{s}C_{1}/\underline{s}^{2}
we have:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-C_{3}|s_{0}^{2}-1|\sqrt{(T-t_{0}+1)a_{T}}\log T-\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)}{2}\log s_{0}^{2}+\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\log\left[1+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{T-t+1}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\>+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T).

(A.56)

The bound (
A.56
) is decreasing as a function of
t
and in this case, since
t_{0}-t>T-t_{0}+1
, then
t<2t_{0}-T+1
. Therefore, we have:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-C_{3}|s_{0}^{2}-1|\sqrt{(T-t_{0}+1)a_{T}}\log T-\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)}{2}\log s_{0}^{2}+(T-t_{0}+1)\log\left[1+\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)}{2}\right]+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T)

\displaystyle=-C_{3}|s_{0}^{2}-1|\sqrt{(T-t_{0}+1)a_{T}}\log T+(T-t_{0}+1)\log\left[1+\frac{s_{0}+\frac{1}{s_{0}}-2}{2}\right]+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T)

If there is some
\underline{\nu}<1
so that
s_{0}\leq\underline{\nu}
for all
T
, then:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-C_{3}\sqrt{(T-t_{0}+1)a_{T}}\log T+(T-t_{0}+1)\log\left[1+\frac{\underline{\nu}+\underline{\nu}^{-1}-2}{2}\right]+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T)

and if there is some
\overline{\nu}>1
so that
s_{0}\geq\underline{\nu}
for all
T
, then:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-C_{3}|\overline{s}^{2}-1|\sqrt{(T-t_{0}+1)a_{T}}\log T+(T-t_{0}+1)\log\left[1+\frac{\overline{\nu}+\overline{\nu}^{-1}-2}{2}\right]+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T)

and since
\min\{\underline{\nu}+\underline{\nu}^{-1},\overline{\nu}+\overline{\nu}^{-1}\}-2>0
and
T-t_{0}+1\gg a_{T}\log^{2}T
, the second term dominates in both the bounds above and for large enough
T
we will have
\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}\geq C_{\pi}\log^{2}T
. On the other hand, if
\lim_{T\to\infty}s_{0}^{2}=1
, then we can use a first order Taylor approximation of
\log(1+x)
around zero to write:

\displaystyle\log\left[1+\frac{s_{0}+\frac{1}{s_{0}}-2}{2}\right]=\frac{s_{0}+\frac{1}{s_{0}}-2}{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(s_{0}+\frac{1}{s_{0}}-2\right)^{2}\right).

Since
\lim_{s_{0}\to 1}s_{0}+\frac{1}{s_{0}}-2=0
, then for large enough
T
, we have:

\displaystyle\log\left[1+\frac{s_{0}+\frac{1}{s_{0}}-2}{2}\right]=\frac{s_{0}+\frac{1}{s_{0}}-2}{4}

and thus:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-C_{3}|s_{0}^{2}-1|\sqrt{(T-t_{0}+1)a_{T}}\log T+(T-t_{0}+1)\left(\frac{s_{0}+\frac{1}{s_{0}}-2}{4}\right)+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T)

Next, since:

\displaystyle\lim_{s_{0}\to 1}\frac{s_{0}+\frac{1}{s_{0}}-2}{(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}=\frac{1}{4}

then for large enough
T
we have:

\displaystyle s_{0}+\frac{1}{s_{0}}-2>\frac{(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}{8}

and thus:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\alpha_{t_{0}}}{\alpha_{t}}

\displaystyle>-C_{3}\sqrt{(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}(T-t_{0}+1)a_{T}}\log T+\frac{(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}}{8}+\mathcal{O}(a_{T}\log^{2}T)

By assumption,
(T-t_{0}+1)(s_{0}^{2}-1)^{2}\gg a_{T}\log^{2}T
, so again the second term dominates the others and (
A.38
) holds on
\mathcal{E}
for large
T
in this case.

∎

A.9
Proof of Corollary
1

Proof.

In the proofs of Theorems
1
,
2
, and
3
, we show that given the true change
t_{0}\in[T]
and the posterior change-point probabilities
\overline{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{1:T}
, we can pick
C_{\beta}>0
large enough when defining the localization rate
\epsilon_{T}
so that:

\displaystyle\lim_{T\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcap_{t\in[T]\;:\;|t_{0}-t|>\epsilon_{T}}\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{t_{0}}}{\overline{\pi}_{t}}>2\log T\right)=1.

Thus, with high probability we will have:

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in[T]\;:\;|t_{0}-t|>\epsilon_{T}}\overline{\pi}_{t}<\sum_{t\in[T]\;:\;|t_{0}-t|>\epsilon_{T}}\frac{\overline{\pi}_{t_{0}}}{T^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{T}.

So all the mass of
\overline{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{1:T}
is concentrating in
\{t\in[T]:|t_{0}-t|\leq\epsilon_{T}\}
, meaning that for any
\alpha\in(0,1)
, (
2.6
) will only pick coordinates within
\epsilon_{T}
of
t_{0}
with probability converging to one as as
T\to\infty
. Since
|\{t\in[T]:|t_{0}-t|\leq\epsilon_{T}\}|\leq 2\epsilon_{T}
this proves the corollary.

Note that in the argument above we ignored the end-point restrictions we built into the set
\mathcal{T}_{\beta}
in the proofs of Theorems
1
,
2
, and
3
, but if we change the
2\epsilon_{t}
bound to
3\epsilon_{T}
, then result still holds since there are at most
\mathcal{O}(\log(\log T))
end points of
[T]
that could be added to
\mathcal{CS}(\alpha,\overline{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{1:T})
. For the detection rule, this omission has no effect since
\log^{2+\delta}T
still dominates
3\epsilon_{T}
. Proving that the result holds for Theorem
4
follows an identical argument with the
\log T
term above replaced by
\log^{2}T
.
∎

Appendix B
Details of Variational Algorithms

B.1
Proof of Proposition
1

Define the
N
variable and
N
parameters blocks:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j}

\displaystyle:=\{b_{j},s_{j},\tau_{j}\},

\displaystyle\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{j}

\displaystyle:=\{\overline{b}_{jt},\overline{\omega}_{jt},\overline{u}_{jt},\overline{v}_{jt},\overline{\pi}_{jt}\}_{t=1}^{T},

\displaystyle 1\leq j\leq J,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\ell}

\displaystyle:=\{b_{\ell},\tau_{\ell}\},

\displaystyle\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell}

\displaystyle:=\{\overline{b}_{\ell t},\overline{\omega}_{\ell t},\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}\}_{t=1}^{T},

\displaystyle J<\ell\leq J+L,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{k}

\displaystyle:=\{s_{k},\tau_{k}\},

\displaystyle\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}

\displaystyle:=\{\overline{u}_{kt},\overline{v}_{kt},\overline{\pi}_{kt}\}_{t=1}^{T},

\displaystyle J+L<k\leq N,

and let
\boldsymbol{\Theta}:=\{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:L},{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:K},{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:J}\}
. We see that Algorithm
1
begins by initializing some
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N}
that parametrizes a
q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\text{MF}}
, then Proposition
2
below shows that the update step for
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}
in Algorithm
1
is equivalent to solving
\max_{q_{i}}\text{ELBO}(q)
while holding all the other component distributions of
q
fixed. Therefore, Algorithm
1
is a coordinate ascent procedure for maximizing
\text{ELBO}(q)
. Proposition
2
also shows that the
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}
that parameterizes the solution to
\text{arg}\max_{q_{i}}\text{ELBO}\left(q\right)
is the unique maximizer of some continuously differentiable objective function
F
when
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N}\setminus\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}
is held fixed, so by Proposition 2.7.1 of
Bertsekas 1997
, the sequence of parameters
\{\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N}^{(n)}\}_{n\geq 1}
returned by Algorithm
1
is converging to some limit point. Because the set of distributions
\{q^{(n)}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
in (
3.9
)-(
3.11
) returned by the
n
th
iteration of Algorithm
1
is fully characterized by
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(n)}_{1:N}
, the sequence of distributions
\{q^{(n)}\}_{n\geq 1}
, where
q^{(n)}:=\prod_{i=1}^{N}q_{i}^{(n)}
, is converging to a stationary point of
\text{ELBO}\left(q\right)
. Therefore, Proposition
1
directly follows from Proposition
2
.

Proposition 2
.

Given the MICH model (
3.1
)-(
3.7
), assume that
\omega_{0},u_{0},v_{0},\pi_{t}>0
. Let
\{q_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
be an arbitrary collection of distributions such that
q:=\prod_{i=1}^{N}q_{i}
and
\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}],\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}],\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}\mu^{2}_{jt}]<\infty
for any
1\leq j\leq J
,
\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell}}[\mu_{\ell t}],\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell}}[\mu^{2}_{\ell t}]<\infty
for any
J<\ell\leq J+L
, and
\mathbb{E}_{q_{k}}[\lambda_{kt}]<\infty
for any
J+L<k\leq N
. Define
\text{ELBO}\left(q\right)
as in (
3.12
), and the mean residual
\tilde{r}_{t}
, expected precision
\overline{\lambda}_{t}
and variance correction term
\delta_{t}
as in (
B.4
)-(
B.6
). Then:

(i)

For
J<\ell\leq J+L
, define the partial mean residual
\tilde{r}_{-\ell t}:=\tilde{r}_{t}+\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell}}[\mu_{\ell t}]
. Then
\text{{arg}}\max_{q_{\ell}}\text{ELBO}\left(q\right)
is equivalent to the mean-scp posterior in (
2.3
) and (
2.9
) with parameters:

\displaystyle\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell}={\texttt{mean-scp}}(\tilde{\mathbf{r}}_{-\ell,1:T}\>;\>\overline{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{1:T},\omega_{0},\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T})

(ii)

For
J+L<k\leq N
, define the partial scale residual
\overline{\lambda}_{-kt}:=\mathbb{E}_{q_{k}}[\lambda_{kt}]^{-1}\overline{\lambda}_{t}
and the variance corrected priors
\tilde{v}_{kt}
and
\tilde{\pi}_{kt}
as in (
B.14
) and (
B.15
). Then
\text{{arg}}\max_{q_{k}}\text{ELBO}\left(q\right)
is equivalent to the var-scp posterior in (
2.3
) and (
2.14
) with parameters:

\displaystyle\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}={\texttt{var-scp}}(\tilde{\mathbf{r}}_{1:T}\>;\>\overline{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{-k,1:T},u_{0},\tilde{\mathbf{v}}_{k,1:T},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{k,1:T})

(B.1)

(iii)

For
1\leq j\leq J
, define the partial mean residual
\tilde{r}_{-jt}:=\tilde{r}_{t}+\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}]^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}]
, the partial scale residual
\overline{\lambda}_{-jt}:=\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}]^{-1}\overline{\lambda}_{t}
, the partial correction term
\delta_{-jt}
as per (
B.20
), and the variance corrected priors
\tilde{v}_{jt}
and
\tilde{\pi}_{jt}
as in (
B.21
) and (
B.22
). Then
\text{{arg}}\max_{q_{j}}\text{ELBO}\left(q\right)
is equivalent to the meanvar-scp posterior in (
2.3
) and (
2.18
) with parameters:

\displaystyle\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{j}:={\texttt{meanvar-scp}}(\tilde{\mathbf{r}}_{-j,1:T}\>;\>\overline{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{-j,1:T},\omega_{0},u_{0},\tilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j,1:T},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{j,1:T})

Furthermore, when
q_{\ell}
,
q_{k}
, and
q_{j}
, have the parametric forms specified in (i)-(iii), then
\text{ELBO}\left(q\right)
is equivalent to a continuously differentiable function
F(\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N})
, and: i)
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell}
is the unique maximizer of
F
holding
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N}\setminus\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell}
fixed, ii)
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}
is the unique maximizer of
F
holding
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N}\setminus\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}
fixed, and iii)
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{j}
is the unique maximizer of
F
holding
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N}\setminus\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{j}
fixed, i.

Proof.

Consider the following generic model:

\displaystyle\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\boldsymbol{\Theta}

\displaystyle\;\sim f(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\boldsymbol{\Theta};\eta),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Theta}

\displaystyle\;=\{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\theta}_{M}\},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{ind.}}{\sim}g_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}),\;\;\forall\;i\in\{1,\ldots,M\},

where
\eta
is nuisance parameter and
g_{i}
is the prior over the block
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}
. Define the mean-field family of distributions over
\boldsymbol{\Theta}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\text{MF}}

\displaystyle:=\left\{q\>:\>q(\boldsymbol{\Theta})=\prod_{i=1}^{M}q_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i})\right\}.

The task of variational Bayesian inference is to find the
q^{*}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\text{MF}}
that maximizes
\text{ELBO}(q;\eta)
as defined in (
3.12
). If
q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\text{MF}}
and we define:

\displaystyle q_{-i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{-i}):=\prod_{1\leq i^{\prime}\leq M,\>i^{\prime}\neq i}q_{i^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i^{\prime}}),

then we can write:

\displaystyle\text{ELBO}(q;\eta)

\displaystyle=\int q(\boldsymbol{\Theta})\log\frac{f(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\boldsymbol{\Theta};\eta)g(\boldsymbol{\Theta})}{q(\boldsymbol{\Theta})}\;d\boldsymbol{\Theta}

\displaystyle=\int\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{M}q_{i^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i^{\prime}})\log\frac{f(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\boldsymbol{\Theta};\eta)\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{M}g_{i^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i^{\prime}})}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{M}q_{i^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i^{\prime}})}\;d\boldsymbol{\Theta}

\displaystyle=\int q_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i})\log\frac{\exp\left\{\mathbb{E}_{q_{-i}}[\log f(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\boldsymbol{\Theta};\eta)]\right\}g_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i})}{q_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i})}\;d\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i^{\prime}\leq M\\
i^{\prime}\neq i\end{subarray}}\text{KL}(q_{i^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i^{\prime}})\>\lVert\>g_{i^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i^{\prime}})).

Note that only the first term in the last line above depends on
q_{i}
, so if we treat
q_{-i}
as fixed, then:

\displaystyle\max_{q_{i}}\;\text{ELBO}(q\>;\>\eta)

(B.2)

is equivalent to solving:

\displaystyle\max_{q_{i}}\;\int q_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i})\log\frac{\exp\left\{\mathbb{E}_{q_{-i}}[\log f(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\boldsymbol{\Theta};\eta)]\right\}g_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i})}{q_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i})}\;d\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}

If we define the normalizing constant:

\displaystyle C_{i}:=\int\exp\left\{\mathbb{E}_{q_{-i}}[\log f(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\boldsymbol{\Theta};\eta)]\right\}\;d\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}

then we can define a new distribution over
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}
:

\displaystyle\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}):=C^{-1}_{i}\exp\left\{\mathbb{E}_{q_{-i}}[\log f(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\boldsymbol{\Theta};\eta)]\right\}g_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i})

Because
C_{i}
does not depend on our choice of
q_{i}
, we have

\displaystyle\underset{q_{i}}{\text{arg max}}\;\text{ELBO}(q\>;\>\eta)

\displaystyle=\underset{q_{i}}{\text{arg max}}\;\int q_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i})\log\frac{\exp\left\{\mathbb{E}_{q_{-i}}[\log f(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\boldsymbol{\Theta};\eta)]\right\}g_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i})}{q_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i})}\;d\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}

\displaystyle=\underset{q_{i}}{\text{arg max}}\;\int q_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i})\log\frac{C^{-1}_{i}\exp\left\{\mathbb{E}_{q_{-i}}[\log f(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\boldsymbol{\Theta};\eta)]\right\}g_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i})}{q_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i})}\;d\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}

\displaystyle=\underset{q_{i}}{\text{arg max}}\;\text{KL}(q_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i})\>\lVert\>\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i})).

If we have a closed-form expression for
\tilde{q}_{i}
, then the last line above shows that the solution to (
B.2
) is to set
q_{i}\equiv\tilde{q}_{i}
, i.e. we will have:

\displaystyle q_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i})\propto\exp\left\{\mathbb{E}_{q_{-i}}[\log f(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\boldsymbol{\Theta};\eta)]\right\}g_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}).

(B.3)

Therefore, to prove Proposition
2
, we must show that (
B.3
) implies that each
q_{\ell}
,
q_{k}
, and
q_{j}
have the functional forms specified in (i)-(iii) of Proposition
2
. To show this, we begin by defining the key terms of the backfitting procedure:

\displaystyle\tilde{r}_{t}

\displaystyle:=y_{t}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell}}[\mu_{\ell t}]-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}]}{\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}]},

(B.4)

\displaystyle\overline{\lambda}_{t}

\displaystyle:=\prod_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{q_{k}}[\lambda_{kt}]\prod_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}],

(B.5)

\displaystyle\delta_{t}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\text{Var}_{q_{\ell}}\left(\mu_{\ell t}\right)+\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}\mu^{2}_{jt}]}{\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}]}-\frac{\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}]^{2}}\right).

(B.6)

Note the slight abuse of notation above, as we are now using the symbols
\ell
,
k
, and
j
both to index the model components as well as to indicate which type of change-point the component represents (mean, variance, or mean and variance). We also introduce notation to help simplify the derivation of the distributions
q_{\ell}
,
q_{j}
, and
q_{j}
. Let
i\in\{\ell,k,j\}
be a generic block index, then we define:

\displaystyle\overline{\pi}_{it}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{q_{i}}\left[\mathbbm{1}{\left\{\tau_{i}=t\right\}}\right],

\displaystyle\overline{b}_{it}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{q_{i}}[b_{i}\>|\>\tau_{i}=t],

\displaystyle\overline{\mu}_{it}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{q_{i}}[\mu_{it}]=\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\mathbbm{1}{\{t\geq t^{\prime}\}}\mathbb{E}_{q_{i}}[b_{i}\>|\>\tau_{i}=t^{\prime}]q_{i}(\tau_{i}=t^{\prime})=\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\overline{b}_{it^{\prime}}\overline{\pi}_{it^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\overline{\mu}_{t}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{q}[\mu_{t}]=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\overline{\mu}_{\ell t}+\sum_{j=1}^{J}\overline{\mu}_{jt},\hskip 9.24994pt\overline{\mu}_{-jt}:=\overline{\mu}_{t}-\overline{\mu}_{jt},\hskip 9.24994pt\overline{\mu}_{-\ell t}:=\overline{\mu}_{t}-\overline{\mu}_{\ell t},

\displaystyle\overline{\sigma}^{2}_{it}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{q_{i}}\left[\left(b_{i}-\overline{b}_{it}\right)^{2}\>|\>\tau_{i}=t\right],

\displaystyle\overline{\mu^{2}_{it}}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{q_{i}}[\mu^{2}_{it}]=\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\mathbbm{1}{\{t\geq t^{\prime}\}}\mathbb{E}_{q_{i}}[b^{2}_{i}\>|\>\tau_{i}=t^{\prime}]q_{i}(\tau_{i}=t^{\prime})=\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}(\overline{b}^{2}_{it^{\prime}}+\overline{\sigma}^{2}_{it^{\prime}})\overline{\pi}_{it^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\overline{s}_{it}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{q_{i}}[s_{i}\>|\>\tau_{i}=t],

\displaystyle\overline{\lambda}_{it}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{q_{i}}[\lambda_{it}]=\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{q_{i}}[s_{i}\>|\>\tau_{i}=t^{\prime}]^{\mathbbm{1}\{t\geq t^{\prime}\}}q_{i}(\tau_{i}=t^{\prime})=\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\overline{s}_{it^{\prime}}\overline{\pi}_{it^{\prime}}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t+1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{it^{\prime}}.

Let
p\left(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\boldsymbol{\Theta}\right)
denote the marginal likelihood (
2.1
) under the MICH model. Let
p_{\ell}
,
p_{k}
, and
p_{j}
be the respective priors for the parameter blocks
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\ell}
,
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{k}
, and
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j}
. Note that we can write:

\displaystyle\text{ELBO}(q\>)

\displaystyle=\int q(\boldsymbol{\Theta})\log\frac{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T},\boldsymbol{\Theta})}{q(\boldsymbol{\Theta})}\;d\boldsymbol{\Theta}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{q}\left[\log p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T},\boldsymbol{\Theta})\right]-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\text{KL}(q_{\ell}\>\lVert\>p_{\ell})-\sum_{k=1}^{K}\text{KL}(q_{k}\>\lVert\>p_{k})-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\text{KL}(q_{j}\>\lVert\>p_{j}).

Note that if
X\sim\text{Gamma}(\alpha,\beta)
, then
\mathbb{E}[\log X]=\psi(\alpha)-\log\beta
, where
\psi
is the digamma function. So if
q_{\ell}
,
q_{k}
, and
q_{j}
do in fact take the forms claimed in (i)-(iii) of Proposition
2
, then we have::

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{q}\left[\log p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T},\boldsymbol{\Theta})\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{q}[\lambda_{t}(y_{t}-\mu_{t})^{2}]+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\log\lambda_{jt}]+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{q_{k}}[\log\lambda_{kt}]\right]+C

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t}(\tilde{r}_{t}^{2}+\delta_{t})+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{J}(T-t+1)\overline{\pi}_{jt}[\psi(\overline{u}_{jt})-\log\overline{v}_{jt}]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{k=1}^{K}(T-t+1)\overline{\pi}_{kt}[\psi(\overline{u}_{kt})-\log\overline{v}_{kt}]+C,

where
C
is a constant that does not depend on
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N}
. Noting that
\tilde{r}_{t}
and
\delta_{t}
can be expressed as continuously differentiable functions of known constants
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N}
, then we see that
\mathbb{E}_{q}\left[\log p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T},\boldsymbol{\Theta})\right]
is a continuously differentiable function of
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N}
. Next, when
q_{\ell}
takes the form in (i):

\displaystyle\text{KL}(q_{\ell}\>\lVert\>p_{\ell})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbbm{1}\{\tau_{\ell}=t\}\left(\log\frac{q(b_{\ell}|\tau_{\ell}=t)}{p(b_{\ell}|\tau_{\ell}=t)}+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}}{\pi_{\ell t}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbbm{1}\{\tau_{\ell}=t\}\left(\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}}{\omega_{\ell}}-\frac{\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}(b_{\ell}-\overline{b}_{\ell t})^{2}-\omega_{\ell}b_{\ell}^{2}}{2}+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}}{\pi_{\ell t}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}\left[\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}}{\omega_{\ell}}-\frac{\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell}}\left[(b_{\ell}-\overline{b}_{\ell t})^{2}|\tau_{\ell}=t\right]-\omega_{\ell}\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell}}\left[b_{\ell}^{2}|\tau_{\ell}=t\right]}{2}+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}}{\pi_{\ell t}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}\left[\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}}{\omega_{\ell}}-\frac{1-\omega_{\ell}(\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}^{-1}+\overline{b}^{2}_{\ell t})}{2}+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}}{\pi_{\ell t}}\right].

So
\text{KL}(q_{\ell}\>\lVert\>p_{\ell})
is a continuously differentiable function of
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell}
. Next, when
q_{k}
takes the form in (ii):

\displaystyle\text{KL}(q_{k}\>\lVert\>p_{k})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{q_{k}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbbm{1}\{\tau_{k}=t\}\left(\log\frac{q(s_{k}|\tau_{k}=t)}{p(s_{k}|\tau_{k}=t)}+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{kt}}{\pi_{kt}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{q_{k}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbbm{1}\{\tau_{k}=t\}\left(\overline{u}_{kt}\log\overline{v}_{kt}-u_{k}\log v_{k}-\log\frac{\Gamma(\overline{u}_{kt})}{\Gamma(u_{k})}+(\overline{u}_{kt}-u_{k})\log s_{k}-(\overline{v}_{kt}-v_{k})s_{k}+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{kt}}{\pi_{kt}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{kt}\left(\overline{u}_{kt}\log\overline{v}_{kt}-u_{k}\log v_{k}-\log\frac{\Gamma(\overline{u}_{kt})}{\Gamma(u_{k})}+(\overline{u}_{kt}-u_{k})\mathbb{E}_{q_{k}}[\log s_{k}|\tau_{k}=t]-(\overline{v}_{kt}-v_{k})\mathbb{E}_{q_{k}}[s_{k}|\tau_{k}=t]+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{kt}}{\pi_{kt}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{kt}\left[\overline{u}_{kt}\log\overline{v}_{kt}-u_{k}\log v_{k}-\log\frac{\Gamma(\overline{u}_{kt})}{\Gamma(u_{k})}+(\overline{u}_{kt}-u_{k})[\psi(\overline{u}_{kt})-\log\overline{v}_{kt}]-\frac{(\overline{v}_{kt}-v_{k})\overline{u}_{kt}}{\overline{v}_{kt}}+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{kt}}{\pi_{kt}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{kt}\left[u_{k}\log\frac{\overline{v}_{kt}}{v_{k}}-\log\frac{\Gamma(\overline{u}_{kt})}{\Gamma(u_{k})}+(\overline{u}_{kt}-u_{k})\psi(\overline{u}_{kt})-\frac{(\overline{v}_{kt}-v_{k})\overline{u}_{kt}}{\overline{v}_{kt}}+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{kt}}{\pi_{kt}}\right].

So
\text{KL}(q_{k}\>\lVert\>p_{k})
is a continuously differentiable function of
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}
. Next, when
q_{j}
takes the form in (iii):

\displaystyle\text{KL}(q_{j}\>\lVert\>p_{j})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbbm{1}\{\tau_{j}=t\}\left(\log\frac{q(b_{j}|s_{j},\tau_{j}=t)}{p(b_{j}|s_{,}j\tau_{j}=t)}+\log\frac{q(s_{j}|\tau_{j}=t)}{p(s_{j}|,\tau_{j}=t)}+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{jt}}{\pi_{jt}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbbm{1}\{\tau_{j}=t\}\left(\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\overline{\omega}_{jt}}{\omega_{j}}-\frac{\overline{\omega}_{jt}s_{j}(b_{j}-\overline{b}_{jt})^{2}-\omega_{j}s_{j}b_{j}^{2}}{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.19447pt+\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbbm{1}\{\tau_{j}=t\}\left(\overline{u}_{jt}\log\overline{v}_{jt}-u_{j}\log v_{j}-\log\frac{\Gamma(\overline{u}_{jt})}{\Gamma(u_{j})}+(\overline{u}_{jt}-u_{j})\log s_{j}-(\overline{v}_{jt}-v_{j})s_{j}+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{jt}}{\pi_{jt}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{jt}\left[\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\overline{\omega}_{jt}}{\omega_{j}}-\frac{\overline{\omega}_{jt}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[s_{j}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[(b_{j}-\overline{b}_{jt})^{2}|s_{j},\tau_{j}=t]|\tau_{j}=t]-\omega_{j}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[s_{j}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[b_{j}^{2}|s_{j},\tau_{j}=t]|\tau_{j}=t]}{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.19447pt+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{jt}\left[\overline{u}_{jt}\log\overline{v}_{jt}-u_{j}\log v_{j}-\log\frac{\Gamma(\overline{u}_{jt})}{\Gamma(u_{j})}+(\overline{u}_{jt}-u_{j})\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\log s_{j}|\tau_{j}=t]-(\overline{v}_{jt}-v_{j})\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[s_{j}|\tau_{j}=t]+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{jt}}{\pi_{jt}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{jt}\left[\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\overline{\omega}_{jt}}{\omega_{j}}-\frac{1-\omega_{j}(\overline{\omega}_{jt}^{-1}+\overline{b}_{jt}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[s_{j}|\tau_{j}=t])}{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.19447pt+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{jt}\left[u_{j}\log\frac{\overline{v}_{jt}}{v_{j}}-\log\frac{\Gamma(\overline{u}_{jt})}{\Gamma(u_{j})}+(\overline{u}_{jt}-u_{j})\psi(\overline{u}_{jt})-\frac{(\overline{v}_{jt}-v_{j})\overline{u}_{jt}}{\overline{v}_{jt}}+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{jt}}{\pi_{jt}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{jt}\left[\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\overline{\omega}_{jt}}{\omega_{j}}-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\omega_{j}}{2\overline{\omega}_{jt}}+\frac{\omega_{j}\overline{u}_{jt}\overline{b}_{jt}^{2}}{2\overline{v}_{jt}}+u_{j}\log\frac{\overline{v}_{jt}}{v_{j}}-\log\frac{\Gamma(\overline{u}_{jt})}{\Gamma(u_{j})}+(\overline{u}_{jt}-u_{j})\psi(\overline{u}_{jt})-\frac{(\overline{v}_{jt}-v_{j})\overline{u}_{jt}}{\overline{v}_{jt}}+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{jt}}{\pi_{jt}}\right]

So
\text{KL}(q_{j}\>\lVert\>p_{j})
is a continuously differentiable function of
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{j}
. Therefore,
\text{ELBO}(q\>)
can be expressed as a continuously differentiable function of
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N}
, i.e. we can define:

\displaystyle F(\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N}):=\text{ELBO}(q)

(B.7)

We now show that the marginal distributions
q_{\ell}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\ell})
,
q_{k}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{k})
, and
q_{j}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j})
that are implied by the expression in (
B.3
) are precisely the same distributions we claim solve the appropriate maximization problems in (i)-(iii) of Proposition
2
. Throughout the proof we take a general approach and allow the hyperparameters
\omega_{i},u_{i},v_{i}
, and
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{i,1:T}
to vary by component
i
.

Case (i):

For fixed a
\ell^{\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,L\}
, we treat the distributions
\{q_{\ell}\}_{\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}}
,
\{q_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K}
, and
\{q_{j}\}_{j=1}^{J}
as fixed and known. Then by (
B.3
), the solution to:

\displaystyle\max_{q_{\ell^{\prime}}}\text{ELBO}\left(q\right),

(B.8)

is to pick
q_{\ell^{\prime}}
so that:

\displaystyle\log q_{\ell^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\ell^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\underset{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\ell^{\prime}}}{\propto}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-\ell^{\prime}}}\left[\log p\left(\mathbf{y}\>|\>\boldsymbol{\Theta}\right)\right]+\log p_{\ell^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\ell^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\underset{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\ell^{\prime}}}{\propto}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-\ell^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{t}\left(y_{t}-\mu_{t}\right)^{2}\right]+\log p_{\ell^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\ell^{\prime}}).

Define the partial residual:

\displaystyle r_{-\ell^{\prime}t}

\displaystyle:=y_{t}-\mu_{t}+\mu_{\ell^{\prime}t}.

If we fix
\tau_{\ell^{\prime}}=t^{\prime}
, then we have:

\displaystyle\log q_{\ell^{\prime}}(b_{\ell^{\prime}},\tau_{\ell^{\prime}}=t^{\prime})

\displaystyle\underset{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\ell^{\prime}}}{\propto}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-\ell^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{t}\left(b_{\ell^{\prime}}^{2}-2r_{-\ell^{\prime}t}b_{\ell^{\prime}}\right)\right]-\frac{b_{\ell^{\prime}}^{2}\omega_{\ell^{\prime}}}{2}+\log\pi_{\ell^{\prime}t}

\displaystyle\;=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\left(\mathbb{E}_{q_{-\ell^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{t}\right]b_{\ell^{\prime}}^{2}-2\mathbb{E}_{q_{-\ell^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{t}r_{-\ell^{\prime}t}\right]b_{\ell^{\prime}}\right)-\frac{b_{\ell^{\prime}}^{2}\omega_{\ell^{\prime}}}{2}+\log\pi_{\ell^{\prime}t}

\displaystyle\;=-\frac{1}{2}\left[b_{\ell^{\prime}}^{2}\left(\omega_{\ell^{\prime}}+\sum_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t}\right)-2b_{\ell^{\prime}}\sum_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-\ell^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{t}r_{-\ell^{\prime}t}\right]\right]+\log\pi_{\ell^{\prime}t}.

We can now calculate the posterior parameters for
b_{\ell^{\prime}}
given
\tau_{\ell^{\prime}}=t^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle\overline{\omega}_{\ell^{\prime}t^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\omega_{\ell^{\prime}}+\sum_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t}

\displaystyle\overline{b}_{\ell^{\prime}t^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\overline{\omega}_{\ell^{\prime}t^{\prime}}^{-1}\sum_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-\ell^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{t}r_{-\ell^{\prime}t}\right].

We now have:

\displaystyle\log q_{\ell^{\prime}}(b_{\ell^{\prime}},\tau_{\ell^{\prime}}=t^{\prime})

\displaystyle\underset{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\ell^{\prime}}}{\propto}\log\overline{\omega}_{\ell^{\prime}t^{\prime}}-\frac{\overline{\omega}_{\ell^{\prime}t^{\prime}}(b_{\ell^{\prime}}-\overline{b}_{\ell^{\prime}t^{\prime}})^{2}}{2}-\log\overline{\omega}_{\ell^{\prime}t^{\prime}}+\frac{\overline{\omega}_{\ell^{\prime}t^{\prime}}\overline{b}^{2}_{\ell^{\prime}t^{\prime}}}{2}+\log\pi_{\ell^{\prime}t^{\prime}}

The first two terms above are just the log density of a Gaussian distribution with mean
\overline{b}_{\ell^{\prime}t^{\prime}}
and precision
\overline{\omega}_{\ell^{\prime}t^{\prime}}
. Therefore, the
q_{\ell^{\prime}}
that solves (
B.8
) satisfies:

\displaystyle b_{\ell^{\prime}}\>|\>\tau_{\ell^{\prime}}=t^{\prime}\sim\mathcal{N}(\overline{b}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}},\overline{\omega}^{-1}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}),

which matches (
2.9
) of the mean-scp posterior. Integrating the joint density
q_{\ell^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\ell^{\prime}})
with respect to
b_{\ell^{\prime}}
and calculating the normalizing constant, we are left with:

\displaystyle q_{\ell^{\prime}}(\tau_{\ell^{\prime}}=t^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi_{\ell^{\prime}t^{\prime}}\overline{\omega}_{\ell^{\prime}t^{\prime}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\exp[\overline{\omega}_{\ell^{\prime}t^{\prime}}\overline{b}^{2}_{\ell^{\prime}t^{\prime}}/2]}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\pi_{\ell^{\prime}t}\overline{\omega}_{\ell^{\prime}t}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\exp[\overline{\omega}_{\ell^{\prime}t}\overline{b}^{2}_{\ell^{\prime}t}/2]}

(B.9)

\displaystyle:=\overline{\pi}_{\ell^{\prime}t^{\prime}},

which matches (
2.3
). Therefore, the
q_{\ell^{\prime}}
that solves (
B.8
) has the same functional form as the posterior distribution of the mean-scp with parameters
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}}:=\{\overline{b}_{\ell^{\prime}t},\overline{\omega}_{\ell^{\prime}t},\overline{\pi}_{\ell^{\prime}t}\}_{t=1}^{T}
. It remains to show
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}}
is the same set of parameters defined in (i) of Proposition
2
. Define a partial mean residual
\tilde{r}_{-\ell t}:=\tilde{r}_{t}+\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell}}[\mu_{\ell t}]
. Then we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{q_{-\ell}}\left[\lambda_{t}r_{-\ell t}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{q_{-\ell}}\left[\prod_{j=1}^{J}\lambda_{jt}\prod_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{kt}\left(y_{t}-\sum_{\ell^{\prime}\neq\ell}\mu_{\ell^{\prime}t}-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mu_{jt}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{q_{k}}\left[\lambda_{kt}\right]\left(y_{t}-\sum_{\ell^{\prime}\neq\ell}\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell^{\prime}}}\left[\mu_{\ell^{\prime}t}\right]-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-\ell}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}\prod_{j^{\prime}\neq j}\lambda_{j^{\prime}t}\right]\right)

(by (
3.14
))

\displaystyle=\overline{\lambda}_{t}\left(y_{t}-\overline{\mu}_{-\ell t}-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\overline{\lambda}_{jt}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\overline{\lambda}_{t}\tilde{r}_{-\ell t}.

Returning to the posterior parameters for
b_{\ell}
and
\tau_{\ell}
, we can now write:

\displaystyle\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}

\displaystyle=\omega_{\ell}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t^{\prime}},

(B.10)

\displaystyle\overline{b}_{\ell t}

\displaystyle=\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}^{-1}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\tilde{r}_{-\ell t^{\prime}},

(B.11)

Examining the mean-scp posterior parameters (
C.3
)-(
C.5
), it is clear that:

\displaystyle\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell}={\texttt{mean-scp}}(\tilde{\mathbf{r}}_{-\ell,1:T}\>;\>\overline{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{1:T},\omega_{\ell},\boldsymbol{\pi}_{\ell,1:T}).

We now show that
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell}
is the unique maximizer of
F(\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N})
holding
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N}\setminus\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell}
fixed. First note that we have:

\displaystyle F(\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N})

\displaystyle\underset{\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell}}{\propto}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t}(\tilde{r}_{t}^{2}+\delta_{t})-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}\left[\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}}{\omega_{\ell}}-\frac{1-\omega_{\ell}(\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}^{-1}+\overline{b}^{2}_{\ell t})}{2}+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}}{\pi_{\ell t}}\right]

\displaystyle\underset{\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell}}{\propto}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t}\left[\left(\tilde{r}_{-\ell t}-\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\overline{b}_{\ell t^{\prime}}\overline{\pi}_{\ell t^{\prime}}\right)^{2}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}(\overline{b}^{2}_{\ell t^{\prime}}+\overline{\omega}^{-1}_{\ell t^{\prime}})\overline{\pi}_{\ell t^{\prime}}-\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\overline{b}_{\ell t^{\prime}}\overline{\pi}_{\ell t^{\prime}}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}\left[\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}}{\omega_{\ell}}-\frac{1-\omega_{\ell}(\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}^{-1}+\overline{b}^{2}_{\ell t})}{2}+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}}{\pi_{\ell t}}\right]

\displaystyle\underset{\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell}}{\propto}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t}\left[\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}(\overline{b}^{2}_{\ell t^{\prime}}+\overline{\omega}^{-1}_{\ell t^{\prime}})\overline{\pi}_{\ell t^{\prime}}-2\tilde{r}_{-\ell t}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\overline{b}_{\ell t^{\prime}}\overline{\pi}_{\ell t^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}\left[\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}}{\omega_{\ell}}-\frac{1-\omega_{\ell}(\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}^{-1}+\overline{b}^{2}_{\ell t})}{2}+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}}{\pi_{\ell t}}\right]

Assume that
\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}>0
for the moment (we will momentarily show that
\pi_{\ell t}>0
implies that this is true for the optimal value of
\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}
), then the expression above is strictly concave as a function of
\overline{b}_{\ell t}
and:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial F}{\partial\overline{b}_{\ell t}}=\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\tilde{r}_{-\ell t^{\prime}}-\left(\omega_{\ell}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\right)\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}\overline{b}_{\ell t}.

Since
\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}>0
,
F
is uniquely maximized by setting:

\displaystyle\overline{b}_{\ell t}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\tilde{r}_{-\ell t^{\prime}}}{\omega_{\ell}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t^{\prime}}},

which matches (
B.11
). Next:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial F}{\partial\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}}=\frac{\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}\left(\omega_{\ell}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t}\right)}{2\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}^{2}}-\frac{\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}}{2\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}}\begin{cases}>0,&\text{if }\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}<\omega_{\ell}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t^{\prime}},\\
=0,&\text{if }\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}=\omega_{\ell}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t^{\prime}},\\
<0,&\text{if }\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}>\omega_{\ell}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t^{\prime}}.\end{cases}

So when
\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}>0
,
F
is uniquely maximized by setting:

\displaystyle\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}=\omega_{\ell}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t^{\prime}},

which matches (
B.10
). Lastly, for any constants
\{C_{t}\}_{t=1}^{T}
, we can solve:

\displaystyle\max_{\overline{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{\ell,1:T}\in\mathcal{S}^{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}C_{t}\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}-\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}\log\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}\text{ s.t. }\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}=1

by solving:

\displaystyle\max_{\overline{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{\ell,1:T}\in\mathcal{S}^{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}C_{t}\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}-\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}\log\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}-\alpha\left(1-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\pi_{\ell t}\right)

where
\alpha\geq 0
is the Lagrange multiplier. Furthermore, since
C_{t}\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}-\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}\log\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}
is strictly concave as a function of
\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}
, and
\mathcal{S}^{T}
is closed and convex, the solution will be unique. The solution is determined by the following KKT conditions:

\displaystyle C_{t}-1-\log\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}+\alpha

\displaystyle=0,\;\forall\;t\in\{1,\ldots,T\}

\displaystyle 1-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}

\displaystyle=0.

The first condition implies
\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}=\exp[C_{t}+\alpha-1]
, which combined with the the second condition implies:

\displaystyle\alpha=1-\log\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\exp[C_{t}]\right)\implies\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}=\frac{\exp[C_{t}]}{\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\exp[C_{t^{\prime}}]}.

(B.12)

Reexamining
F
, we see that:

\displaystyle C_{t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\left(\overline{b}_{\ell t}^{2}+\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}^{-1}\right)\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t^{\prime}}}{2}+\overline{b}_{\ell t}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\tilde{r}_{-\ell t^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}}{\omega_{\ell}}+\frac{1-\omega_{\ell}(\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}^{-1}+\overline{b}^{2}_{\ell t})}{2}+\log\pi_{\ell t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\left(\overline{b}_{\ell t}^{2}+\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}^{-1}\right)\left(\omega_{\ell}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\right)}{2}+\overline{b}_{\ell t}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\tilde{r}_{-\ell t^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2}\log\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}+\log\pi_{\ell t}+\frac{1}{2}\left(1+\log\omega_{\ell}\right)

Note that
\pi_{\ell t}>0
and the expression above imply the optimal value of
\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}
