is positive for any values of
\overline{b}_{\ell t}
and
\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}
, so we are at an interior solution and the optimal values of
\overline{b}_{\ell t}
and
\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}
must take the form (
B.11
) and (
B.10
), but for these values we have:

\displaystyle C_{t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\left(\overline{b}_{\ell t}^{2}+\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}^{-1}\right)\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}}{2}+\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}\overline{b}^{2}_{\ell t}-\frac{1}{2}\log\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}+\log\pi_{\ell t}+\frac{1}{2}\left(1+\log\omega_{\ell}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}\overline{b}^{2}_{\ell t}}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\log\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}+\log\pi_{\ell t}+\frac{1}{2}\log\omega_{\ell},

which along with (
B.12
) implies:

\displaystyle\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}=\frac{\pi_{\ell t}\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\exp[\overline{\omega}_{\ell t}\overline{b}^{2}_{\ell t}/2]}{\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\pi_{\ell t^{\prime}}\overline{\omega}_{\ell t^{\prime}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\exp[\overline{\omega}_{\ell t^{\prime}}\overline{b}^{2}_{\ell t^{\prime}}/2]},

which matches (
B.9
).

Case (ii):

For fixed
k^{\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,K\}
, we treat the distributions
\{q(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\ell})\}_{\ell=1}^{L}
,
\{q(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{k})\}_{k\neq k^{\prime}}
, and
\{q(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j})\}_{j=1}^{J}
as fixed and known. Then by (
B.3
), the solution to:

\displaystyle\max_{q_{k^{\prime}}}\text{ELBO}\left(q\right),

(B.13)

is to pick
q_{k^{\prime}}
so that:

\displaystyle\log q_{k^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{k^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\underset{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{k^{\prime}}}{\propto}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-k^{\prime}}}\left[\log p\left(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\boldsymbol{\Theta}\right)\right]+\log p_{k^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{k^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\underset{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{k^{\prime}}}{\propto}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-k^{\prime}}}\left[\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\log\lambda_{t}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\lambda_{t}\left(y_{t}-\mu_{t}\right)^{2}\right]+\log p_{k^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{k^{\prime}}).

Define
\lambda_{-k^{\prime}t}:=\lambda_{k^{\prime}t}^{-1}\lambda_{t}
. If we fix
\tau_{k^{\prime}}=t^{\prime}
, then we have:

\displaystyle\log q_{k^{\prime}}(s_{k^{\prime}},\tau_{k^{\prime}}=t^{\prime})

\displaystyle\underset{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{k^{\prime}}}{\propto}\frac{T-t^{\prime}+1}{2}\log s_{k^{\prime}}-\frac{s_{k^{\prime}}}{2}\sum_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-k^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{-k^{\prime}t}\left(y_{t}-\mu_{t}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-k^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{-k^{\prime}t}\left(y_{t}-\mu_{t}\right)^{2}\right]+(u_{k^{\prime}}-1)\log s_{k^{\prime}}-v_{k^{\prime}}s_{k^{\prime}}+\log\pi_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}}.

Define:

\displaystyle\overline{u}_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=u_{k^{\prime}}+\frac{T-t^{\prime}+1}{2}

\displaystyle\overline{v}_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=v_{k^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-k^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{-k^{\prime}t}\left(y_{t}-\mu_{t}\right)^{2}\right].

Then we have:

\displaystyle\log q_{k^{\prime}}(s_{k^{\prime}},\tau_{k^{\prime}}=t^{\prime})

\displaystyle\underset{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{k^{\prime}}}{\propto}-\log\Gamma(\overline{u}_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}})+\overline{u}_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}}\log\overline{v}_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}}+\left(\overline{u}_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}}-1\right)\log s_{k^{\prime}}-\overline{v}_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}}s_{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad+\log\pi_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}}+\log\Gamma(\overline{u}_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}})-\overline{u}_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}}\log\overline{v}_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-k^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{-k^{\prime}t}\left(y_{t}-\mu_{t}\right)^{2}\right]

The first line above is just the log density of a Gamma distribution with shape and rate parameters
\overline{u}_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}}
, and
\overline{v}_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}}
. Therefore, the
q_{k^{\prime}}
that solves (
B.13
) satisfies:

\displaystyle s_{k^{\prime}}\>|\>\tau_{k^{\prime}}=t^{\prime}\sim\text{Gamma}(\overline{u}_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}},\overline{v}_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}}),

which matches (
2.14
) of the var-scp posterior. Integrating the joint density
q_{k^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{k^{\prime}})
with respect to
s_{k^{\prime}}
and calculating the normalizing constant, we are left with:

\displaystyle q_{k^{\prime}}(\tau_{k^{\prime}}=t)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi_{k^{\prime}t}\Gamma(\overline{u}_{k^{\prime}t})\overline{v}_{k^{\prime}t}^{-\overline{u}_{k^{\prime}t}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-k^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{-k^{\prime}t^{\prime}}(y_{t^{\prime}}-\mu_{t^{\prime}})^{2}\right]\right)}{\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\pi_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}}\Gamma(\overline{u}_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}})\overline{v}_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}}^{-\overline{u}_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime\prime}=1}^{t^{\prime}-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-k^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{-k^{\prime}t^{\prime\prime}}(y_{t^{\prime\prime}}-\mu_{t^{\prime\prime}})^{2}\right]\right)}

\displaystyle:=\overline{\pi}_{k^{\prime}t},

which matches (
2.3
). Therefore, the
q_{k^{\prime}}
that solves (
B.13
) has the same functional form as the posterior distribution of the var-scp model with parameters
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k^{\prime}}:=\{\overline{u}_{k^{\prime}t},\overline{v}_{k^{\prime}t},\overline{\pi}_{k^{\prime}t}\}_{t=1}^{T}
. It remains is to show
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k^{\prime}}
is the same set of parameters defined in (ii) of Proposition
2
. Define a partial scale residual
\overline{\lambda}_{-kt}:=\overline{\lambda}_{kt}^{-1}\overline{\lambda}_{t}
. Then using the residual
\tilde{r}_{t}
(
B.4
) and the variance correction term
\delta_{t}
(
B.6
), we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{q_{-k}}\left[\lambda_{-kt}(y_{t}-\mu_{t})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{q_{-k}}\left[\prod_{j=1}^{J}\lambda_{jt}\prod_{k^{\prime}\neq k}\lambda_{k^{\prime}t}\left(y_{t}-\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{J}\mu_{j^{\prime}t}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mu_{\ell t}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\right]\prod_{k^{\prime}\neq k}\mathbb{E}_{q_{k^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{k^{\prime}t}\right]\mathbb{E}_{q_{-k}}\left[\left(y_{t}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mu_{\ell t}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt-2\prod_{k^{\prime}\neq k}\mathbb{E}_{q_{k^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{k^{\prime}t}\right]\mathbb{E}_{q_{-k}}\left[y_{t}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mu_{\ell t}\right]\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}\right]\prod_{j^{\prime}\neq j}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j^{\prime}}}[\lambda_{j^{\prime}t}]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+\prod_{k^{\prime}\neq k}\mathbb{E}_{q_{k^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{k^{\prime}t}\right]\mathbb{E}_{q_{-k}}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{J}\prod_{j^{\prime\prime}=1}^{J}\lambda_{j^{\prime\prime}t}\mu_{jt}\mu_{j^{\prime}t}\right]

\displaystyle=\overline{\lambda}_{-kt}\left[\left(y_{t}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\overline{\mu}_{\ell t}\right)^{2}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left(\overline{\mu^{2}_{\ell t}}-\overline{\mu}^{2}_{\ell t}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt-2\overline{\lambda}_{-kt}\left(y_{t}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\overline{\mu}_{\ell t}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{J}\overline{\lambda}_{jt}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+\overline{\lambda}_{-kt}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{J}\overline{\lambda}_{jt}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-k}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}\right]\overline{\lambda}_{j^{\prime}t}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{j^{\prime}t}\mu_{j^{\prime}t}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+\overline{\lambda}_{-kt}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left(\overline{\lambda}_{jt}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}^{2}\right]-\overline{\lambda}_{jt}^{-2}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}\right]^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\overline{\lambda}_{-kt}\left(y_{t}-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\overline{\lambda}_{jt}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}\right]-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\overline{\mu}_{\ell t}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+\overline{\lambda}_{-kt}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left(\overline{\mu^{2}_{\ell t}}-\overline{\mu}^{2}_{\ell t}\right)+\sum_{j=1}^{J}\left(\overline{\lambda}_{jt}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}^{2}\right]-\overline{\lambda}_{jt}^{-2}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}\right]^{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\overline{\lambda}_{-kt}(\tilde{r}^{2}_{t}+\delta_{t}).

(by (
B.4
) and (
B.6
))

We now define variance corrected prior parameters for the rate of
s_{k}
and location of the change-point:

\displaystyle\tilde{v}_{kt}

\displaystyle:=v_{k}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-kt^{\prime}}\delta_{t^{\prime}},

(B.14)

\displaystyle\tilde{\pi}_{kt}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\pi_{kt}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}\overline{\lambda}_{-kt^{\prime}}\delta_{t^{\prime}}\right)}{\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\pi_{kt^{\prime}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime\prime}=1}^{t^{\prime}-1}\overline{\lambda}_{-kt^{\prime\prime}}\delta_{t^{\prime\prime}}\right)}.

(B.15)

Then, returning to the posterior parameters for
s_{k}
when
\tau_{k}=t
, we now have:

\displaystyle\overline{u}_{kt}

\displaystyle=u_{k}+\frac{T-t+1}{2},

(B.16)

\displaystyle\overline{v}_{kt}

\displaystyle=v_{k}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-kt^{\prime}}\tilde{r}^{2}_{t^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-kt^{\prime}}\delta_{t^{\prime}}

(B.17)

\displaystyle=\tilde{v}_{kt}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-kt^{\prime}}\tilde{r}^{2}_{t^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\overline{\pi}_{kt}

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}\overline{\lambda}_{-kt^{\prime}}\delta_{t^{\prime}}\right)\pi_{kt}\Gamma(\overline{u}_{kt})\overline{v}_{kt}^{-\overline{u}_{kt}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}\overline{\lambda}_{-kt^{\prime}}\tilde{r}_{t^{\prime}}^{2}\right)}{\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime\prime}=1}^{t^{\prime}-1}\overline{\lambda}_{-kt^{\prime\prime}}\delta_{t^{\prime\prime}}\right)\pi_{kt^{\prime}}\Gamma(\overline{u}_{kt^{\prime}})\overline{v}_{kt^{\prime}}^{-\overline{u}_{kt^{\prime}}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime\prime}=1}^{t^{\prime}-1}\overline{\lambda}_{-kt^{\prime\prime}}\tilde{r}_{t^{\prime\prime}}^{2}\right)}

(B.18)

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{\pi}_{kt}\Gamma(\overline{u}_{kt})\overline{v}_{kt}^{-\overline{u}_{kt}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}\overline{\lambda}_{-kt^{\prime}}\tilde{r}_{t^{\prime}}^{2}\right)}{\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\tilde{\pi}_{kt^{\prime}}\Gamma(\overline{u}_{kt^{\prime}})\overline{v}_{kt^{\prime}}^{-\overline{u}_{kt^{\prime}}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime\prime}=1}^{t^{\prime}-1}\overline{\lambda}_{-kt^{\prime\prime}}\tilde{r}_{t^{\prime\prime}}^{2}\right)}.

Examining the var-scp posterior parameters (
C.8
)-(
C.10
), it is clear that:

\displaystyle\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}={\texttt{var-scp}}(\tilde{\mathbf{r}}_{1:T}\>;\>\overline{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{-k,1:T},u_{k},\tilde{\mathbf{v}}_{k,1:T},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{k,1:T}).

We now show that
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}
is the unique maximizer of
F(\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N})
holding
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N}\setminus\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}
fixed. First note that we have:

\displaystyle F(\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N})

\displaystyle\underset{\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}}{\propto}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t}(\tilde{r}_{t}^{2}+\delta_{t})+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(T-t+1)\overline{\pi}_{kt}[\psi(\overline{u}_{kt})-\log\overline{v}_{kt}]

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{kt}\left[u_{k}\log\frac{\overline{v}_{kt}}{v_{k}}-\log\frac{\Gamma(\overline{u}_{kt})}{\Gamma(u_{k})}+(\overline{u}_{kt}-u_{k})\psi(\overline{u}_{kt})-\frac{(\overline{v}_{kt}-v_{k})\overline{u}_{kt}}{\overline{v}_{kt}}+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{kt}}{\pi_{kt}}\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\frac{\overline{\pi}_{kt^{\prime}}\overline{u}_{kt^{\prime}}}{\overline{v}_{kt^{\prime}}}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t+1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{kt^{\prime}}\right)\overline{\lambda}_{-kt}(\tilde{r}_{t}^{2}+\delta_{t})+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(T-t+1)\overline{\pi}_{kt}[\psi(\overline{u}_{kt})-\log\overline{v}_{kt}]

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{kt}\left[u_{k}\log\frac{\overline{v}_{kt}}{v_{k}}-\log\frac{\Gamma(\overline{u}_{kt})}{\Gamma(u_{k})}+(\overline{u}_{kt}-u_{k})\psi(\overline{u}_{kt})-\frac{(\overline{v}_{kt}-v_{k})\overline{u}_{kt}}{\overline{v}_{kt}}+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{kt}}{\pi_{kt}}\right].

First, we have:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial F}{\partial\overline{v}_{kt}}=\frac{\overline{\pi}_{kt}\overline{u}_{kt}}{\overline{v}_{kt}^{2}}\left[v_{k}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\frac{\overline{\lambda}_{-kt^{\prime}}(\tilde{r}^{2}_{t^{\prime}}+\delta_{t^{\prime}})}{2}\right]-\frac{\overline{\pi}_{kt}}{\overline{v}_{kt}}\left[u_{k}+\frac{(T-t+1)}{2}\right].

Assume that
\overline{\pi}_{kt}>0
for the moment (we will momentarily show that
\pi_{kt}>0
implies that this is true for the optimal value of
\overline{\pi}_{kt}
). For any given
\overline{u}_{kt}
, define:

\displaystyle\overline{v}_{kt}\circ(\overline{u}_{kt}):=\frac{\overline{u}_{kt}}{u_{k}+\frac{(T-t+1)}{2}}\left[v_{k}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-kt^{\prime}}(\tilde{r}^{2}_{t^{\prime}}+\delta_{t^{\prime}})\right].

Then:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial F}{\partial\overline{v}_{kt}}\begin{cases}>0,&\text{if }\overline{v}_{kt}<\overline{v}_{kt}\circ(\overline{u}_{kt}),\\
=0,&\text{if }\overline{v}_{kt}=\overline{v}_{kt}\circ(\overline{u}_{kt}),\\
<0,&\text{if }\overline{v}_{kt}>\overline{v}_{kt}\circ(\overline{u}_{kt}).\end{cases}

So
F
is uniquely maximized by setting
\overline{v}_{kt}=\overline{v}_{kt}\circ(\overline{u}_{kt})
. Next we have:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial F}{\partial\overline{u}_{kt}}\bigg|_{\overline{v}_{kt}=\overline{v}_{kt}\circ(\overline{u}_{kt})}

\displaystyle=\overline{\pi}_{kt}\psi^{\prime}(\overline{u}_{kt})\left(u_{k}+\frac{T-t+1}{2}-\overline{u}_{kt}\right)-\frac{\overline{\pi}_{kt}}{\overline{u}_{kt}}\left(u_{k}+\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)+\overline{\pi}_{kt}.

We can use the following bounds for the trigamma function when
u>0
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{u}+\frac{1}{2u^{2}}\leq\psi^{\prime}(u)\leq\frac{1}{u}+\frac{1}{u^{2}}

to write:

\displaystyle\frac{\overline{\pi}_{kt}}{2\overline{u}_{kt}^{2}}\left(u_{k}+\frac{T-t+1}{2}-\overline{u}_{kt}\right)\leq\frac{\partial F}{\partial\overline{u}_{kt}}\bigg|_{\overline{v}_{kt}=\overline{v}_{kt}\circ(\overline{u}_{kt})}\leq\frac{\overline{\pi}_{kt}}{\overline{u}_{kt}^{2}}\left(u_{k}+\frac{T-t+1}{2}-\overline{u}_{kt}\right).

Therefore:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial F}{\partial\overline{u}_{kt}}\bigg|_{\overline{v}_{kt}=\overline{v}_{kt}\circ(\overline{u}_{kt})}\begin{cases}>0,&\text{if }\overline{u}_{kt}<u_{k}+\frac{T-t+1}{2},\\
=0,&\text{if }\overline{u}_{kt}=u_{k}+\frac{T-t+1}{2},\\
<0,&\text{if }\overline{u}_{kt}>u_{k}+\frac{T-t+1}{2}.\end{cases}

Showing that
F
is uniquely maximized when we set:

\displaystyle\overline{u}_{kt}

\displaystyle=u_{k}+\frac{T-t+1}{2}

\displaystyle\overline{v}_{kt}

\displaystyle=\overline{v}_{kt}\circ\left(u_{k}+\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right),

\displaystyle=v_{k}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-kt^{\prime}}(\tilde{r}^{2}_{t^{\prime}}+\delta_{t^{\prime}}),

which match (
B.16
) and (
B.17
). Lastly we can write:

\displaystyle F(\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N})

\displaystyle\underset{\overline{\pi}_{kt}}{\propto}C_{t}\overline{\pi}_{kt}+\overline{\pi}_{kt}\log\overline{\pi}_{kt},

\displaystyle C_{t}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{2}\left[\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}\overline{\lambda}_{-kt^{\prime}}(\tilde{r}_{t^{\prime}}^{2}+\delta_{t^{\prime}})+\frac{\overline{u}_{kt}}{\overline{v}_{kt}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-kt^{\prime}}(\tilde{r}_{t^{\prime}}^{2}+\delta_{t^{\prime}})-(T-t+1)[\psi(\overline{u}_{kt})-\log\overline{v}_{kt}]\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\left[[u_{k}\log\frac{\overline{v}_{kt}}{v_{k}}-\log\frac{\Gamma(\overline{u}_{kt})}{\Gamma(u_{k})}+(\overline{u}_{kt}-u_{k})\psi(\overline{u}_{kt})-\frac{(\overline{v}_{kt}-v_{k})\overline{u}_{kt}}{\overline{v}_{kt}}-\log\pi_{kt}\right].

Note that
\pi_{kt}>0
and the expression above imply the optimal value of
\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}
is positive for any values of
\overline{u}_{kt}
and
\overline{v}_{kt}
, so we are at an interior solution and the optimal values of
\overline{u}_{kt}
and
\overline{v}_{\ell t}
must take the form (
B.16
) and (
B.17
), but for these values we have:

\displaystyle C_{t}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}\overline{\lambda}_{-kt^{\prime}}(\tilde{r}_{t^{\prime}}^{2}+\delta_{t^{\prime}})-\overline{u}_{kt}\log\overline{v}_{kt}++\log\Gamma(\overline{u}_{kt})+\log\pi_{kt}+u_{k}\log v_{k}-\log\Gamma(u_{k}).

which along with (
B.12
) implies:

\displaystyle\overline{\pi}_{kt}=\frac{\pi_{kt}\Gamma(\overline{u}_{kt})\overline{v}_{kt}^{-\overline{u}_{kt}}\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}\overline{\lambda}_{-kt^{\prime}}(\tilde{r}_{t^{\prime}}^{2}+\delta_{t^{\prime}})\right]}{\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\pi_{kt^{\prime}}\Gamma(\overline{u}_{kt^{\prime}})\overline{v}_{kt^{\prime}}^{-\overline{u}_{kt^{\prime}}}\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime\prime}=1}^{t^{\prime}-1}\overline{\lambda}_{-kt^{\prime\prime}}(\tilde{r}_{t^{\prime\prime}}^{2}+\delta_{t^{\prime\prime}})\right]},

which matches (
B.18
).

Case (iii):
For fixed
j^{\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,J\}
, we treat the distributions
\{q_{\ell}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\ell})\}_{\ell=1}^{L}
,
\{q_{k}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{k})\}_{k=1}^{K}
, and
\{q_{j}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j})\}_{j\neq j^{\prime}}
as fixed and known. Then by (
B.3
), the solution to:

\displaystyle\max_{q_{j^{\prime}}}\text{ELBO}\left(q\right),

(B.19)

is to pick
q_{j^{\prime}}
so that:

\displaystyle\log q_{j^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\underset{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j^{\prime}}}{\propto}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[\log p\left(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\boldsymbol{\Theta}\right)\right]+\log p_{j^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\underset{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j^{\prime}}}{\propto}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\log\lambda_{t}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\lambda_{t}\left(y_{t}-\mu_{t}\right)^{2}\right]+\log p_{j^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j^{\prime}}).

Define
\lambda_{-j^{\prime}t}:=\lambda_{j^{\prime}t}^{-1}\lambda_{t}
. If we fix
\tau_{j^{\prime}}=t^{\prime}
, then we have:

\displaystyle\log q_{j^{\prime}}(b_{j^{\prime}},s_{j^{\prime}},\tau_{j^{\prime}}=t^{\prime})

\displaystyle\underset{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j^{\prime}}}{\propto}\frac{T-t^{\prime}+1}{2}\log s_{j^{\prime}}-\frac{s_{j^{\prime}}}{2}\sum_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{-j^{\prime}t}(r_{-j^{\prime}t}-b_{j^{\prime}})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{-j^{\prime}t}r_{-j^{\prime}t}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\log s_{j^{\prime}}-\frac{s_{j^{\prime}}\omega_{j^{\prime}}b_{j^{\prime}}^{2}}{2}+(u_{j^{\prime}}-1)\log s_{j^{\prime}}-v_{j^{\prime}}s_{j^{\prime}}+\log\pi_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}.

Define:

\displaystyle\overline{\omega}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\omega_{j^{\prime}}+\sum_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{-j^{\prime}t}\right],

\displaystyle\overline{b}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\overline{\omega}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}^{-1}\sum_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{-j^{\prime}t}r_{-j^{\prime}t}\right],

\displaystyle\overline{u}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=u_{j^{\prime}}+\frac{T-t^{\prime}+1}{2},

\displaystyle\overline{v}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=v_{j^{\prime}}-\frac{\overline{\omega}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}\overline{b}^{2}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{-j^{\prime}t}r^{2}_{-j^{\prime}t}\right].

Then we have:

\displaystyle\log q_{j^{\prime}}(b_{j^{\prime}},s_{j^{\prime}},\tau_{j^{\prime}}=t^{\prime})

\displaystyle\underset{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j^{\prime}}}{\propto}-\log\Gamma(\overline{u}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}})+\overline{u}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}\log\overline{v}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}+\left(\overline{u}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}-1\right)\log s_{j^{\prime}}-\overline{v}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}s_{j^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\log(\overline{\omega}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}s_{j^{\prime}})-\frac{\overline{\omega}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}s_{j^{\prime}}}{2}(b_{j^{\prime}}-\overline{b}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\log\pi_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}+\log\Gamma(\overline{u}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}})-\overline{u}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}\log\overline{v}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2}\log\overline{\omega}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{t^{\prime}-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{-j^{\prime}t}r_{-j^{\prime}t}^{2}\right].

The first two lines above are just the log density of a Normal-Gamma distribution with parameters
\overline{b}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}
,
\overline{\omega}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}
,
\overline{u}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}
, and
\overline{v}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}
. Therefore, the
q_{j^{\prime}}
that solves (
B.19
) satisfies:

\displaystyle b_{j^{\prime}},s_{j^{\prime}}\>|\>\tau_{j^{\prime}}=t^{\prime}\sim\text{Normal-Gamma}(\overline{b}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}},\overline{\omega}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}},\overline{u}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}},\overline{v}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}),

which matches (
2.18
) of the meanvar-scp posterior. Integrating the joint density
q_{j^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{j^{\prime}})
with respect to
b_{j^{\prime}}
and
s_{j^{\prime}}
and calculating the normalizing constant, we are left with:

\displaystyle q_{j^{\prime}}(\tau_{j^{\prime}}=t)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi_{j^{\prime}t}\Gamma(\overline{u}_{j^{\prime}t})\overline{v}_{j^{\prime}t}^{-\overline{u}_{j^{\prime}t}}\overline{\omega}_{j^{\prime}t}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{-j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}r_{-j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}^{2}\right]\right)}{\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\pi_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}\Gamma(\overline{u}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}})\overline{v}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}^{-\overline{u}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}}\overline{\omega}_{j^{\prime}t^{\prime}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime\prime}=1}^{t^{\prime}-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{-j^{\prime}t^{\prime\prime}}r_{-j^{\prime}t^{\prime\prime}}^{2}\right]\right)}

\displaystyle:=\overline{\pi}_{j^{\prime}t},

which matches (
2.3
). Therefore, the
q_{j^{\prime}}
that solves (
B.19
) has the same functional form as the posterior distribution of the meavar-scp model with parameters
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{j^{\prime}}:=\{\overline{b}_{j^{\prime}t},\overline{\omega}_{j^{\prime}t},\overline{u}_{j^{\prime}t},\overline{v}_{j^{\prime}t},\overline{\pi}_{j^{\prime}t}\}_{t=1}^{T}
. It remains to show
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{j^{\prime}}
is the same set of parameters defined in (iii) of Proposition
2
. We have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{-j^{\prime}t}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[\prod_{j\neq j^{\prime}}\lambda_{jt}\prod_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{kt}\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{j\neq j^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\right]\prod_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{q_{k}}\left[\lambda_{kt}\right]

(by (
3.14
))

\displaystyle=\overline{\lambda}_{-jt},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{-j^{\prime}t}r_{-j^{\prime}t}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[\prod_{j\neq j^{\prime}}\lambda_{jt}\prod_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{kt}\left(y_{t}-\sum_{j^{\prime\prime}\neq j^{\prime}}\mu_{j^{\prime\prime}t}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mu_{\ell t}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{j\neq j^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\right]\prod_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{q_{k}}\left[\lambda_{kt}\right]\left(y_{t}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell}}\left[\mu_{\ell t}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt-\prod_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{q_{k}}\left[\lambda_{kt}\right]\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[\sum_{j^{\prime\prime}\neq j^{\prime}}\prod_{j\neq j^{\prime}}\mu_{j^{\prime\prime}t}\lambda_{jt}\right]

(by (
3.14
))

\displaystyle=\overline{\lambda}_{-j^{\prime}t}\left(y_{t}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\overline{\mu}_{\ell t}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt-\prod_{k=1}^{K}\overline{\lambda}_{kt}\sum_{j^{\prime\prime}\neq j^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[\mu_{j^{\prime\prime}t}\lambda_{j^{\prime\prime}t}\prod_{j\not\in\{j^{\prime},j^{\prime\prime}\}}\lambda_{jt}\right]

\displaystyle=\overline{\lambda}_{-j^{\prime}t}\left(y_{t}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\overline{\mu}_{\ell t}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt-\prod_{k=1}^{K}\overline{\lambda}_{kt}\sum_{j^{\prime\prime}\neq j^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j^{\prime\prime}}}\left[\mu_{j^{\prime\prime}t}\lambda_{j^{\prime\prime}t}\right]\prod_{j\not\in\{j^{\prime},j^{\prime\prime}\}}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\right]

\displaystyle=\overline{\lambda}_{-j^{\prime}t}\left(y_{t}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\overline{\mu}_{\ell t}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt-\prod_{k=1}^{K}\overline{\lambda}_{kt}\sum_{j\neq j^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\mu_{jt}\lambda_{jt}\right]\overline{\lambda}_{jt}^{-1}\overline{\lambda}_{-j^{\prime}t}

(multiply and divide by
\overline{\lambda}_{jt}
)

\displaystyle=\overline{\lambda}_{-j^{\prime}t}\left(y_{t}-\sum_{j\neq j^{\prime}}\overline{\lambda}_{jt}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\mu_{jt}\lambda_{jt}\right]-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\overline{\mu}_{\ell t}\right),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\mu_{jt}\lambda_{jt}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\mu_{jt}\>|\>s_{j},\tau_{j}\right]\lambda_{jt}\>|\>\tau_{j}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\mu_{jt}\>|\>s_{j},\tau_{j}=t^{\prime}\right]\lambda_{jt}\>|\>\tau_{j}=t^{\prime}\right]\overline{\pi}_{jt^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\overline{b}_{jt^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[s_{j}^{\mathbbm{1}\{t\geq t^{\prime}\}}\>|\>\tau_{j}=t^{\prime}\right]\overline{\pi}_{jt^{\prime}}

(
\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\mu_{jt}\>|\>s_{j},\tau_{j}=t^{\prime}\right]=\mathbbm{1}{\{t\geq t^{\prime}\}}\overline{b}_{jt^{\prime}}
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\overline{b}_{jt^{\prime}}\overline{s}_{jt^{\prime}}\overline{\pi}_{jt^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\frac{\overline{\pi}_{jt^{\prime}}\overline{b}_{jt^{\prime}}\overline{u}_{jt^{\prime}}}{\overline{v}_{jt^{\prime}}}

Define the partial mean residual
\tilde{r}_{-jt}:=\tilde{r}_{t}+\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\mu_{jt}]
, the partial scale residual
\overline{\lambda}_{-jt}:=\overline{\lambda}_{jt}^{-1}\overline{\lambda}_{t}
, and the partial correction term:

\displaystyle\delta_{-j^{\prime}t}:=\delta_{t}-\overline{\lambda}_{j^{\prime}t}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{j^{\prime}t}^{2}\right]+\overline{\lambda}_{j^{\prime}t}^{-2}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{j^{\prime}t}\mu_{j^{\prime}t}\right]^{2}.

(B.20)

Then we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{-j^{\prime}t}r_{-j^{\prime}t}\right]

\displaystyle=\overline{\lambda}_{-j^{\prime}t}\tilde{r}_{-j^{\prime}t}

We also have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{-j^{\prime}t}r^{2}_{-j^{\prime}t}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[\prod_{j\neq j^{\prime}}\lambda_{jt}\prod_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{kt}\left(y_{t}-\sum_{j^{\prime\prime}\neq j^{\prime}}\mu_{j^{\prime\prime}t}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mu_{\ell t}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{j\neq j^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\right]\prod_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{q_{k}}\left[\lambda_{kt}\right]\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[\left(y_{t}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mu_{\ell t}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt-2\prod_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{q_{k}}\left[\lambda_{kt}\right]\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[y_{t}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mu_{\ell t}\right]\sum_{j^{\prime\prime}\neq j^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j^{\prime\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{j^{\prime\prime}t}\mu_{j^{\prime\prime}t}\right]\prod_{j\not\in\{j^{\prime},j^{\prime\prime}\}}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+\prod_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}_{q_{k}}\left[\lambda_{kt}\right]\mathbb{E}_{q_{-j^{\prime}}}\left[\sum_{j^{*}\neq j^{\prime}}\sum_{j^{\prime\prime}\neq j^{\prime}}\prod_{j\neq j^{\prime}}\lambda_{jt}\mu_{j^{\prime\prime}t}\mu_{j^{*}t}\right]

\displaystyle=\overline{\lambda}_{-j^{\prime}t}\left[\left(y_{t}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\overline{\mu}_{\ell t}\right)^{2}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left(\overline{\mu^{2}_{\ell t}}-\overline{\mu}^{2}_{\ell t}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt-2\overline{\lambda}_{-j^{\prime}t}\left(y_{t}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\overline{\mu}_{\ell t}\right)\sum_{j\neq j^{\prime}}\overline{\lambda}_{jt}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+\overline{\lambda}_{-j^{\prime}t}\sum_{j\neq j^{\prime}}\sum_{j^{\prime\prime}\neq j^{\prime}}\overline{\lambda}_{j^{\prime\prime}t}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j^{\prime\prime}}}\left[\lambda_{j^{\prime\prime}t}\mu_{j^{\prime\prime}t}\right]\overline{\lambda}_{jt}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+\overline{\lambda}_{-j^{\prime}t}\sum_{j\neq j^{\prime}}\left(\overline{\lambda}_{jt}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}^{2}\right]-\overline{\lambda}_{jt}^{-2}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}\right]^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\overline{\lambda}_{-j^{\prime}t}\left(y_{t}-\sum_{j\neq j^{\prime}}\overline{\lambda}_{j^{\prime}t}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}\right]-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\overline{\mu}_{\ell t}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+\overline{\lambda}_{-j^{\prime}t}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left(\overline{\mu^{2}_{\ell t}}-\overline{\mu}^{2}_{\ell t}\right)+\sum_{j\neq j^{\prime}}\left(\overline{\lambda}_{jt}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}^{2}\right]-\overline{\lambda}_{jt}^{-2}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}\right]^{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\overline{\lambda}_{-j^{\prime}t}\left[\tilde{r}^{2}_{-j^{\prime}t}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\text{Var}_{q_{\ell}}\left(\mu_{\ell t}\right)+\sum_{j\neq j^{\prime}}\left(\overline{\lambda}_{jt}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}^{2}\right]-\overline{\lambda}_{jt}^{-2}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}\right]^{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\overline{\lambda}_{-j^{\prime}t}\left[\tilde{r}^{2}_{-j^{\prime}t}+\delta_{-j^{\prime}t}\right]

Finally, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\lambda_{jt}\mu^{2}_{jt}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\mu^{2}_{jt}\>|\>s_{j},\tau_{j}\right]\lambda_{jt}\>|\>\tau_{j}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[\mu^{2}_{jt}\>|\>s_{j},\tau_{j}=t^{\prime}\right]\lambda_{jt}\>|\>\tau_{j}=t^{\prime}\right]\overline{\pi}_{jt^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}\left[(\overline{b}_{jt^{\prime}}^{2}+(\overline{\omega}_{jt^{\prime}}s_{j})^{-1})s_{j}^{\mathbbm{1}\{t\geq t^{\prime}\}}\>|\>\tau_{j}=t^{\prime}\right]\overline{\pi}_{jt^{\prime}}

(
\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\mu^{2}_{jt}\>|\>s_{j},\tau_{j}=t^{\prime}]=\mathbbm{1}{\{t\geq t^{\prime}\}}(\overline{b}_{jt^{\prime}}^{2}+(\overline{\omega}_{jt^{\prime}}s_{j})^{-1})
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\left(\overline{b}^{2}_{jt^{\prime}}\overline{s}_{jt^{\prime}}+\overline{\omega}_{jt^{\prime}}^{-1}\right)\overline{\pi}_{jt^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\overline{\pi}_{jt^{\prime}}\left(\frac{\overline{b}^{2}_{jt^{\prime}}\overline{u}_{jt^{\prime}}}{\overline{v}_{jt^{\prime}}}+\frac{1}{\overline{\omega}_{jt^{\prime}}}\right).

We now define variance corrected prior parameters for the rate of
s_{j}
and location of the change-point:

\displaystyle\tilde{v}_{jt}

\displaystyle:=v_{j}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}\delta_{-jt^{\prime}},

(B.21)

\displaystyle\tilde{\pi}_{jt}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\pi_{jt}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}\delta_{-jt^{\prime}}\right)}{\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\pi_{jt^{\prime}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime\prime}=1}^{t^{\prime}-1}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime\prime}}\delta_{-jt^{\prime\prime}}\right)}.

(B.22)

Then, returning to the posterior parameters for
b_{j}
and
s_{j}
when
\tau_{j}=t
, we now have:

\displaystyle\overline{\omega}_{jt}

\displaystyle=\omega_{j}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}},

(B.23)

\displaystyle\overline{b}_{jt}

\displaystyle=\overline{\omega}_{jt}^{-1}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}\tilde{r}_{-jt^{\prime}},

(B.24)

\displaystyle\overline{u}_{jt}

\displaystyle=u_{j}+\frac{T-t+1}{2},

(B.25)

\displaystyle\overline{v}_{jt}

\displaystyle=v_{j}-\frac{\overline{\omega}_{jt}\overline{b}^{2}_{jt}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}\tilde{r}^{2}_{jt^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}\delta_{-jt^{\prime}}

(B.26)

\displaystyle=\tilde{v}_{jt}-\frac{\overline{\omega}_{jt}\overline{b}^{2}_{jt}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}\tilde{r}^{2}_{jt^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}\delta_{-jt^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\overline{\pi}_{jt}

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}\delta_{-jt^{\prime}}\right)\pi_{jt}\Gamma(\overline{u}_{jt})\overline{v}_{jt}^{-\overline{u}_{jt}}\overline{\omega}_{jt}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}\tilde{r}_{-jt^{\prime}}^{2}\right)}{\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime\prime}=1}^{t^{\prime}-1}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime\prime}}\delta_{-j^{\prime}t^{\prime\prime}}\right)\pi_{jt^{\prime}}\Gamma(\overline{u}_{jt^{\prime}})\overline{v}_{jt^{\prime}}^{-\overline{u}_{jt^{\prime}}}\overline{\omega}_{jt^{\prime}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime\prime}=1}^{t^{\prime}-1}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime\prime}}\tilde{r}_{-jt^{\prime\prime}}^{2}\right)}

(B.27)

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{\pi}_{jt}\Gamma(\overline{u}_{jt})\overline{v}_{jt}^{-\overline{u}_{jt}}\overline{\omega}_{jt}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}\tilde{r}_{-jt^{\prime}}^{2}\right)}{\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\tilde{\pi}_{jt^{\prime}}\Gamma(\overline{u}_{jt^{\prime}})\overline{v}_{jt^{\prime}}^{-\overline{u}_{jt^{\prime}}}\overline{\omega}_{jt^{\prime}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime\prime}=1}^{t^{\prime}-1}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime\prime}}\tilde{r}_{-jt^{\prime\prime}}^{2}\right)}.

Examining the meanvar-scp posterior parameters (
C.14
)-(
C.18
), it is clear that:

\displaystyle\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{j}:={\texttt{meanvar-scp}}(\tilde{\mathbf{r}}_{-j,1:T}\>;\>\overline{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{-j,1:T},\omega_{j},u_{j},\tilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j,1:T},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{j,1:T}).

We now show that
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{j}
is the unique maximizer of
F(\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N})
holding
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N}\setminus\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{j}
fixed. First note that we have:

\displaystyle F(\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N})

\displaystyle\underset{\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{j}}{\propto}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{t}(\tilde{r}_{t}^{2}+\delta_{t})+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(T-t+1)\overline{\pi}_{jt}[\psi(\overline{u}_{jt})-\log\overline{v}_{jt}]

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{jt}\left[\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\overline{\omega}_{jt}}{\omega_{j}}-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\omega_{j}}{2\overline{\omega}_{jt}}+\frac{\omega_{j}\overline{u}_{jt}\overline{b}_{jt}^{2}}{2\overline{v}_{jt}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{jt}\left[u_{j}\log\frac{\overline{v}_{jt}}{v_{j}}-\log\frac{\Gamma(\overline{u}_{jt})}{\Gamma(u_{j})}+(\overline{u}_{jt}-u_{j})\psi(\overline{u}_{jt})-\frac{(\overline{v}_{jt}-v_{j})\overline{u}_{jt}}{\overline{v}_{jt}}+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{jt}}{\pi_{jt}}\right].

Note that:

\displaystyle\overline{\lambda}_{t}(\tilde{r}_{t}^{2}+\delta_{t})

\displaystyle=\overline{\lambda}_{jt}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt}\left[\left(\tilde{r}_{-jt}-\frac{\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}]}{\overline{\lambda}_{jt}}\right)^{2}+\delta_{-jt}+\frac{\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}\mu^{2}_{jt}]}{\overline{\lambda}_{jt}}-\frac{\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}]^{2}}{\overline{\lambda}_{jt}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\overline{\lambda}_{jt}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt}(\tilde{r}_{-jt}^{2}+\delta_{-jt})+\overline{\lambda}_{-jt}(\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}\mu^{2}_{jt}]-2\tilde{r}_{-jt}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}])

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\frac{\overline{\pi}_{jt^{\prime}}\overline{u}_{jt^{\prime}}}{\overline{v}_{jt^{\prime}}}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t+1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{jt^{\prime}}\right)\overline{\lambda}_{-jt}(\tilde{r}_{-jt}^{2}+\delta_{-jt})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\overline{\lambda}_{-jt}\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\overline{\pi}_{jt^{\prime}}\left(\frac{\overline{b}^{2}_{jt^{\prime}}\overline{u}_{jt^{\prime}}}{\overline{v}_{jt^{\prime}}}+\frac{1}{\overline{\omega}_{jt^{\prime}}}\right)-2\tilde{r}_{-jt}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\frac{\overline{\pi}_{jt^{\prime}}\overline{b}_{jt^{\prime}}\overline{u}_{jt^{\prime}}}{\overline{v}_{jt^{\prime}}}\right).

Thus:

\displaystyle F(\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N})

\displaystyle\underset{\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{j}}{\propto}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\frac{\overline{\pi}_{jt^{\prime}}\overline{u}_{jt^{\prime}}}{\overline{v}_{jt^{\prime}}}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t+1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{jt^{\prime}}\right)\overline{\lambda}_{-jt}(\tilde{r}_{-jt}^{2}+\delta_{-jt})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt}\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\overline{\pi}_{jt^{\prime}}\left(\frac{\overline{b}^{2}_{jt^{\prime}}\overline{u}_{jt^{\prime}}}{\overline{v}_{jt^{\prime}}}+\frac{1}{\overline{\omega}_{jt^{\prime}}}\right)-2\tilde{r}_{-jt}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\frac{\overline{\pi}_{jt^{\prime}}\overline{b}_{jt^{\prime}}\overline{u}_{jt^{\prime}}}{\overline{v}_{jt^{\prime}}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(T-t+1)\overline{\pi}_{jt}[\psi(\overline{u}_{jt})-\log\overline{v}_{jt}]

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{jt}\left[\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\overline{\omega}_{jt}}{\omega_{j}}-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\omega_{j}}{2\overline{\omega}_{jt}}+\frac{\omega_{j}\overline{u}_{jt}\overline{b}_{jt}^{2}}{2\overline{v}_{jt}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\pi}_{jt}\left[u_{j}\log\frac{\overline{v}_{jt}}{v_{j}}-\log\frac{\Gamma(\overline{u}_{jt})}{\Gamma(u_{j})}+(\overline{u}_{jt}-u_{j})\psi(\overline{u}_{jt})-\frac{(\overline{v}_{jt}-v_{j})\overline{u}_{jt}}{\overline{v}_{jt}}+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{jt}}{\pi_{jt}}\right].

Assume that
\overline{\pi}_{jt}>0
for the moment (we will momentarily show that
\pi_{jt}>0
implies that this is true for the optimal value of
\overline{\pi}_{jt}
), then the expression above is strictly concave as a function of
\overline{b}_{jt}
and:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial F}{\partial\overline{b}_{jt}}=\frac{\overline{\pi}_{jt}\overline{u}_{jt}}{\overline{v}_{jt}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}\tilde{r}_{-jt^{\prime}}-\left(\omega_{j}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}\right)\frac{\overline{\pi}_{jt}\overline{u}_{jt}\overline{b}_{jt}}{\overline{v}_{jt}},

Since
\overline{\pi}_{jt},\overline{u}_{jt},\overline{v}_{jt}>0
,
F
is uniquely maximized by setting:

\displaystyle\overline{b}_{jt}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}\tilde{r}_{-jt^{\prime}}}{\omega_{j}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}},

which matches (
B.24
). Next:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial F}{\partial\overline{\omega}_{jt}}=\frac{\overline{\pi}_{jt}\left(\omega_{j}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt}\right)}{2\overline{\omega}_{jt}^{2}}-\frac{\overline{\pi}_{jt}}{2\overline{\omega}_{jt}}\begin{cases}>0,&\text{if }\overline{\omega}_{jt}<\omega_{j}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}},\\
=0,&\text{if }\overline{\omega}_{jt}=\omega_{j}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}},\\
<0,&\text{if }\overline{\omega}_{jt}>\omega_{j}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}.\end{cases}

So when
\overline{\pi}_{jt}>0
,
F
is uniquely maximized by setting:

\displaystyle\overline{\omega}_{jt}=\omega_{j}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}},

which matches (
B.23
). Next, we have:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial F}{\partial\overline{v}_{jt}}=\frac{\overline{\pi}_{jt}\overline{u}_{jt}}{\overline{v}_{jt}^{2}}\left[v_{j}+\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}(\tilde{r}^{2}_{-jt^{\prime}}+\delta_{-jt^{\prime}})}{2}+\frac{\overline{b}_{jt}^{2}\left(\omega_{j}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}\right)}{2}-\overline{b}^{2}_{jt}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}\tilde{r}_{-jt^{\prime}}\right]-\frac{\overline{\pi}_{jt}}{\overline{v}_{jt}}\left[u_{j}+\frac{(T-t+1)}{2}\right].

For any given
\overline{b}_{jt},\overline{u}_{jt}
, define:

\displaystyle\overline{v}_{jt}\circ(\overline{u}_{jt},\overline{b}_{jt}):=\frac{\overline{u}_{jt}}{u_{j}+\frac{(T-t+1)}{2}}\left[v_{j}+\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}(\tilde{r}^{2}_{-jt^{\prime}}+\delta_{-jt^{\prime}})}{2}+\frac{\overline{b}_{jt}^{2}\left(\omega_{j}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}\right)}{2}-\overline{b}^{2}_{jt}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}\tilde{r}_{-jt^{\prime}}\right].

Then:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial F}{\partial\overline{v}_{jt}}\begin{cases}>0,&\text{if }\overline{v}_{jt}<\overline{v}_{jt}\circ(\overline{u}_{jt},\overline{b}_{jt}),\\
=0,&\text{if }\overline{v}_{jt}=\overline{v}_{jt}\circ(\overline{u}_{jt},\overline{b}_{jt}),\\
<0,&\text{if }\overline{v}_{jt}>\overline{v}_{jt}\circ(\overline{u}_{jt},\overline{b}_{jt}).\end{cases}

So
F
is uniquely maximized by setting
\overline{v}_{jt}=\overline{v}_{jt}\circ(\overline{u}_{jt},\overline{b}_{jt})
. Next we have:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial F}{\partial\overline{u}_{jt}}\bigg|_{\overline{v}_{jt}=\overline{v}_{jt}\circ(\overline{b}_{jt},\overline{u}_{jt})}

\displaystyle=\overline{\pi}_{kt}\psi^{\prime}(\overline{u}_{kt})\left(u_{k}+\frac{T-t+1}{2}-\overline{u}_{kt}\right)-\frac{\overline{\pi}_{kt}}{\overline{u}_{kt}}\left(u_{k}+\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)+\overline{\pi}_{kt}.

Therefore an identical argument as in part (ii) shows that
F
is uniquely maximized when we set:

\displaystyle\overline{u}_{jt}

\displaystyle=u_{j}+\frac{T-t+1}{2}

which matches (
B.25
). Plugging in the optimal values of
\overline{u}_{jt}
into
\overline{v}_{jt}\circ(\overline{u}_{jt},\overline{b}_{jt})
, we get:

\displaystyle\overline{v}_{jt}=v_{j}+\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}(\tilde{r}^{2}_{-jt^{\prime}}+\delta_{-jt^{\prime}})}{2}+\frac{\overline{b}_{jt}^{2}\left(\omega_{j}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}\right)}{2}-\overline{b}^{2}_{jt}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}\tilde{r}_{-jt^{\prime}},

which is equivalent to:

\displaystyle\overline{v}_{jt}=v_{j}+\frac{\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-jt^{\prime}}(\tilde{r}^{2}_{-jt^{\prime}}+\delta_{-jt^{\prime}})}{2}-\frac{\overline{\omega}_{jt}\overline{b}_{jt}^{2}}{2},

at the optimal value of
\overline{\omega}_{jt}
, and which matches (
B.26
) at the optimal value of
\overline{b}_{jt}
. Finally, an identical argument as in parts (i) and (ii) gives that the
\overline{\pi}_{jt}
that uniquely maximizes
F
matches the value (
B.27
), which is positive so long as
\pi_{jt}>0
.
∎

B.2
Estimating
\mu_{0}
and
\lambda_{0}
with Empirical Bayes

In most cases,
\mathbb{E}[y_{1}]\neq 0
and
\text{Var}(y_{1})\neq 1
; however,
\mathbf{y}_{1:T}
is nonstationary so standardizing the data is not straightforward. In this case, it is desirable to include an intercept
\mu_{0}
and initial scale
\lambda_{0}
term in (
3.2
) and (
3.3
):

\displaystyle\mu_{t}

\displaystyle:=\mu_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{J^{*}+L^{*}}\mu_{it}:=\sum_{j=1}^{J^{*}}b_{j}\mathbbm{1}\{t\geq\tau_{j}\}+\sum_{\ell=J^{*}+1}^{J^{*}+L^{*}}b_{\ell}\mathbbm{1}\{t\geq\tau_{\ell}\},

\displaystyle\lambda_{t}

\displaystyle:=\lambda_{0}\prod_{i=1}^{J^{*}+K^{*}}\lambda_{it}:=\prod_{j=1}^{J^{*}}s_{j}^{\mathbbm{1}\{t\geq\tau_{j}\}}\prod_{k=J^{*}+L^{*}+1}^{N}s_{k}^{\mathbbm{1}\{t\geq\tau_{k}\}}.

We treat
\mu_{0}
and
\lambda_{0}
as unknown nuisance parameters that must be estimated. One approach is to just set
\mu_{0}=0
and
\lambda_{0}=1
in Algorithm
1
. Then, if
\mu_{0}\neq 0
or
\lambda_{0}\neq 1
, Algorithm
1
will attempt to detect a change-point at
t=1
and center
\mu_{t}
and
\lambda_{t}
around
\mu_{0}
and
\lambda_{0}
respectively for all
t
up until the first real change-point. The problem with fitting
\mu_{0}
and
\lambda_{0}
this way is that it requires MICH to search for a change-point whose location is known. Searching for an additional change-point requires MICH to estimate
\mathcal{O}(T)
additional parameters, when we only actually needed to fit two parameters
\mu_{0}
and
\lambda_{0}
. For example, in the case of
\mu_{0}\neq 0
and
\lambda_{0}\neq 1
, we end up having to estimate
5T
additional parameters
\{\overline{b}_{t},\overline{\tau}_{t},\overline{u}_{t},\overline{v}_{t},\overline{\pi}_{t}\}_{t=1}^{T}
. Another problem with this approach is that we risk missing a real change-point, e.g. if the are
J^{*}>0
true changes and we set
J=J^{*}
to fit MICH, we will end up using one component of
J
to fit
\mu_{0}
and
\lambda_{0}
leaving only
J^{*}-1
components to fit the remaining
J^{*}
true change-points.

To circumvent these challenges, we adopt an empirical Bayes (EB) approach for estimating
\mu_{0}
and
\lambda_{0}
. We seek the values
\mu_{0}
and
\lambda_{0}
that maximize the evidence
\log p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>;\mu_{0},\lambda_{0})
. Since the evidence is now a function of
\mu_{0}
and
\lambda_{0}
, so is the ELBO:

\displaystyle\text{ELBO}(q\>;\mu_{0},\lambda_{0})

\displaystyle:=\int q(\boldsymbol{\Theta})\log\frac{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T},\boldsymbol{\Theta}\>;\mu_{0},\lambda_{0})}{q(\boldsymbol{\Theta})}\;d\boldsymbol{\Theta}

(B.28)

\displaystyle\;=\log p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>;\mu_{0},\lambda_{0})-\text{KL}(q\>\lVert\>p)

(B.29)

Note that in the expression above,
\text{ELBO}(q;\mu_{0},\lambda_{0})
is equivalent to
\log p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T};\mu_{0},\lambda_{0})
plus a term that does not depend on
\mu_{0}
or
\lambda_{0}
. Therefore, for fixed
q
, the
\mu_{0}
and
\lambda_{0}
that maximize the evidence are equivalent to:

\displaystyle\underset{\{\mu_{0},\lambda_{0}\}\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{++}}{\text{arg max}}\;\text{ELBO}(q\>;\mu_{0},\lambda_{0}),

(B.30)

which in turn is equivalent to:

\displaystyle\underset{\{\mu_{0},\lambda_{0}\}\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{++}}{\text{arg max}}\;\mathbb{E}_{q}\left[\log p\left(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\boldsymbol{\Theta};\mu_{0},\lambda_{0}\right)\right].

(B.31)

Wang
et al. 2020
use this same approach, and as they note, optimizing the ELBO over
\mu_{0}
and
\lambda_{0}
constitutes the M-step of an EM algorithm where the E-step is approximate (
Dempster
et al. 1977
;
Heskes
et al. 2003
;
Neal and
Hinton 1998
) do to the use of
q
. Define
\tilde{r}_{t}
,
\overline{\lambda}_{t}
and
\delta_{t}
as in (
B.4
)-(
B.6
) along with:

\displaystyle\tilde{r}_{-0t}

\displaystyle:=\tilde{r}_{t}+\mu_{0}

(B.32)

\displaystyle\overline{\lambda}_{-0t}

\displaystyle:=\lambda_{0}^{-1}\overline{\lambda}_{t}

(B.33)

then (
B.31
) can be rewritten as:

\displaystyle\underset{\{\mu_{0},\lambda_{0}\}\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{++}}{\text{arg max}}\;\frac{T}{2}\log\lambda_{0}-\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-0t}\left[\left(\tilde{r}_{-0t}-\mu_{0}\right)^{2}+\delta_{t}\right].

(B.34)

The solutions to (
B.34
) is given by:

\displaystyle\hat{\mu}_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-0t}\tilde{r}_{-0t}}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-0t}}

(B.35)

\displaystyle\hat{\lambda}_{0}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-0t}[\left(\tilde{r}_{-0t}-\hat{\mu}_{0}\right)^{2}+\delta_{t}]}{T}\right]^{-1}.

(B.36)

We incorporate the estimation step for
\mu_{0}
and
\lambda_{0}
into Algorithm
3
.

Inputs:
\mathbf{y}_{1:T},\>L,\>K,\>J,\>\omega_{0},\>u_{0},v_{0},\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T}
;

Initialize:
\mu_{0},\>\lambda_{0},\>\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N}
;

repeat

for

j=1

to

J

do

\tilde{r}_{-jt}:=y_{t}-\mu_{0}-\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq j}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j^{\prime}}}[\lambda_{j^{\prime}t}\mu_{j^{\prime}t}]/\mathbb{E}_{q_{j^{\prime}}}[\lambda_{j^{\prime}t}]-\sum_{\ell=J+1}^{J+L}\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell}}[\mu_{\ell t}]
;

//
j
th
partial mean residual

\overline{\lambda}_{-jt}:=\lambda_{0}\prod_{j^{\prime}\neq j}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j^{\prime}}}[\lambda_{j^{\prime}t}]\prod_{k=J+L+1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{q_{k}}[\lambda_{kt}]
;

//
j
th
partial scale residual

Compute
\delta_{-jt}
,
\tilde{v}_{jt}
, and
\tilde{\pi}_{jt}
by (
B.20
), (
B.21
), and (
B.22
) ;

//
variance corrected priors

\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{j}:=\texttt{meanvar-scp}(\tilde{\mathbf{r}}_{-j,1:T}\>;\>\overline{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{-j,1:T},\omega_{0},u_{0},\tilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j,1:T},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}_{j,1:T})
;

//
update meanvar-scp parameters

end for

for

\ell=J+1

to

J+L

do

\tilde{r}_{-\ell t}:=y_{t}-\mu_{0}-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}]/\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}]-\sum_{\ell^{\prime}\neq\ell}\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell^{\prime}}}[\mu_{\ell^{\prime}t}]
;

//
l
th
partial mean residual

\overline{\lambda}_{t}:=\lambda_{0}\prod_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}]\prod_{k=J+L+1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{q_{k}}[\lambda_{kt}]
;

//
precision of residual

\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell}:=\texttt{mean-scp}(\tilde{\mathbf{r}}_{-\ell,1:T}\>;\>\overline{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{1:T},\omega_{0},\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{1:T})
;

//
update mean-scp parameters

end for

for

k=J+L+1

to

N

do

\tilde{r}_{t}:=y_{t}-\mu_{0}-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}]/\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}]-\sum_{\ell=J+1}^{J+L}\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell}}[\mu_{\ell t}]
;

//
mean residual

\overline{\lambda}_{-kt}:=\lambda_{0}\prod_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}]\prod_{k^{\prime}\neq k}\mathbb{E}_{q_{k^{\prime}}}[\lambda_{k^{\prime}t}]
;

//
k
th
partial scale residual

Compute
\delta_{t}
,
\tilde{v}_{kt}
, and
\tilde{\pi}_{kt}
by (
B.6
), (
B.14
), and (
B.15
) ;

//
variance corrected priors

\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{k}:=\texttt{var-scp}(\tilde{\mathbf{r}}_{1:T}\>;\>\overline{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{-k,1:T},u_{0},\tilde{\mathbf{v}}_{k,1:T},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\pi}}}_{k,1:T})
;

//
update var-scp parameters

end for

\tilde{r}_{-0t}:=y_{t}-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}\mu_{jt}]/\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}]-\sum_{\ell=J+1}^{J+L}\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell}}[\mu_{\ell t}]
;

\overline{\lambda}_{-0t}:=\prod_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{q_{j}}[\lambda_{jt}]\prod_{k=J+L+1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{q_{k}}[\lambda_{kt}]\;
;

Compute
\mu_{0}
and
\lambda_{0}
by (
B.35
) and (
B.36
) ;

//
EB step

until

Convergence
;

return

Model Parameters:
\mu_{0},\>\lambda_{0},\>\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:N}
.

Algorithm 3

Empirical Bayes Variational Approximation to MICH Posterior.

Note that Algorithm
3
embeds Algorithm
1
with the additional step of maximizing the ELBO with respect to
\mu_{0}
and
\lambda_{0}
while holding the approximate posterior
q
fixed. Therefore, Algorithm
3
also defines a coordinate ascent procedure. The objective function in (
B.34
) is continuously differentiable with respect to
\mu_{0}
and
\lambda_{0}
, so if we can show that
\hat{\mu}_{0}
and
\hat{\lambda}_{0}
are the unique maximizers of (
B.34
), then the conditions of Proposition 2.7.1 of
Bertsekas 1997
will still hold even with the added coordinate ascent step of maximizing the ELBO with respect to
\mu_{0}
and
\lambda_{0}
.

To prove the uniqueness of
\hat{\mu}_{0}
and
\hat{\lambda}_{0}
, first note that the term:

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-0t}\left(\tilde{r}_{-0t}-\mu_{0}\right)^{2}

is strictly concave as a function of
\mu_{0}
and is maximized at
\hat{\mu}_{0}
. Therefore, for any
\mu_{0}\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{\hat{\mu}_{0}\}
we have:

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-0t}\left(\tilde{r}_{-0t}-\hat{\mu}_{0}\right)^{2}>-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-0t}\left(\tilde{r}_{-0t}-\mu_{0}\right)^{2}

and thus for any
\lambda_{0}>0
,

\displaystyle\frac{T}{2}\log\lambda_{0}-\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-0t}\left(\tilde{r}_{-0t}-\hat{\mu}_{0}\right)^{2}>\frac{T}{2}\log\lambda_{0}-\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-0t}\left(\tilde{r}_{-0t}-\mu_{0}\right)^{2}.

Since the
\log
is strictly concave, the left-hand side of the above inequality is uniquely maximized by
\hat{\lambda}_{0}
. So for any
\lambda_{0}>0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{T}{2}\log\hat{\lambda}_{0}-\frac{\hat{\lambda}_{0}}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-0t}\left(\tilde{r}_{-0t}-\hat{\mu}_{0}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle>\frac{T}{2}\log\lambda_{0}-\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-0t}\left(\tilde{r}_{-0t}-\hat{\mu}_{0}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle>\frac{T}{2}\log\lambda_{0}-\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{\lambda}_{-0t}\left(\tilde{r}_{-0t}-\mu_{0}\right)^{2}.

Proving that
\hat{\mu}_{0}
and
\hat{\lambda}_{0}
are the global maximizers of (
B.34
). Therefore, Proposition
1
still holds with the included maximization step for
\mu_{0}
and
\lambda_{0}
and Algorithm
3
will also converge to a stationary point.

B.3
MICH for Multivariate Mean Changes

In order to simplify the exposition of the MICH model in Section
3
, we solely focused on detecting multiple mean and variance change-points for univariate data. However, when
d>1
, it is still possible for MICH to identify multiple breaks in the mean, assuming that:

\displaystyle\mathbf{y}_{t}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{ind}.}{\sim}\mathcal{N}_{d}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t},\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{-1}_{t}),

(B.37)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}

\displaystyle:=\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{*}}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\ell t},

(B.38)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\ell t}

\displaystyle:=\mathbf{b}_{\ell}\mathbbm{1}\{t\geq\tau_{\ell}\},

(B.39)

\displaystyle\tau_{\ell}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{ind}.}{\sim}\text{Categorical}(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T})

(B.40)

\displaystyle\mathbf{b}_{\ell}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{ind}.}{\sim}\mathcal{N}_{d}(\mathbf{0},\omega_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{I}_{d}),

(B.41)

where
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is an unknown intercept and
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{1:T}
is a known sequence of precision matrices. We again define the variable and parameter blocks
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{\ell}:=\{\mathbf{b}_{\ell},\tau_{\ell}\}
and
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell}:=\{\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{\ell t},\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{\ell t},\overline{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{\ell t}\}_{t=1}^{T}
. Then Algorithm
4
shows how to modify Algorithm
3
to handle multivariate mean changes of the form (
B.38
).

Inputs:
\mathbf{y}_{1:T},\>\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{1:T},\>L,\>\omega_{0},\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T}\>;

Initialize:
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0},\>\{\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{L}\>
;

repeat

for

\ell=1

to

L

do

\overline{\mathbf{r}}_{-\ell t}:=\mathbf{y}_{t}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0}-\sum_{\ell^{\prime}\neq\ell}^{L}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{\ell^{\prime}t^{\prime}}\overline{\pi}_{\ell^{\prime}t^{\prime}}
;

//
l
th
partial mean residual

\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell}:=\texttt{mean-scp}(\overline{\mathbf{r}}_{-\ell,1:T}\>;\>\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{1:T},\omega_{0},\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{1:T})
;

//
update mean parameters

end for

\overline{\mathbf{r}}_{t}:=\mathbf{y}_{t}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{\ell t^{\prime}}\overline{\pi}_{\ell t^{\prime}}
;

//
mean residual

\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0}:=\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}\right)^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}\overline{\mathbf{r}}_{t}
;

//
update intercept

until

Convergence
;

return

Posterior Parameters:
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0},\>\{\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{L}
.

Algorithm 4

Variational Bayes Approximation to MICH Posterior for Mulivariate Mean Changes.

As with Algorithms
1
and
3
, Algorithm
4
begins by initializing some values for
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:L}
, which are then used to approximate the residual
\overline{\mathbf{r}}_{t}
. Each
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell}
is then updated iteratively by fitting the mean-scp model to the partial residual
\overline{\mathbf{r}}_{-\ell t}
in place of
\mathbf{y}_{t}
. Fitting each mean-scp model returns a distribution, so given the value of
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:L}
at the current iteration of Algorithm
4
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbf{b}_{\ell}\>|\>\overline{\mathbf{r}}_{-\ell,1:T},\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{1:T},\tau_{\ell}=t

\displaystyle\sim\mathcal{N}_{d}\left(\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{\ell t},\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}^{-1}_{\ell t}\right).

(B.42)

Let
\{q_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{L}
stand in for the
L
conditional distributions in (
B.42
) with
q_{\ell}(\tau_{\ell}=t)=\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}
. If we define
q:=\prod_{\ell=1}^{L}q_{\ell}
, then
q
is a variational approximation to the true posterior
p:=p(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1:L}\>\mathbf{y}_{1:T};\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0},\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{1:T})
that converges to a stationary point of the ELBO:

\displaystyle\text{ELBO}(q\>;\>\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0},\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{1:T})

\displaystyle:=\int q(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1:L})\log\frac{p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1:L};\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0},\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{1:T})}{q(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1:L})}\;d\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1:L}

\displaystyle\;=\log p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T};\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0},\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{1:T})-\text{KL}(q\>\lVert\>p).

This follows directly from the following result:

Proposition 3
.

Assume the model (
B.37
)-(
B.41
) with
\omega_{0},\pi_{t}>0
. Let
\{q_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{L}
be an arbitrary collection of distributions such that
q:=\prod_{\ell=1}^{L}q_{\ell}
and each coordinate of
\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell}}[\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\ell t}]
and
\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell}}[\lVert\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\ell t}\rVert_{2}^{2}]
are finite. Define the residual
\overline{\mathbf{r}}_{t}:=\mathbf{y}_{t}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell}}[\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\ell t}]
and the partial residual
\overline{\mathbf{r}}_{-\ell t}:=\overline{\mathbf{r}}_{t}+\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell}}[\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\ell t}]
. Then:

\displaystyle\text{{arg}}\max_{q_{\ell}}\text{ELBO}\left(q;\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0},\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{1:T}\right)

is equivalent to the mean-scp posterior in (
2.3
) and (
2.9
) with parameters:

\displaystyle\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell}={\texttt{mean-scp}}(\overline{\mathbf{r}}_{-\ell,1:T}\>;\>\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{1:T},\omega_{\ell},\boldsymbol{\pi}_{\ell,1:T}).

Furthermore, when each
q_{\ell}
takes this functional form, then
\text{ELBO}\left(q;\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0},\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{1:T}\right)
is equivalent to a continuously differentiable function
F(\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:L};\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0},\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{1:T})
, and
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell}
is the unique maximizer of
F
holding
\{\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\}_{\ell^{\prime}\neq\ell}
fixed.

The proof of Proposition
3
follows an identical argument to the proof of Proposition
2
(i). We simply replace the univariate variables
b_{\ell}
and
\lambda_{t}
with their multivariate analogues
\mathbf{b}_{\ell}
and
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}
. For the purpose of monitoring convergence (see Appendix
C.5
), we characterize the function
F
:

\displaystyle F(\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:L};\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0},\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{1:T})

\displaystyle\quad:=\mathbb{E}_{q}\left[\log p(\mathbf{y}_{1:T},\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0},\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{1:T})\right]-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\text{KL}(q_{\ell}\>\lVert\>p_{\ell})

\displaystyle\underset{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1:L}}{\propto}\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\log|\boldsymbol{\Lambda_{t}}|-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{q}\left[\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{y}_{t}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t})\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad-\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell}}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbbm{1}\{\tau_{\ell}=t\}\left(\log\frac{q(\mathbf{b}_{\ell}|\tau_{\ell}=t)}{p(\mathbf{b}_{\ell}|\tau_{\ell}=t)}+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}}{\pi_{\ell t}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\log|\boldsymbol{\Lambda_{t}}|-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}^{\frac{1}{2}}\overline{\mathbf{r}}_{t}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{q}\left[\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}-\boldsymbol{\overline{\mu}}_{t})\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}\left[\log|\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{\ell t}-d\log\omega_{\ell}-E_{q_{\ell}}[\lVert\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{\ell t}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{b}_{\ell}-\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{\ell t})\rVert_{2}^{2}|\tau_{\ell}]+\omega_{\ell}E_{q_{\ell}}[\lVert\mathbf{b}_{\ell}\rVert_{2}^{2}|\tau_{\ell}]+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}}{\pi_{\ell t}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\log|\boldsymbol{\Lambda_{t}}|-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}^{\frac{1}{2}}\overline{\mathbf{r}}_{t}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left(\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell}}\left[\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\ell t}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\right]-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}^{\frac{1}{2}}\overline{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\ell t}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}\left[\log|\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{\ell t}|-d\log\omega_{\ell}-d+\omega_{\ell}\left(\text{trace}\left(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{\ell t}^{-1}\right)+\lVert\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{\ell t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\right)+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}}{\pi_{\ell t}}\right].

Note that we can write:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{q_{\ell}}\left[\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\ell t}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\overline{\pi}_{\ell t^{\prime}}\sum_{1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq d}\left[\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}\right]_{ii^{\prime}}\left(\left[\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}^{-1}_{\ell t^{\prime}}\right]_{ii^{\prime}}+\left[\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{\ell t^{\prime}}\right]_{i}\left[\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{\ell t^{\prime}}\right]_{i^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\overline{\pi}_{\ell t^{\prime}}\left(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}^{\frac{1}{2}}\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{\ell t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}+\mathbf{1}_{d}^{T}\left[\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}\odot\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}^{-1}_{\ell t^{\prime}}\right]\mathbf{1}_{d}\right),

where
\odot
denotes the Hadamard product. So:

\displaystyle F(\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1:L};\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0},\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{1:T})

\displaystyle\underset{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1:L}}{\propto}\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\log|\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}|-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}^{\frac{1}{2}}\overline{\mathbf{r}}_{t}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}^{\frac{1}{2}}\overline{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\ell t}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\overline{\pi}_{\ell t^{\prime}}\left(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}^{\frac{1}{2}}\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{\ell t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}+\mathbf{1}_{d}^{T}\left[\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}\odot\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}^{-1}_{\ell t^{\prime}}\right]\mathbf{1}_{d}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}\left[\log|\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{\ell t}|-d\log\omega_{\ell}-d+\omega_{\ell}\left(\text{trace}\left(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{\ell t}^{-1}\right)+\lVert\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{\ell t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\right)+\log\frac{\overline{\pi}_{\ell t}}{\pi_{\ell t}}\right].

As argued in Appendix
B.2
, the empirical Bayes step for updating
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0}
in Algorithm
4
also constitutes a coordinate ascent step for maximizing the ELBO with respect to
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0}
seeing as:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{q}\left[\log p\left(\mathbf{y}_{1:T}\>|\>\boldsymbol{\Theta};\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0},\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{1:T}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\underset{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1:L}}{\propto}-\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0},\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}\overline{\mathbf{r}}_{t}\right\rangle,

which is convex as a function of
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{0}
and uniquely maximized on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
at:

\displaystyle\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{0}:=\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}\right)^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}\overline{\mathbf{r}}_{t}

So once again, Algorithm
4
constitutes a coordinate ascent procedure. It is possible that the sequence of precision matrices is unknown. In this case, we can replace
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{1:T}
with a consistent estimator
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{1:T}
in Algorithm
4
. For instance, if
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}=\boldsymbol{\Lambda}\;\forall\;t\in[T]
, then
\text{Var}(y_{t}-y_{t-1})=2\boldsymbol{\Lambda}^{-1}
for each
t>1
. We can define
\tilde{y}_{t}:=y_{t}-y_{t-1}
, then:

\displaystyle\hat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{T}:=\left[\frac{\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\left(\tilde{y}_{t}-\frac{\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\tilde{y}_{t}}{T-1}\right)\left(\tilde{y}_{t}-\frac{\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\tilde{y}_{t}}{T-1}\right)^{T}}{2(T-2)}\right]^{-1}

will be a consistent estimator of
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}
that we can plug directly into Algorithm
4
.

Appendix C
Computational Details

C.1
Posterior Parameters

C.1.1
Mean-SCP

The full posterior for the mean-scp model is given by:

\displaystyle\mathbf{b}\>|\>\tau=t,\>\mathbf{y}_{1:T}

\displaystyle\sim\mathcal{N}_{d}\left(\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{t},\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{t}^{-1}\right)

(C.1)

\displaystyle\tau\>|\>\mathbf{y}_{1:T}

\displaystyle\sim\text{Categorical}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{1:T})

(C.2)

\displaystyle\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\omega_{0}\mathbf{I}_{d}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t^{\prime}}

(C.3)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{t}^{-1}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}

(C.4)

\displaystyle\overline{\pi}_{t}

\displaystyle\propto\pi_{t}|\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{t}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\exp\left[\frac{\lVert\overline{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}_{t}^{\frac{1}{2}}\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{2}\right].

(C.5)

C.1.2
Var-SCP

The full posterior for the var-scp model is given by:

\displaystyle s\>|\>\tau=t,\>\mathbf{y}_{1:T}

\displaystyle\sim\text{Gamma}\left(\overline{u}_{t},\overline{v}_{t}\right)

(C.6)

\displaystyle\tau\>|\>\mathbf{y}_{1:T}

\displaystyle\sim\text{Categorical}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{1:T})

(C.7)

\displaystyle\overline{u}_{t}

\displaystyle=u_{0}+\frac{T-t+1}{2}

(C.8)

\displaystyle\overline{v}_{t}

\displaystyle=v_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\omega_{t^{\prime}}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}

(C.9)

\displaystyle\overline{\pi}_{t}

\displaystyle\propto\frac{\pi_{t}\Gamma(\overline{u}_{t})}{\overline{v}_{t}^{\overline{u}_{t}}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}\omega_{t^{\prime}}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}\right).

(C.10)

Note that the summation in the last line may be ill-defined if
t=1
. We use the convention that a sum is equal to zero if the indexing set is empty.

C.1.3
MeanVar-SCP

The full posterior for the meanvar-scp model is given by:

\displaystyle b\>|\>s,\tau=t,\mathbf{y}_{1:T}

\displaystyle\sim\mathcal{N}(\overline{b}_{t},(\overline{\omega}_{t}s)^{-1})

(C.11)

\displaystyle s\>|\>\tau=t,\mathbf{y}_{1:T}

\displaystyle\sim\text{Gamma}(\overline{u}_{t},\overline{v}_{t})

(C.12)

\displaystyle\tau\>|\>\mathbf{y}_{1:T}

\displaystyle\sim\text{Categorical}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{1:T})

(C.13)

\displaystyle\overline{\omega}_{t}

\displaystyle=\omega_{0}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\omega_{t^{\prime}}

(C.14)

\displaystyle\overline{b}_{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\frac{\omega_{t^{\prime}}y_{t^{\prime}}}{\overline{\omega}_{t}}

(C.15)

\displaystyle\overline{u}_{t}

\displaystyle=u_{0}+\frac{T-t+1}{2}

(C.16)

\displaystyle\overline{v}_{t}

\displaystyle=v_{0}-\frac{\overline{\omega}_{t}\overline{b}^{2}_{t}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\omega_{t^{\prime}}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}

(C.17)

\displaystyle\overline{\pi}_{t}

\displaystyle\propto\frac{\pi_{t}\Gamma(\overline{u}_{t})}{\overline{v}_{t}^{\overline{u}_{t}}\overline{\omega}_{t}^{1/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t-1}\omega_{t^{\prime}}y^{2}_{t^{\prime}}\right).

(C.18)

C.2
Priors on Change-Point Locations

In the absence of any prior knowledge about the location of the change-point
\tau\in[T]
, one natural choice is the uniform prior,
\pi_{t}=T^{-1}
. While this choice of
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T}
satisfies the assumption of Theorems
1
-
4

that for some constant
C_{\pi}>0
:

\displaystyle\min_{t\in[T]}|\log\pi_{t}|\leq C_{\pi}\log T

(C.19)

and therefore guarantees asymptotic consistency for each of the single change-point models in Section
2
, the uniform prior may reduce detection power or even result in false positives when
T
is small. To see this, we first show that the uniform prior does not induce a uniform posterior in the absence of a change-point. In Figure
4
, we draw 10,000 replicates of
\mathbf{y}_{1:T}
for
T=50
under the null model, i.e. where
y_{t}\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, then fit each SCP model to
\mathbf{y}_{1:T}
and use the average of the resulting posterior probabilities
\overline{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{1:T}
to produce MCMC estimates of
\mathbb{E}[\overline{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{1:T}]
for each of SCP models.

Figure 4:
Posterior Probabilities under Null Model
. MCMC estimates of
\mathbb{E}[\overline{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{1:T}]
under Null Model with
T=50
. Gray bars show
\mathbb{E}[\overline{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{1:T}]
under the uniform prior and black bars show
\mathbb{E}[\overline{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{1:T}]
under the weighted prior (red bars).

Figure
4
shows an exponential increase in the posterior probabilities as
t
approaches
T
. With a uniform prior, the SCP models may fail to detect a true change when the signal is weak or in small samples due to the large weight placed on times near
T
. For the var-scp and meanvar-scp models in particular, we see that the probabilities may be large enough to incorrectly detect a change-point in the vicinity of time
T
, even when no change is present. To rectify this behavior we propose selecting
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T}
so that under the null model we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\log\overline{\pi}_{t}-\log\overline{\pi}_{t+1}\right]=0.

(C.20)

In Figure
4
we also plot the weighted priors
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T}
that satisfy (
C.20
) and MCMC estimates of
\mathbb{E}[\overline{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t}]
under this choice
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T}
. We clearly see that these estimates adhere much more closely to the uniform dashed line at
T^{-1}
. Note that (
C.20
) also implies a uniform condition on the posterior probabilities since for any
r>t
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\log\overline{\pi}_{t}-\log\overline{\pi}_{r}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{r-t-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\log\overline{\pi}_{t+i}-\log\overline{\pi}_{t+i+1}\right]=0.

We now show how to calculate
\pi_{t}
so that (
C.20
) holds for each of the SCP models. In each case we show that, if
t_{0}
is the true location of the change point

C.2.1
Mean-SCP Prior

For the mean-scp model in Section
2.1
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\log\overline{\pi}_{t}-\log\overline{\pi}_{t+1}\right]

\displaystyle=\log\pi_{t}-\log\pi_{t+1}-\frac{1}{2}\log\left|\omega_{0}\mathbf{I}_{d}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\right|+\frac{1}{2}\log\left|\omega_{0}\mathbf{I}_{d}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t+1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\left[\omega_{0}\mathbf{I}_{d}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\right]^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\right]-\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\left[\omega_{0}\mathbf{I}_{d}+\sum_{t^{\prime}=t+1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\right]^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t+1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\right].

Letting
\omega_{0}\to 0
and noting that
\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}\sim\mathcal{N}_{d}\left(\mathbf{0},\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\right)
under the null model, we get:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\log\overline{\pi}_{t}-\log\overline{\pi}_{t+1}\right]

\displaystyle=\log\pi_{t}-\log\pi_{t+1}-\frac{1}{2}\log\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\right|+\frac{1}{2}\log\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t+1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\left[\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\right]^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\right]-\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\left[\sum_{t^{\prime}=t+1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\right]^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{t^{\prime}=t+1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\mathbf{y}_{t^{\prime}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\log\pi_{t}-\log\pi_{t+1}-\frac{1}{2}\log\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\right|+\frac{1}{2}\log\left|\sum_{t^{\prime}=t+1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t^{\prime}}\right|.

If
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{t}=\boldsymbol{\Lambda}
for all
t
, we can further simplify to:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\log\overline{\pi}_{t}-\log\overline{\pi}_{t+1}\right]

\displaystyle=\log\pi_{t}-\log\pi_{t+1}+\frac{d}{2}\log\left(\frac{T-t}{T-t+1}\right).

Therefore, when (
C.20
) holds, we can set
\log\pi_{1}=0
and solve for
\log\pi_{t}
for
t>1
using the recurrence relation above, then normalize the sequence
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{1:T}
to get:

\displaystyle\log\pi_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{2}\log\left(\frac{T-t+1}{T}\right)-\log\left[\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T}\left(\frac{T-t+1}{T}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq-\left(1+\frac{d}{2}\right)\log T.

(
\frac{T-t+1}{T}\leq 1\;\forall\;t\in[T]
)

So for fixed
d
this choice of
\pi_{t}
satisfies (
C.19
) with
C_{\pi}=1+d/2
.

C.2.2
Var-SCP Prior

For the var-scp model in Section
2.2
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\log\overline{\pi}_{t}-\log\overline{\pi}_{t+1}\right]

\displaystyle=\log\pi_{t}-\log\pi_{t+1}+\log\Gamma\left(u_{0}+\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)-\log\Gamma\left(u_{0}+\frac{T-t}{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}[\omega_{t}y_{t}^{2}]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\>+\left(u_{0}+\frac{T-t}{2}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left[v_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t+1}^{T}\omega_{t^{\prime}}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}\right)\right]\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\>-\left(u_{0}+\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left[v_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\omega_{t^{\prime}}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}\right)\right]\right].

Letting
u_{0},v_{0}\to 0
and noting that
\omega_{t}y^{2}_{t}\sim\chi^{2}_{1}
under the null model, we get:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\log\overline{\pi}_{t}-\log\overline{\pi}_{t+1}\right]

\displaystyle=\log\pi_{t}-\log\pi_{t+1}+\log\Gamma\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)-\log\Gamma\left(\frac{T-t}{2}\right)+\frac{1+\log 2}{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\>+\left(\frac{T-t}{2}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t+1}^{T}\omega_{t^{\prime}}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}\right)\right]-\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\omega_{t^{\prime}}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}\right)\right].

Since
\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\omega_{t^{\prime}}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}\sim\chi^{2}_{T-t+1}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\omega_{t^{\prime}}y_{t^{\prime}}^{2}\right)\right]=\psi\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)+\log 2

where
\psi
is the digamma function. Therefore:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\log\overline{\pi}_{t}-\log\overline{\pi}_{t+1}\right]

\displaystyle=\log\pi_{t}-\log\pi_{t+1}+\log\Gamma\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)-\log\Gamma\left(\frac{T-t}{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\quad\>+\left(\frac{T-t}{2}\right)\psi\left(\frac{T-t}{2}\right)-\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)\psi\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right).

So again we have defined a recurrence relation for calculating
\pi_{t}
. Recall Binet’s first formula for the log gamma function for
x>0
(see e.g. p. 249 of
Whittaker and
Watson 1996
):

\displaystyle\log\Gamma(x)=\left(x-\frac{1}{2}\right)\log x-x+\frac{1}{2}\log 2\pi+\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{t}+\frac{1}{e^{t}-1}\right)\frac{e^{-tx}}{t}\;dt.

Since in Theorem
2
we only consider indexes
t
such that
T-t+1>\log^{\varepsilon}T
, we actually only need
\log\pi_{t}>-C_{\pi}\log T
for just these indexes. Since
T-t+1>\log^{\varepsilon}T\implies T-t+1\to\infty
, we have the approximations:

\displaystyle\log\Gamma\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\approx\left(\frac{T-t}{2}\right)\log\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)-\frac{T-t+1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\log 2\pi

\displaystyle\psi\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\approx\log\left(\frac{T-t+1}{2}\right)-\frac{1}{T-t+1}.

Then we can write:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\log\overline{\pi}_{t}-\log\overline{\pi}_{t+1}\right]

\displaystyle\approx\log\pi_{t}-\log\pi_{t+1}+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{T-t}{T-t+1}\right).

This is the same recurrence relation from the mean-scp model with
d=1
, so again the choice of
\pi_{t}
implied by the var-scp recurrence relation satisfies (
C.19
).

C.2.3
MeanVar-SCP Prior

For the meanvar-scp model in Section
2.3
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\log\overline{\pi}_{t}-\log\overline{\pi}_{t+1}\right]

\displaystyle=\log\pi_{t}-\log\pi_{t+1}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}[\omega_{t}y_{t}^{2}]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\>+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t+1}^{T}\omega_{t^{\prime}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\omega_{t^{\prime}}\right)+\log\Gamma\left(\frac{u_{0}+T-t+1}{2}\right)-\log\Gamma\left(\frac{u_{0}+T-t}{2}\right)
